# Welcome to Vaelond

Vaelond is a fractured world built around the absence of **Ardynia**, the civilisation that vanished five centuries ago. Its surviving peoples rebuilt in four great regions, divided by geography, belief and competing versions of history.

## The surviving realms

- **Perfid** in the north is shaped by the Church of Elgira and its struggle with secular power.
- **Doro** in the west is governed through noble houses, law, trade and inherited influence.
- **Minir** in the east values knowledge, careful accumulation and long-term advantage.
- **Rakara** in the south is divided among five clans, each with its own culture, territory and ambitions.

At the centre lie the **Sera Wilds**, the inaccessible remains of old Ardynia.

## Begin exploring

- [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/The Powers That Be|The Powers That Be]]

- [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/Religion/Religion|Religion]]
- [[02 Places/Rakara/Rakara|Rakara]]
- [[content/05 Campaign Chronicle/index|index]]

## Current campaign focus

The Defracturers began their story in [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Somnolent|Somnolent]], where the murder of [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot]] drew them into a conflict involving the [[04 Organisations/Church of Elgira|Church of Elgira]], [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|the Rootbound]], [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|the Broken Fang]] and the surviving relics of Ardynia.

# Doroan Faiths

Doro shares several gods with [[Rakaran Faiths|Rakara]], but interprets them through its own culture of authority, reputation, contracts and noble obligation.

> [!note] Shared gods
> The symbol **âˆ‘** marks a god shared with Rakara.

## Pantheon at a glance

| Deity                                  | Associated with                        | Shared with Rakara |
| -------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- | ------------------ |
| [[#Ulvarn Ascendant âˆ‘]]                | Power, ambition, rule, war             | Yes                |
| [[#Virei of the Gilded Mask âˆ‘]]        | Art, rhetoric, performance, lies       | Yes                |
| [[#Talik of the Balanced Ledger âˆ‘]]    | Trade, contracts, luck                 | Yes                |
| [[#Ezaa the Soft Whisper âˆ‘]]           | Influence, secrets, diplomacy          | Yes                |
| [[#Domara the Matron Unbroken]]        | Family, lineage, duty, marriage        | No                 |
| [[#Cassivar the Oath-Bound]]           | Honour, law, reputation                | No                 |
| [[#Veltrin the Veiled Hand]]           | Assassination, silence, necessary evil | No                 |
| [[#Rhozka, She Who Records the End âˆ‘]] | Death, legacy, memory                  | Yes                |

## Ulvarn Ascendant âˆ‘

*Power, Ambition, Rule, War*

Once a steppe god of conquest, Ulvarn in Doro is worshipped as the **legitimiser of authority**. He does not care *how* power is takenâ€”only that it is held.

- **Doctrine:** Power unused is power wasted.
- **Tension:** Orvak and Rakaran traditionalists see this as a corruption of Ulvarn's trial-by-strength ethos.
- **Common rite:** A noble swears a private oath to Ulvarn before accepting office.

### Prayer, spoken privately

> *â€œUlvarn, set me above those who doubt me.â€*

## Virei of the Gilded Mask âˆ‘

*Art, Rhetoric, Performance, Lies*

Virei is **controlled deception**â€”the god of speeches, fashion, ceremony and lies that sound like truth.

- **Doctrine:** Reality is what the audience believes.
- **Worship:** Plays, salons, political debates and curated scandal.
- **Symbol:** A polished mask with no cracks.

### Prayer before court

> *â€œVirei, let them see only what I choose to show.â€*

## Talik of the Balanced Ledger âˆ‘

*Trade, Contracts, Luck*

Talik survives in Doro as a god of **contracts, margins and loopholes**, rather than chaos.

- **Doctrine:** A deal is sacredâ€”interpretation is flexible.
- **Worship sites:** Counting houses, tax halls and gambling rooms.
- **Common view:** Many swear by Talik publicly, but curse him privately.

### Prayer written in margins

> *â€œTalik, let the numbers favour me.â€*

## Ezaa the Soft Whisper âˆ‘

*Influence, Secrets, Diplomacy*

Ezaa's winds are quieter in Doroâ€”felt in corridors, not open skies.

- **Doctrine:** Words shape nations more surely than armies.
- **Followers:** Envoys, spies, blackmailers and letter-writers.
- **Rite:** Burning unread letters to â€œreturn secrets to the windâ€.

### Prayer

> *â€œEzaa, carry this truth where I cannot.â€*

## Domara the Matron Unbroken

*Family, Lineage, Duty, Marriage*

Domara is the **spine of Doroan society**. She sanctifies bloodlines, arranged marriages and the continuation of the house above the individual.

- **Doctrine:** The family endures; the self is temporary.
- **Worship:** Births, betrothals and inheritance rites.
- **Heresy:** Choosing love over obligation.

### Prayer spoken at weddings

> *â€œDomara, bind us tighter than affection ever could.â€*

## Cassivar the Oath-Bound

*Honour, Law, Reputation*

Cassivar governs **public honour**, not private morality. What matters is what can be proven, sworn or witnessed.

- **Doctrine:** An unbroken oath is worth more than truth.
- **Worshippers:** Judges, advocates and duellists.
- **Ritual:** Swearing before steel or ink.

### Prayer

> *â€œCassivar, let my name survive the telling.â€*

## Veltrin the Veiled Hand

*Assassination, Silence, Necessary Evil*

Veltrin is never worshipped openly. His shrines are hidden and his priests unnamed.

- **Doctrine:** Some deaths preserve order.
- **Followers:** Intelligence circles and sanctioned killers.
- **Public stance:** Officially denied by all houses.

### Prayer, never written

> *â€œVeltrin, let this end quietly.â€*

## Rhozka, She Who Records the End âˆ‘

*Death, Legacy, Memory*

Rhozka is shared with Rakara but interpreted differently: not as a gentle guide, but as **the archivist of consequence**.

- **Doctrine:** Death is finalâ€”but reputation is not.
- **Worship:** Funerals, sealed wills and historical records.
- **Fear:** Rhozka remembers what others erase.

### Prayer

> *â€œRhozka, remember me as I intend to be remembered.â€*

# The Faith of Elgira

*The dominant religion in Perfid.*

The institution is most commonly called the **Church of Elgira**, though **Church of the Saviour**, **Saviour's Church** and **Faith of Elgira** are established aliases.

> [!info] Perfidian doctrine
> The Church teaches that Elgira sacrificed herself during the Battle of the Vale and became divine in that same perfect act. Surviving historical evidence instead indicates that she emerged from the battle transformed, having absorbed far more life and time energy than the sealing ritual was meant to draw, and ascended shortly afterwards. The Church's doctrine turned that terrible miscalculation into a story of deliberate sacrifice.

Perfid worships **Elgira** alone. Once an Ardynian general, she betrayed her empire to save the wider world and later rose to godhood. She is revered as a symbol of righteous rebellion, sacrifice, vigilance and protection.

No other gods are officially recognised in Perfid. Elgira's worship is solemn, widespread and deeply embedded in law, culture and identity.

Her four surviving generals are revered as **saints**, not gods. Each represents a path through which mortals can strive towards Elgira's example.

## The Four Saints of the Vale

### Saint Vaelen of the Vigilant Hand

**Virtue:** Duty, Watchfulness, Endurance  
**Symbol:** An open eye on a gauntlet

> *â€œStand, even when others sleep.â€*

### Saint Erith of the Silent Tongue

**Virtue:** Discretion, Self-Control, Bearing Burdens  
**Symbol:** A closed mouth encircled by thorns

> *â€œSpeak only what must be spoken.â€*

### Saint Marra of the Merciful Blade

**Virtue:** Compassion, Justice in War, Remorse  
**Symbol:** A weeping sword

> *â€œEvery life taken is a wound on the soul.â€*

### Saint Desmon of the Bound Faith

**Virtue:** Humility, Devotion, Selflessness  
**Symbol:** A bound scroll without a name

> *â€œForget the self, remember the purpose.â€*

# Rakaran Faiths

In [[The Powers That Be#Rakara|Rakara]], where the clans each hold close ties to different gods, the variety of Vaelondian worship is especially clear. A [[Clan Temir|Temir]] fisherman might honour the Sea-Mother daily, yet still whisper a prayer to Ezaa before a long journey. An [[Clan Orvak|Orvak]] herder gives thanks to Orun for fertile fields but lights a candle to Rhozka when burying kin. The gods are many, and all are real, so the wise show respect to all.

Each clan tends to favour certain godsâ€”[[Clan Temir|Temir]] with the Sea-Mother and Moon-Father, [[Clan Khaali|Khaali]] with Ashka and Virei, [[Clan Erzhin|Erzhin]] with Ezaa and Khurmog, and so onâ€”but even within clans, individuals may feel a personal pull towards other deities. A warrior might wear a token of Ulvarn for strength in battle, while a young performer might paint symbols of Virei before a festival dance.

People offer thanks when crops grow well or storms pass safely, and they make requests when danger looms or luck runs dry. Even gods feared for their wrath, like Ashka or Talik, receive their dueâ€”to ignore them is to invite misfortune. Worship might be as simple as a whispered phrase at dawn, a coin left at a roadside shrine, or a formal visit to a local temple or spirit-tent.

For most in Rakara, the gods are not distant figures but present forces in the world: capricious, powerful and worth honouring. Faith is not blind devotion, but a conversation between mortals and the divineâ€”offerings for blessings, prayers for safety and praise for favour received.

## Pantheon at a glance

| Deity                            | Associated with                      | Symbol                                      |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------- |
| [[#Sea-Mother and Moon-Father]]  | Water, moon, tides, cycles, rebirth  | A silver wave curled around a crescent moon |
| [[#Orun, the Verdant Kin]]       | Nature, growth, earth, fertility     | A twisted horn filled with vines and grain  |
| [[#Ashka the Ember-Dancer]]      | Fire, chaos, inspiration, renewal    | A spiral flame with dancing feet            |
| [[#Virei the Shattered Mask]]    | Art, expression, emotion, madness    | A half-mask painted with abstract symbols   |
| [[#Ulvarn the Towering Sun]]     | Ambition, power, order, war          | A blazing tower crowned with light          |
| [[#Ezaa of the Whispering Gale]] | Wind, influence, speech, travel      | A bird's feather wrapped in a spiral gust   |
| [[#Khurmog the Silent Deep]]     | Stone, memory, permanence, ancestors | A square spiral engraved into stone         |
| [[#Talik the Laughing Coin]]     | Trade, luck, trickery                | A laughing-and-crying coin                  |
| [[#Rhozka the Veiled One]]       | Secrets, death, dreams, passage      | A black veil over a sleeping face           |

## Sea-Mother and Moon-Father

**Associated with:** Water, Moon, Tides, Cycles, Rebirth  
**Symbol:** A silver wave curled around a crescent moon

- **Sea-Mother** is nurturing but vast and unknowable, worshipped for her bounty and feared for her wrath.
- **Moon-Father** is distant and wise, often interpreted as the one who measures the Sea-Mother's moods with his rhythms.
- **Clerical schism:** Some sects say Moon-Father tempers the Sea-Mother's power; others say he tries to steal it.

### Myth

Long ago, the Moon-Father wandered the sky alone, shedding cold tears into the sea below. One night, his sorrow stirred the depths, and the Sea-Mother rose, her laughter drowning his sadness. They dancedâ€”ebb and pullâ€”until their love birthed the tides. But when they quarrel, the sea storms rage and the moon weeps in eclipse.

### Prayer

> *â€œMoon-Father, calm her breath. Sea-Mother, cradle our sails. Let your rhythm carry us through storm and still.â€*

## Orun, the Verdant Kin

**Associated with:** Nature, Growth, Earth, Fertility  
**Symbol:** A twisted horn filled with vines and grain

- Revered as the sustainer of herds, crops and kinship.
- Believed to dwell in sacred groves and earthen mounds.
- Worship often involves seasonal rites and fertility dances.

### Myth

When the first beast was hungry, Orun buried her heart beneath the plains. From it grew the first grain. Her blood turned into rivers, her breath into pollen. It is said she returns each spring to dance among the sprouts, barefoot and smiling.

### Prayer

> *â€œOrun of root and horn, bless our herds and seed. Let green life rise where your name is sung.â€*

## Ashka the Ember-Dancer

**Associated with:** Fire, Chaos, Inspiration, Renewal  
**Symbol:** A spiral flame with dancing feet

- Both destroyer and muse; fire is feared and revered.
- Shamans say she speaks through dreams and madness.
- Some Khaali clans say she was born of lightning striking bone.

### Myth

Ashka was born when lightning struck the skull of a slain tyrant. She sprang from the bones laughing, clad in smoke. Her steps set the hills ablaze, but from their ashes rose bright wildflowers. She is chaos, and she is beauty.

### Prayer

> *â€œAshka, whose laughter cracks the sky, strike me with your flame. Let my spirit burn wild and bright.â€*

## Virei the Shattered Mask

**Associated with:** Art, Expression, Emotion, Madness  
**Favoured by:** Khaali, Voruun  
**Symbol:** A half-mask painted with abstract symbols

- Patron of performers, mad prophets and radical visionaries.
- Worshipped through ecstatic ritual, theatre and music.
- Seen as either a divine artist or a cosmic prankster.

### Myth

Virei sculpted the first faces from clay, then shattered their mould. From the shards came poetry, song and dream. She hides her face so that no one may copy her, and whispers inspiration through the cracks in the mind.

### Prayer

> *â€œVirei, mask-maker, soul-breaker, speak through my hands and voice. Let me create what even the gods fear to imagine.â€*

## Ulvarn the Towering Sun

**Associated with:** Ambition, Power, Order, War  
**Symbol:** A blazing tower crowned with a crown of light

- Said to have forged the first empires in the age before clans.
- Worshipped by chieftains and those who seek dominion.
- Believed to test mortals with obstacles to strengthen the worthy.

### Myth

Ulvarn rose from the mountain's shadow, bearing a crown he forged from the sun itself. He cast down the false kings and raised his banner across the plains. It is said each victory adds another flame to his tower.

### Prayer

> *â€œUlvarn of light and law, raise my path. Let none stand between me and the summit I seek.â€*

## Ezaa of the Whispering Gale

**Associated with:** Wind, Influence, Speech, Travel  
**Symbol:** A bird's feather wrapped in a spiral gust

- Messenger god, patron of scouts, messengers and spies.
- Worshipped through wind-chimes and sky offerings.
- Erzhini traders pray to Ezaa before bartering or diplomacy.

### Myth

Ezaa was born from the breath of the world itself. With every word spoken, she grows. Her wings beat on every caravan's sail and every liar's tongue. The wind may be still, but she is always listening.

### Prayer

> *â€œEzaa, whispering wind, guide my steps and sharpen my tongue. May my words ride your currents.â€*

## Khurmog the Silent Deep

**Associated with:** Stone, Memory, Permanence, Ancestors  
**Symbol:** A square spiral engraved into stone

- Guardian of ancestral knowledge, cairns and tombs.
- Worshipped through cairn-building and stone-etching.
- Erzhini lorekeepers say Khurmog remembers what the wind forgets.

### Myth

Before time flowed, Khurmog sat beneath the mountains, etching memory into stone. He taught the ancestors to carve their stories, so even in death they would endure. It is said earthquakes are his slow, thoughtful turns.

### Prayer

> *â€œKhurmog, old and still, hold our names in stone. Let the weight of our deeds outlast the winds.â€*

## Talik the Laughing Coin

**Associated with:** Trade, Luck, Trickery  
**Symbol:** A spinning coin showing a laughing face on one side and a crying face on the other

- Popular among merchants, gamblers and wandering folk.
- Stories claim Talik once tricked a god out of their domain.
- Seen as both a patron of freedom and a necessary chaos.

### Myth

Talik tricked a god of law into a wager, then won his own divinity in the deal. Ever since, he walks among markets and battlefields, flipping his coin. He offers luckâ€”fair or foulâ€”to those who gamble with fate.

### Prayer

> *â€œTalik, spinner of fortunes, let the coin land light in my favour. And if notâ€”make the fall worth the tale.â€*

## Rhozka the Veiled One

**Associated with:** Secrets, Death, Dreams, Passage  
**Symbol:** A black veil over a sleeping face

- Keeper of the final passage and hidden knowledge.
- Worship varies: some fear Rhozka as the ender, while others revere Rhozka as a gentle guide.
- Dream-diviners and death-singers invoke Rhozka in moments of loss.

### Myth

Rhozka was the first to pass beyond the veil, and the only one to return. They carry dreams in one hand and endings in the other. It is said that when they sing, the dead remember.

### Prayer

> *â€œRhozka, veiled and knowing, walk with us in silence. When our breath stills, open the next door gently.â€*

# Religion

Though wizards work wonders with their Art and adventurers take their fates into their own hands, it is on the gods that most folk in Vaelond depend when they have need.

The gods play a role in the lives of nearly everyone, from the mightiest lord to the meanest urchin. Most people worship many gods throughout their lives, depending on their needs, work and family. Worship is deeply practical: people make offerings, say prayers or keep small tokens of the gods they feel closest to, not out of dogma, but to earn favour or avoid misfortune.

Although worship is common across Vaelond, the visage of the gods varies from region to region. Each pantheon reflects the culture that shaped itâ€”from monolithic Perfid and its Church of Elgira to the disparate and unruly collection of faiths found throughout Rakara.

## Regional traditions

- [[Rakaran Faiths]] â€” a varied and practical pantheon shaped by the Five Clans
- [[Doroan Faiths]] â€” gods reframed through authority, reputation, contracts and noble society
- [[Perfidian Faith]] â€” the exclusive worship of Elgira and veneration of her four saints

> [!note] Minir
> The source extract does not yet contain the Miniran religious tradition. A placeholder and suggested image are included in [[Missing Images and Content]].

# The Powers That Be

## Perfid

Perfid is locked in a perpetual **cold war with Doro**, both sides trading steel and spellfire over a harsh, blood-soaked stretch of contested land known as **the Spear and the Maw**â€”a narrow peninsula and canyon that neither side can afford to lose.

Within its borders, Perfid is caught in a fraught stalemate between church and state. The **[[Church of Elgira]]** holds vast spiritual and social power, while the government and military under **House d'Eamon** push for greater secular control.

Tensions rise as both factions become more extreme. The Church's efforts to expand its influence into Minir and Rakara are increasingly treated as political acts rather than simple missionary work.

## Doro

Doro wages war with Perfid by day, and with itself by night.

It is a realm of grand palaces, endless bureaucracy and veiled knives. Though powerful on paper, **Doro is paralysed by internal politics**. Rule is divided between **six Great Houses**, each competing through marriage pacts, scandal, trade and sabotage.

Those outside the corridors of power are often treated as thralls or collateral for the war. The people grow weary, but few dare break the ancient web of alliances holding the realm together.

See also: [[Doroan Faiths]].

## Minir

Minir remains outwardly **detached**, building its strength in quiet. Its universities, archives and arcane workshops have become the envy of the world. While others fight, Minir studies, trading with every side while committing fully to none.

The **Pale Seminary** is a university located in **Vehr**. Rumours persist of hidden projects beneath its halls, including research into ancient magic and Ardynian technology.

Minir refuses to join the war between Doro and Perfid, preferring to use their rivalry to secure favourable trade and political leverage.

## Rakara

Far to the south, **Rakara** stands on the edge of transformation.

Over centuries, dozens of tribes were gradually absorbed into the Five Clans. Ten years ago, **Anthea Bloodclot** began styling herself the **Lord of the Five Clans**. The office had never formally existed: Anthea drew upon an old myth and used the title to build a personal following, mediate disputes and encourage shared law and cooperation.

Her authority depended upon reputation, negotiation and the willingness of the clans to participate. It was not a recognised crown or permanent constitutional office.

One year ago, Anthea was murdered near [[Somnolent]]. Voruun now publicly claims to be her rightful successor, with Khaali the only clan offering open support. The others reject, avoid or carefully qualify the claim. Rakara remains fractured, and old rivalries are returning beneath the language of unity.

See also: [[Rakara]], [[Rakara - Background]], [[Rakara's Regions and Clans]], [[Somnolent]], [[Emberwake Festival]] and [[Rakaran Faiths]].

# The Sundering of Ardynia

## The Last Dusk of Ardynia

*As preserved in the oldest hymns of the Church of Elgira, fragments of the Broken Annals and later historical reconstruction.*

> [!info] A remembered history
> The broad events are accepted, but much of the final ritual was hidden, misunderstood or deliberately turned into religious symbolism.

In the age before reckoning, when the sky cracked with sorcery and the stones wept beneath the weight of empire, there came a woman cloaked in ash and resolve. Her name was **Elgira**, once a scholar-general of Ardyniaâ€”the gleaming bastion of knowledge, ambition and cruel eternity.

Ardynia had ceased to conquer for need. It conquered for permanence. Obsessed with escaping death, its rulers traded in years as coin and stripped time from other peoples. What they could not consume, they entombed. The land groaned beneath engines of stasis that reshaped hills, dried rivers and imprisoned living things outside the natural passage of time.

Elgira turned against the empire she had helped serve. With four trusted generals and a growing rebellion, she marched upon Ardynia to end what it had become.

The final battle was fought beneath **Moonshadow Crag**, in the shadow of **the Vale**, an old Ardynian outpost from which the conflict later took its common name: the **Battle of the Vale**. Elgira led a host of ten thousand against Ardynia's legions and temporal war-machines. The rebels held until the ground had turned to glass and the air itself seemed worn thin.

Then all fell quiet.

## The terrible calculation

Elgira's final plan required an impossible quantity of life and time energy. The army had volunteered to sustain the sealing ritual, but the power required had been catastrophically underestimated. When the ritual took hold, it drew far more from the rebel host than Elgira intended.

The ten thousand were consumed.

Ardyniaâ€”its capital, armies and heartlandsâ€”vanished behind a temporal seal. It was not destroyed, but removed from the ordinary world and held beyond reach.

Elgira and her four generals emerged from the silence hollow-eyed and changed. Elgira had absorbed an immense portion of the released life and time energy. She survived the battle, but no longer as an ordinary mortal. Shortly afterwards, the transformation completed and she ascended to godhood.

This was not the deliberate self-sacrifice later taught by the Church. It was the consequence of a terrible miscalculation: a victory that saved the wider world while costing the lives of nearly everyone who had trusted the ritual.

## Memory becomes doctrine

Elgira and the four generals refused to explain the full price of the Sundering. They taught only vigilance: Ardynia had been **sealed**, not slain, and one day the seal might fail.

From these teachings grew the **[[Perfidian Faith|Church of Elgira]]**, also called the Church of the Saviour, the Saviour's Church and the Faith of Elgira. Over generations, guilt, secrecy and incomplete memory hardened into doctrine. Elgira's survival, transformation and later ascension were retold as a single perfect sacrifice at the moment of victory.

Her four generals became saints:

- **Saint Vaelen of the Vigilant Hand**, guardian of duty, watchfulness and endurance.
- **Saint Erith of the Silent Tongue**, keeper of discretion and unbearable knowledge.
- **Saint Marra of the Merciful Blade**, patron of compassion, justice in war and remorse.
- **Saint Desmon of the Bound Faith**, exemplar of devotion and surrender of the self.

The ten thousand were remembered as the **Ashen Choir**: martyrs whose lives sustained the sealing of Ardynia. Church teaching presents their deaths as willing and perfectly measured. The surviving historical fragments suggest something much more tragic.

## Legacy and omen

Ardynia remains sealed rather than destroyed. Cracks in time, cold winds beneath stone and impossible ruins are treated as signs that the old prison may not be permanent.

The Church teaches that Elgira watches from beyond the mortal world. Historians agree on less, but most accept one warning left by those who survived the Vale:

**Ardynia can return.**

# Time and Calendars

A week in Vaelond lasts **eight days**. Some days have traditional purposes attached to them, although customs vary by region.

## Days of the week

| Day | Traditional association |
|---|---|
| **Onesday** | Worship and rest |
| **Twosday** | â€” |
| **Thirdsday** | â€” |
| **Fourthsday** | â€” |
| **Fifthsday** | â€” |
| **Sixthday** | Remembrance and funerals |
| **Seventhday** | â€” |
| **Eighthday** | Family and feasts |

## The year

Each month consists of five full weeks, mirroring the moon's cycle. There are ten months in a year, making a Vaelondian year **400 days** long.

| Month | Seasonal character |
|---|---|
| **Frostmelt** | Winter's thaw; the new year begins |
| **Thawen** | First blooms and lambing season |
| **Greengold** | Spring in full; trade resumes |
| **Blazereach** | Dry heat begins; fire rituals |
| **Highsun** | Peak of summer; war campaigns |
| **Duskhail** | Fading light; storms gather |
| **Fallshade** | Leaves turn; long hunts begin |
| **Stonecrack** | Frost returns; ancestors are honoured |
| **Whitesky** | Snows rise; spirits walk |
| **Deeprest** | End of year; sacred stillness |

## Interactive calendar

The campaign's interactive calendar is hosted on Fantasy Calendar:

<https://app.fantasy-calendar.com/calendars/4f425715469a9c378f1105857c515efa>

---

---
title: An Outsider's View of Rakara
type: in-world-text
status: published
player_facing: true
aliases:
  - A Consideration of Rakara
  - The Frontier Between Powers
  - Sarlan Vinthel's Account of Rakara
tags:
  - lore
  - places
  - politics
  - rakara
  - minir
region: Rakara
author: Sarlan Vinthel
perspective: Miniri academic
source_date: 2025-06-08
updated: 2026-07-01
---

# A Consideration of Rakara: The Frontier Between Powers

> [!warning] In-world prejudice
> This essay reflects Sarlan Vinthel's Miniri assumptions, including his condescending distinction between â€œbarbarismâ€ and â€œcivilisationâ€. It is preserved as an in-world viewpoint rather than an objective description.

It is often said that Rakara is a land between thingsâ€”between powers, eras and civilisations. I would amend that description: Rakara is a land of suspended motion, caught in the moment just before transformation or collapse.

Geographically, Rakara forms the broad basin between **Doro to the west** and **Minir to the east**. The inaccessible **Sera Wilds** wall off the north. There is no direct route from Rakara to Perfid across those mountains. Rakara therefore serves as the principal viable land route between Doro and Minir, although prudent merchants often choose the sea rather than risk the region's instability.

To speak of Rakara's governance is to speak of a vacuum shaped like a crownâ€”though, strictly speaking, there was never a crown to empty. Ten years ago, Anthea Bloodclot revived the mythic title **Lord of the Five Clans** and used it to organise a personal federation around herself. She was less monarch than war-broker, adjudicator and ceremonial anchor. The arrangement brought relative peace and a degree of legal standardisation, encouraging those of us in Minir who hoped Rakara might be moving towards proper statehood.

This optimism was short-lived. Less than a year ago, Anthea was found dead near Somnolent. No publicly accepted culprit emerged. A thousand theories followed, ranging from Perfidian interference to betrayal by a rival clan. Some clans now retreat inward. Others eye the central roads hungrily, seeing profit in taxation and control.

Rakara's culture bears the marks of its steppe heritage. Strength is a currency of honour, whether martial or magical, and a peculiar code governs its expression. In a duel to the deathâ€”especially in the fighting pits of [[Clan Voruun|Voruun]]â€”healing magic is forbidden unless applied evenly to both combatants.

The Rakaran population includes humans, elves and other humanoid peoples alongside notable communities of beastkin, orcs and certain extraplanar immigrants. Rakaran society is paradoxically conservative and accepting, provided that strength and contribution are demonstrated.

Religiously, Rakara is diverse. Each clan maintains its own combination of gods, lesser spirits and local traditions. Temples are commonly rustic, sometimes indistinguishable from longhouses or grain halls, yet their devotion is sincere. The [[Church of Elgira]], with characteristic ambition, has sent missionaries into southern Rakara with coin and clerical magic. Their reception has been mixed.

Two roads form the bones of Rakara's trade infrastructure. The **Salt Spine** runs north to south, while the **Trade Horizon** stretches between Doro and Minir. Their crossing is among the most contested and closely watched places on the continent. Rakara's continued relevance depends upon its utility as a bridge.

Should Rakara find a new unifying forceâ€”Voruun, a foreign-backed regime or something unexpectedâ€”it may yet become a nation. If not, it will remain what it has long been: a place where the wheels of the world pass through but rarely stop.

To study Rakara is to observe the border between what my discipline calls barbarism and civilisationâ€”not, I insist, in judgement, but in taxonomy. It is not that the Rakaran peoples lack culture, for theirs is rich and storied. It is that they lack cohesion.

â€” **Sarlan Vinthel**, Scholar of Regional Histories and Master Archivist of the **Pale Seminary**, a university in Vehr, Minir

# Clan Erzhin

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Northeastern foothills and accessible mountain bases of the Sera Wilds |
| **Leaders** | Talia and Malen |
| **Known for** | Stonebooks, mountain halls, scholarship and the balance of wind and stone |
| **Closest foreign relationship** | Minir |

## Sigil

![[Clan Erzhin Sigil.png]]

The sigil of Clan Erzhin bears a **twin spiral on a cool stone-grey shield**. One spiral resembles etched stone and the other flowing wind. A chiseled mountain silhouette rises behind them.

- **Twin spirals:** stone and windâ€”enduring memory and evolving truth.
- **Mountain:** the clan's physical and spiritual foundation in the Sera Wilds.

## Background

Along the accessible foothills, mountain bases and outer valleys of the Sera Wilds, where wind howls through craggy corridors and clouds settle in cliffside hollows, Clan Erzhin endures.

The sealed interior of the range remains inaccessible. The Erzhini are a people of **rock and air**, carving both their homes and their memories into the bones of the world.

Their culture orbits two complementary pillars: **stone for what endures, and wind for what changes**. The clan's twin leaders embody that balance. **Talia** is a stern, calculating warrior; **Malen** is a charismatic and persuasive diplomat.

## People and culture

Erzhini settlements are often half-hidden, with homes and halls tunnelled into cliffs and mountains. Only minimal structures are visible from the surface.

The belief that **to forget the past is to weaken the present** shapes Erzhini society. Each family maintains a *Stonebook*: an ancestral and personal record carved from the mountain, passed down and expanded across generations.

The Erzhini are typically:

- Reserved and formal, speaking with careful purpose.
- Exceptionally educated, with reading and inscription taught from a young age.
- Deeply loyal to their clan and its record-keeping traditions.
- Fiercely proud of their cultural autonomy and precision of memory.

## Trade and tensions

Erzhin's close alignment with Minir causes concern elsewhere in Rakara. Centuries of trade, scholarship and diplomacy have made the clan a quiet beneficiary of Miniri wealth and technology.

- **Minir** regards Erzhin as a reliable partner in trade and academia.
- **Khaali and Voruun** are uneasy about Erzhin's perceived eastern tilt and the spread of Miniri ideals.
- **Orvak** maintains a distant but respectful relationship with Erzhin.

Following Anthea Bloodclot's assassination, Erzhin has remained calm and methodical, focusing on preserving accurate accounts.

> [!question] Rumour
> Some observers believe Erzhin might abandon Rakaran unity if Minir offered sufficient protection or incentive. No such agreement is publicly known.

## Faith

Wind and stone also shape Erzhini worship. The clan is particularly associated with [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/Religion/Rakaran Faiths#Ezaa of the Whispering Gale|Ezaa of the Whispering Gale]] and [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/Religion/Rakaran Faiths#Khurmog the Silent Deep|Khurmog the Silent Deep]].

# Clan Khaali

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Fertile southeastern Rakara beneath Mount Khal |
| **Leader** | Chieftain Sifaya Flameborn |
| **Capital** | Emberrest |
| **Known for** | Fire worship, agriculture, artistry and trained pyromancers |

## Sigil

![[Clan Khaali Sigil.png]]

The sigil of Clan Khaali bears a **golden flame above a stylised volcano on a deep red shield**. The volcano represents Mount Khal, while the flame evokes sacred fire and creative transformation.

- **Flame:** the Ember Court and fire as both destroyer and creator.
- **Volcano:** Mount Khal, ancestral connection to the land and the fragile nature of stability.

## Background

Where fertile land meets the sea and ash-fed fields bloom, Clan Khaali thrives beneath long-slumbering **Mount Khal**. The Khaalites are a people of fire, art and passion whose culture combines vibrant creativity with deep loyalty to a fiery pantheon.

The clan is ruled by **Chieftain Sifaya Flameborn**, a charismatic matriarch descended from an unbroken line of fire-blooded leaders. Sifaya was a close ally of Anthea Bloodclot. Anthea rose to prominence and power in Khaali lands, which led many outsiders to assumeâ€”incorrectlyâ€”that she was Khaali by origin. Since Anthea's assassination, Sifaya has redoubled her efforts to preserve cooperation between the clans. Khaali is the only clan to offer open support to [[Clan Voruun|Voruun's]] public claim to succeed Anthea.

## People and culture

Khaali society is built around **the Ember Court**, a collection of deities associated with flame, passion and transformation. Fire is not merely destructive: it is divine change. To burn is to grow; to destroy is to renew.

Khaali buildings are painted in rich reds, oranges and deep blacks. Music and dance are central to education and storytelling. Artisans who work volcanic glass, obsidian and forged metals are revered almost as highly as warriors.

The Khaalites carry colour in their dress, boldness in their speech and spectacle into their rituals. Every harvest becomes a celebration and every decision a performance. Beneath that pageantry lies formidable force: Khaali maintains the largest circle of trained pyromancers in Rakara.

The clan's faith is particularly associated with [[Rakaran Faiths#Ashka the Ember-Dancer|Ashka the Ember-Dancer]] and [[Rakaran Faiths#Virei the Shattered Mask|Virei the Shattered Mask]].

## Trade and tensions

Other powers treat Khaali carefully because of **the Tide of Flame**. During that confrontation, an elite cadre of Khaali fire mages advanced towards Vehr and easily defeated Miniri soldiers sent against them. Minir chose to parley and paid reparations rather than risk a wider battle. The incident is still taught in Miniri universities as a lesson in â€˜the cost of provocationâ€™. 

- **Voruun:** Khaali's closest ally and strategic partner. Their leaders meet regularly and publicly invoke Anthea's dream of unity.
- **Orvak and Erzhin:** respected but considered too withdrawn or academic to be completely trusted in a crisis.
- **Perfid:** relations are tense. The Church of Elgira regards Khaali fire worship as heresy, and Sifaya has rebuked missionary efforts.
- **Somnolent:** profitable but watched warily. Its independence is viewed by some as evidence that Anthea never fully united Rakara.

# Clan Orvak

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Forested northeastern foothills of the Sera Wilds |
| **Leader** | Balka |
| **Known as** | The Quiet Root |
| **Known for** | Resilience, self-sufficiency, woodland expertise and decentralised rule |

## Sigil

![[Clan Orvak Sigil.png]]

The sigil of Clan Orvak bears a **sturdy oak tree on a forest-green shield**, with a mountain silhouette at its base. The roots extend into the mountain, representing grounded strength, endurance and quiet resolve.

- **Oak tree:** resilience, ancestral wisdom and deep-rooted tradition.
- **Mountain base:** the Sera Wild foothills and the clan's enduring bond with a difficult land.

## Background

Clan Orvak is known as the **Quiet Root of Rakara**. Its cold, damp homeland lies in forested cradles beneath the Sera Wild mountains, where farming is difficult and endurance essential. The Orvaki are hardy, soft-spoken and deeply tied to the land, with a reverence for wilderness that borders on the spiritual.

Their society is clannish but egalitarian. Small, semi-autonomous villages are led by councils of elders. Although **Balka** serves as the clan's figurehead, he gives those councils considerable freedom and responsibility. This decentralised governance has made Orvak particularly resistant to outside control. Anthea Bloodclot's success in bringing them into the confederation was regarded as a diplomatic masterstroke, even in Doro.

## People and culture

Orvaki people tend to be **stoic, slow to trust and slower to forgive**. They prize self-sufficiency, resilience and communal loyalty. Outsiders sometimes mistake their quietness for backwardnessâ€”a dangerous assumption.

The Orvaki are especially skilled in:

- Woodland survival and guerrilla tactics.
- Hunting and trapping, including knowledge of rare creatures from the Sera Wilds.
- Herbal alchemy and wilderness medicine.
- Spiritual traditions venerating forest spirits, ancestor shades and weather gods.

Despite its isolation, Orvak is the most racially and culturally diverse of the five clans. It has a long history of accepting exiles and people fleeing persecution elsewhere. In Orvaki lands, quiet contribution matters more than bloodline.

## Known campaign site

[[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]], an Ardynian Chronarch ruin explored by the Defracturers, lies in the Orvak forest. The party recovered the Bands of Distortion there after defeating a stone golem.
## Trade and tensions

Orvak shares a long and tense border with Doro:

- Doro values Orvaki pelts, rare herbs and mountain ore, and border settlements profit from seasonal trade.
- Orvak resents Doroan political meddling and attempts to gain influence through diplomacy or coin.
- Many Orvaki fear that Doro would gladly absorb themâ€”and that cultural and economic annexation has already begun.

Within Rakara, the other clans tend to regard Orvak as reliable but frustrating. Since Anthea's death, Orvak has withdrawn further from wider politics, strengthened local militias and placed greater emphasis on regional unity.

> [!question] Rumour
> Some conspiratorial voices claim Orvak shelters sleeper agents or exiles involved in Anthea Bloodclot's assassination. The allegation is unproven and strongly denied by Orvaki elders.

# Clan Temir

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Rocky southern coast of Rakara |
| **Leader** | Avaz |
| **Known for** | Martial discipline, ports, sailors and the Wavecutters |
| **Primary gods** | Sea-Mother and Moon-Father |

## Sigil

![[Clan Temir Sigil.png]]

The sigil of Clan Temir shows a **crescent moon above ocean waves on a deep navy shield**, pierced by a downward-pointing white sword. Its clean lines reflect Temir's martial culture and belief in strength through discipline.

- **Moon:** the Moon-Father, guidance, discipline and timing in warfare.
- **Waves:** the Sea-Mother, providence, danger and survival through hardship.
- **Sword:** martial heritage and the belief that steel, not words, defines legacy.

## Background

Clan Temir is Rakara's most openly martial powerâ€”**fierce, unyielding and forged in salt and blood**. Its homeland spans rocky southern coasts, deep fjords and storm-battered bays lined with fishing villages and stone watchtowers. Temiri culture is built on endurance, discipline and the conviction that steel holds truth better than any tongue.

Temir's strategic ports and seaborne trade are as important as its warriors. Salted fish, dried kelp and shell-based alchemical goods flow through its harbours. Perfid merchants have historically used Temir as their preferred coastal stop when travelling towards Minir, circumventing Doro's inland controls and earning Temir both wealth and enemies.

Clan leaders are selected through demonstrated skill and campaign merit as much as bloodline. The current leader, **Avaz**, won acclaim by destroying a pirate keep off the Temiri coast.

## People and culture

The Temiri are pragmatic and proud, raised to the rhythms of the tide and disciplined from childhood. Brutal winters, storm-tossed seas and the constant strain of shipboard life have produced a culture of resilience, clarity and loyalty to kin. They have little patience for elaborate diplomacy but respect honour, commitment and deeds.

Their religion centres on the [[Rakaran Faiths#Sea-Mother and Moon-Father|Sea-Mother and Moon-Father]]. Common rites include:

- Moonlight duels to resolve disputes.
- Offerings of bone and salt cast into the sea before voyages.
- Coming-of-age trials in which young warriors dive from cliffs to recover relics placed by elders.

> [!quote]
> *â€œNothing sacred is safe from the sea.â€*

The saying reminds the Temiri that even greatness must survive testing.

## Trade and tensions

Temir's control over southern sea routes makes it indispensable to Perfid and Minir, but that influence is being challenged.

- **Perfid** relies on Temiri ports but regularly causes friction by pushing Elgiran religious influence into coastal settlements.
- **Minir** maintains a practical and respectful trading relationship, although its diplomats find Temir difficult to influence.
- **Doro** resents the routes that allow merchants to bypass its tolls and controls.
- **Voruun's rival port** is viewed as a direct challenge to Temiri coastal supremacy. Temiri warships now patrol closer to Voruun territory.

Temir remains powerful but increasingly restless. While the clan respects Anthea Bloodclot's memory, many Wavecutters are frustrated by the political stalemate and whisper that peace must be seized through strength rather than negotiated.

> [!question] Unconfirmed
> Several merchant convoys have disappeared amid the Temirâ€“Voruun dispute. Neither clan publicly accepts responsibility.

# FILE: C:\Users\ellis\Documents\vaelond-wiki\content\02 Places\Rakara\Clans\Clan Voruun.md
---

---
title: Clan Voruun
type: clan
status: published
player_facing: true
aliases:
  - Voruun
  - The Vorunni
tags:
  - lore
  - places
  - politics
  - rakara
  - clan
region: Rakara
clan: Voruun
leader: Relka of the Chain-Eye
source_date: 2025-07-07
updated: 2026-07-01
---

# Clan Voruun

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Central Rakara |
| **Leader** | Relka of the Chain-Eye |
| **Key routes** | Salt Spine and Trade Horizon |
| **Known for** | Trade, infrastructure, social mobility, fighting pits and controlled aggression |

## Sigil

![[Clan Voruun Sigil.png]]

The sigil of Clan Voruun bears a **silver serpent passing through a broken chain on a dark bronze shield**, backed by an angular sunburst. It presents liberation and progress, although critics see opportunism and unchecked ambition.

- **Serpent:** cleverness, patience and deadly precision.
- **Broken chain:** strength through liberationâ€”and rejection of unity or tradition when convenient.
- **Sunburst:** innovation and a dawning era, with a sharpness that can resemble self-righteous ambition.

## Background

Clan Voruun is the dominant force in central Rakara, controlling the **Salt Spine** and **Trade Horizon**, the great roads connecting east to west and north to south. Wealth flows through Voruun hands, and power follows it.

Under **Relka of the Chain-Eye**, the clan has become a force of opportunists, modernisers and masters of controlled aggression. Relka is a strategist, populist and tactician with no illusions about power. She rose from pit-fighter to general and then ruler, not merely through force but by making herself indispensable.

Voruun claims to represent a new Rakara built on infrastructure, diplomacy and enlightened government. Since Anthea's death, it has publicly declared itself the rightful successor to her project. Only Khaali openly supports this claim. Its cities expand, its envoys travel widely and scholars write decrees beside mercenaries. Beneath that polish remains the heart of a war-clan. The fighting pits of **Dazuun** still determine advancement, honour still demands blood and council seats may be earned through cunning or brute force.

## People and culture

Voruun culture is fiercely pragmatic and unusually socially mobile. Anyone can rise through merit, might or manipulation. Advancement occurs in courtrooms and combat rings; success is revered and failure quickly forgotten.

- Ambition is considered a virtue and is pursued openly.
- Vorunni citizens are known for silver tongues, hard elbows and quick blades.
- Education, architecture and trade reform exist comfortably beside feats of strength, ritual duels and blood oaths.

Voruun belief is non-centralised: a patchwork of warrior-ancestor reverence, urban shrines and new philosophical cults. Many have borrowed Miniri ideas and reshaped them into something harsher and more direct.

## Trade and tensions

Control of the Salt Spine and Trade Horizon gives Voruun enormous leverage. The clan taxes caravans, controls supply flows and is constructing a southern port to challenge [[Clan Temir|Temir's]] coastal dominance.

- **Minir and Doro** both maintain trade pacts with Voruun, but neither trusts it. Adjusted boundaries and manipulated route access have caused diplomatic standoffs.
- **Temir** treats the new port as an act of war in slow motion.
- **Perfid** remains officially neutral but wary. Some Elgiran clergy condemn Relka's unchecked ambition.
- **Somnolent** is the deepest irritation. Although enclosed by Voruun claims, it rejects Voruun soldiers, taxes and rule.

Voruun casts a long and hungry shadow. It leads Rakara in wealth, movement and centrality, but aggression has left it with few true friends. Relka balances the need for unity against the temptation to force submission from those who will not cooperate.

# Hushvale

> [!summary] At a glance
> An Orvaki settlement that the Defracturers intended to reach after exploring Rana Viam. The party has not yet arrived, and little reliable information about the community has entered the campaign record.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Rakara |
| **Territory** | [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Orvak|Clan Orvak]] |
| **Party status** | Intended destination |
| **Known prospective resident** | [[03 People/Notable Figures/Mireya Dinak|Mireya Dinak]] |

## What the party knows

Mireya and her husband were travelling towards Hushvale with plans to establish a gambling hall there. Their journey ended in an ogre attack that killed Mireya's husband and destroyed their cart.

After the Defracturers rescued Mireya, she travelled with them to [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]] and looked after their horses while they entered the ruin.

Following the recovery of the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]], the party planned to leave Rana Viam and continue to Hushvale. That journey was interrupted when Broken Fang scouts confronted them and revealed that [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] had taken their horses.

## Current significance

Hushvale remains the party's intended destination once the immediate Broken Fang crisis is resolved.

No session record has yet established the settlement's leadership, size, principal buildings, defences or attitude towards outsiders.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Why did Mireya and her husband choose Hushvale for their gambling hall?
> - Who governs the settlement?
> - What reception will the Defracturers receive?
> - Has news of the Broken Fang reached Hushvale?
> - Will Mireya continue with her original plan after her husband's death?

# Rakara: A Land Once Wild, Now Watchful

For much of its history, Rakara was a patchwork of clashing steppe tribes: nomadic, fiercely independent and in near-constant flux. Each held to its own gods, laws and grudges. Blood feuds outlasted generations. Trade was a gamble. War was tradition.

Over the past three centuries, five dominant powers gradually absorbed, allied with or displaced their smaller rivals:

- [[Clan Orvak]]
- [[Clan Erzhin]]
- [[Clan Temir]]
- [[Clan Khaali]]
- [[Clan Voruun]]

## The Five Clans

The resulting clans were never a single state, but their influence imposed a recognisable political shape on the region. Each retained its own leaders, customs, military and laws.

The supposed office of the **Lord of the Five Clans** was an old story rather than an established institution. Ten years ago, **Anthea Bloodclot** adopted the title and used the myth to create a personal following. Through diplomacy, leverage and force of personality, she acted as war-broker and adjudicator, encouraging shared laws, reducing raids and pooling knowledge between the clans.

Anthea rose to prominence in [[Clan Khaali|Khaali lands]], and many outsiders therefore assume she was Khaali by origin. That assumption is incorrect: her true place of origin was never publicly established.

## A crown-shaped vacuum

One year ago, Anthea was murdered near [[Somnolent]]. No formal office died with her, but the network of agreements and personal loyalties she had built began to unravel.

Voruun publicly claims the right to succeed her and continue the project of unity. Only Khaali openly supports that claim. Erzhin, Orvak and Temir have not accepted Voruun's position.

- Some clans have retreated inward.
- Others compete for influence over the central roads.
- Somnolent's independence has become more strategically important.
- Foreign churches, traders and diplomats are taking a greater interest in the region.

## Geography

Rakara lies between Doro to the west and Minir to the east. The inaccessible **Sera Wilds** form the great mountain barrier to the north and prevent any direct route between Rakara and Perfid.

Orvak and Erzhin do not occupy the sealed interior of the range. Their communities lie in the forests, foothills, mountain bases and accessible outer valleys of the Sera Wilds.

## The roads that hold Rakara together

Two routes form the bones of Rakara's trade network:

- The **Salt Spine** runs north to south through the heart of the region.
- The **Trade Horizon** crosses east to west between Doro and Minir.

Their intersection near Somnolent gives central Rakara enormous strategic importance.

# Rakara

Rakara is a southern realm shaped by five great clans, each with its own territory, customs and ambitions. For decades those clans moved towards a shared confederation under [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot]], the Lord of the Five Clans.

Her assassination left the title empty, the union uncertain and every clan watching the others closely.

![[Rakara Regions and Clans.png]]

# Rakara's Regions and Clans

There are five great clans in Rakaraâ€”five names spoken like compass points, shaping law, war and the reception offered to a stranger at the fire. Once there were dozens. Time, trade and blood reduced them to five.

The title **Lord of the Five Clans** was never an official office. It was an old myth revived by Anthea Bloodclot, who used it for ten years to build a personal following and hold a fragile web of agreements together. Her death left no constitutional vacancy, but it did leave a dangerous absence of influence.

## Areas of influence

![[Rakara Regions and Clans.png]]

The coloured boundaries show broad areas of influence rather than fixed national borders:

| Marking | Area |
|---|---|
| Grey | [[Clan Orvak]] |
| Yellow | [[Clan Erzhin]] |
| Blue | [[Clan Temir]] |
| Red | [[Clan Khaali]] |
| Green | [[Clan Voruun]] |
| Orange | [[Somnolent]] |

## Clan Orvak

Known as the **Quiet Root of Rakara**, [[Clan Orvak]] occupies northeastern forests and the lower foothills of the Sera Wilds. Orvaki communities do not extend into the inaccessible heart of the range. They are hardy farmers, hunters and foragers who value action over words and are regarded as stubborn but dependable.

Orvak is the most diverse of the clans, home to many ancestries and customs united by respect for the land. Its long border with Doro makes it wary of political and economic encroachment.

## Clan Erzhin

[[Clan Erzhin]] lives in the accessible mountain bases, outer valleys and cliff settlements of the northeastern Sera Wilds. The **Erzhini** are a people of record and reflection who revere wind and stone. Their histories are cut into cliff faces and mountain halls.

Led by the siblings **Malen** and **Talia**, the clan balances diplomacy with martial strength. Its close ties to Minir cause concern among other clans, but Erzhin remains fiercely protective of its own autonomy and Rakara's memory.

## Clan Temir

[[Clan Temir]] rules the storm-wracked southern coast. The Temiri value strength, discipline and action over words. Their warriors, fishers and sailors worship the [[Rakaran Faiths#Sea-Mother and Moon-Father|Sea-Mother and Moon-Father]].

Temir's ports have long served merchants bypassing Doro's inland controls. The construction of a rival Voruun harbour has sharply increased tension. Under **Avaz** and the Wavecutters, Temir remains proud and prepared for conflict.

## Clan Khaali

[[Clan Khaali]] occupies fertile southeastern Rakara beneath dormant **Mount Khal**. Its capital, **Emberrest**, is known for art, agriculture, fire worship and trained pyromancers.

Anthea Bloodclot rose to prominence in Khaali lands, causing the widespread but incorrect assumption that she originated there. **Chieftain Sifaya Flameborn** was one of her closest allies and remains the strongest supporter of Voruun's claim to continue Anthea's project.

## Clan Voruun

[[Clan Voruun]] dominates central Rakara and controls the **Salt Spine** and **Trade Horizon**. It combines expanding cities and diplomatic skill with fighting pits, conquest-driven politics and advancement through bloodshed.

**Relka of the Chain-Eye** publicly claims that Voruun is Anthea's rightful successor and should lead a renewed Rakaran union. Only Khaali openly supports this position. Orvak, Erzhin and Temir have not accepted the claim.

The **Vorunni** present themselves as the future of Rakara. Their neighbours remain unsure whether that future means cooperation or conquest.

## Somnolent

[[Somnolent]] is an independent town where the Salt Spine and Trade Horizon meet. Although surrounded by Voruun's territorial claims, it has never submitted to clan rule.

Its open markets, foreign missions and spiritual pluralism make it a centre of trade and diplomacy. Foreign support for its independence is widely known to occur, though the exact arrangements remain a poorly kept secret rather than an openly acknowledged policy.

The phoenix is Somnolent's **civic spiritual symbol**, representing renewal, resilience and independence. It is celebrated most visibly during the [[Emberwake Festival]].

# Rana Viam

> [!summary] At a glance
> An Ardynian ruin in the forests of Orvak containing Chronarch mechanisms, a message from Saint Vaelen and the construct that guarded the Bands of Distortion.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Rakara |
| **Territory** | Erzhin hills |
| **Known builders or users** | Ardynian Chronarchs |
| **Explored** | Sessions 17â€“19 |
| **Relic recovered** | Bands of Distortion |

## Approach and entrance

The Defracturers reached Rana Viam while travelling through the Orvak forest towards Hushvale. Evidence within and around the site showed that members of the [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]] had previously searched the ruin and died there.

The entrance presented a sequence built around the commands **jump**, **cross** and **run**, establishing the ruin's pattern of testing movement, sacrifice and understanding rather than relying on an ordinary locked door.

## Vaelen's warning

Within the ruin, the party encountered a recorded message or preserved communication from **Saint Vaelen**.

The message identified relics used in the Sundering, indicated that Vaelen had hidden some of them and warned of conflict among surviving ancient figures. Vaelen expressed particular concern about Marra pursuing war at any cost and Desmon seeking divine power, while presenting Erith as a possible ally.

The message expanded the party's understanding of the ancient saints, but it did not prove that [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot]] was Saint Marra.

## The crossing chamber

One chamber forced the party to cross lanes of moving creatures and hazards. Collisions returned travellers to the start and repeated failure risked exhaustion.

A central dais accepted sacrifices of personal power, such as spellcasting resources, in return for further opportunities. The warning associated with the trial suggested that giving nothing would lead to failure.

The party eventually crossed after several dangerous attempts.

## The golem and the Bands

A great stone golem guarded the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]]. The party's first attempt ended in a decisive defeat and retreat.

After resting and preparing, the Defracturers returned. They controlled the construct with command magic and webbing before destroying it. Ieuan determined that the Bands were linked to the golem through a heart stone. The party recovered both and stored the relic in a fitted case.

A sealed Ardynian scroll was also recovered.

## Current state

- The guardian golem has been destroyed.
- The Bands of Distortion and their heart stone have been removed.
- The party left the ruin at the end of Session 19.
- Broken Fang scouts were waiting outside, beginning the pursuit recorded in Session 20.
- It is not clear whether every chamber or function of Rana Viam has been discovered.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - What does the sealed scroll contain?
> - Are there unexplored chambers or active mechanisms remaining?
> - Why had the Broken Fang been searching the site?
> - What are the full properties of the heart stone and the Bands?
> - Who originally designed the crossing trial and what was it intended to teach?

# The Emberwake Festival

The **Emberwake Festival** is [[Somnolent|Somnolent's]] most beloved celebration: an annual rite held at the dawn of spring to mark rebirth, forgiveness and the chance to begin again.

## Origins

Emberwake began as a small ritual observed by early settlers and traders. They gathered to burn old ledgers, worn goods and scraps of parchment representing burdens or misfortunes from the year before. What began as an act of letting go gradually became a beacon of second chances known across Vaelond.

## Opening the festival

The celebration takes place in **Festival Square** and is traditionally opened by three speakers:

1. The Headman of Somnolent, offering words of hope and reflection.
2. The town's leading innkeeper, blessing the crowd and providing free food and ale.
3. A rotating guestâ€”often a cleric, merchant, performer or other visitorâ€”offering a tale or blessing connected to renewal.

After the speeches, the streets fill with music, fire-dancers, games, communal feasts and storytelling. Stalls sell charms, masks and trinkets representing the shedding of the past. Festivalgoers cast old tokens, letters and keepsakes into ceremonial braziers as acts of symbolic cleansing.

## The great fire

As evening falls, Somnolent's **Elder Druid** approaches a towering structure of timber and herbs in the centre of the square. The pyre is constructed over several days and crowned with the phoenix, Somnolent's civic spiritual symbol.

A single ember taken from the hearth of the town's oldest home ignites the fire. Local tradition holds that a regret whispered into the flames will be carried away in the smoke, leaving space for a new beginning.

## The fire that faltered

Last year's festival was intended to be the grandest in living memory. **Anthea Bloodclot** had agreed to appear as the honoured guest speaker, an unprecedented gesture of cooperation between independent Somnolent and the movement she had built around the Five Clans.

The words never came.

On the morning of Emberwake, Anthea was found dead **inside her caravan**, stationed approximately two miles east of Somnolent along the southern road. At the time, the public knew little more than that she had died from unknown means. The crime scene and caravan disappeared during the festival, preventing a full public investigation.

The town erupted in confusion. Some demanded that the festival be cancelled. Others argued that a rite of renewal should honour Anthea with light rather than silence. Headman [[Romayne Drayton]] chose to continue. A local bard filled Anthea's place with a mournful song, and the Elder Druid lit the bonfire beneath a cloud of fear and suspicion.

That year's fire did not cleanse so much as mark a fractureâ€”a scar written in ash and ember.

## What the party later learned

The Defracturers later procured [[Report on the Death of Anthea Bloodclot|Alaaric Hammerfall's official report]]. It records evidence that Anthea was poisoned with **Bloodspore**, probably administered in wine, and that the original scene was removed by unknown parties before it could be properly examined.

# Somnolent

| | |
|---|---|
| **Status** | Fiercely independent town within Voruun's claimed territory |
| **Leader** | Headman Romayne Drayton |
| **Location** | Junction of the Salt Spine and Trade Horizon |
| **Known for** | Open markets, foreign embassies, religious pluralism and the [[Emberwake Festival]] |

Somnolent is both outlier and keystone: a place that belongs to no clan yet matters to every one of them.

## Sigil

![[Somnolent Sigil.png]]

The sigil of Somnolent bears a **golden phoenix on an ember-orange shield**, rising before a ring of flame. The design evokes warmth, resilience and quiet power.

- **Phoenix:** the town's civic spiritual symbol, representing rebirth, uniqueness and Somnolent's refusal to be extinguished.
- **Circle of flame:** enlightenment, renewal and spiritual openness.
- **Crossroads and landscape:** the town's role as a place of commerce, neutrality and meeting.

## Background

Somnolent began as a simple waystation at the fork of two trade routes. Merchants could rest and sell their goods without paying the shifting border tariffs of surrounding clans, instead purchasing a fair, flat merchant's licence. Its accessibility and neutrality gradually attracted peddlers, diplomats, couriers, adventurers and spies from across Vaelond.

Today, Somnolent is a neutral haven surrounded by the territory claimed by [[Clan Voruun]]. It never submitted to Anthea Bloodclot's self-styled authority, even at the height of her influence. **Headman Romayne Drayton** has preserved that autonomy through careful diplomacy and, according to persistent rumours, discreet foreign backing.

At the campaign's outset, permanent foreign embassies represented **Doro**, **Minir** and **Perfid**. Perfid's former embassy has since become [[The Defracturersâ€™ Bastion]], while Doro and Minir retain their missions. Among the Rakaran clans, **Eden Carrion** of Voruun, **Summer Quaresh** of Khaali and **Jericho Beech** of Temir maintain permanent missions. Orvak and Erzhin rely on less formal representatives within the town's druid community: **Mother Joygrove** and **Father Zantos**.

> [!note] A poorly kept secret
> Doro, Minir and Perfid are known to provide discreet support that helps preserve Somnolent's independence. The fact of that support is widely accepted; the precise arrangements are treated as rumour because no government publicly admits them.

## People and culture

The people of Somnolent are known less for aggression than for **unyielding resolve**. They have endured Voruun militias, Doroan political manoeuvring, Miniri inducements and Perfidic religious pressure without surrendering their identity.

Their culture is rooted in pluralism, respect and shared endurance. Shrines to many gods line the streets. Local druids do not serve a single deity or circle; instead, they care for the town's varied holy places, maintain balance between faiths and lead shared prayers during seasonal festivals.

At Somnolent's spiritual heart stands the phoenix, a **civic spiritual symbol** rather than the object of an organised temple faith. Residents wear its image, weave it into tapestries and burn its likeness during ceremonies. It represents renewal, independence and the possibility of beginning again.

## Trade and tensions

Somnolent's open-market systemâ€”no border tax, only a flat merchant's licenceâ€”has made it a critical hub for goods and information. The system allows traders to bypass Doroan tariffs, offers Perfid an apolitical route towards Minir and weakens Voruun's control over trade within Rakara.

The town's markets deal in textiles, spices, scrolls, alchemical goods, livestock and artisan work. Its success also places it under constant pressure. Voruun considers Somnolent a humiliating gap in its map, refusing tribute and allegiance despite being encircled by Voruun claims.

With Anthea Bloodclot assassinated just outside its walls, the town is more visibleâ€”and vulnerableâ€”than ever. Whispers of sabotage, bribery and foreign influence are constant. Somnolent nevertheless endures: a steady flame at the crossroads of power.

## Town map

![[Somnolent Town Map.png]]

### Map key

| No. | Location | Description |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | **The Dustmere Grounds** | A dirt-packed caravan ground where traders pitch tents, exchange news and rest overnight. |
| 2 | **Grethel's Yards** | Well-maintained stables for visiting merchants and their beasts. |
| 3 | **The Drowsy Coin** | The principal travellers' inn, known for stiff beds, honest stew and stories traded by candlelight. |
| 4 | **Ashwake Rest** | A place of remembrance where the Phoenix Shrine's flames burn beside the graveyard. |
| 5 | **The Crooked Hat** | Elmwood's tilted, blue-topped wizard's towerâ€”equal parts mystery and eyesore. |
| 6 | **Church of Elgira** | A serene hilltop sanctuary serving the Perfidian faith. |
| 7 | **Harth & Sons Distillery** | A riverside distillery famous for spiced rye and barrel-aged whisky, sometimes infused with unusual herbs. |
| 8 | **Town Hall of Somnolent** | The headman's seat and administrative centre of the town. |
| 9 | **Embassy Row** | Modest but distinct buildings associated with Doro, Minir and the former Perfid mission, alongside permanent Voruun, Khaali and Temir envoys. Orvak and Erzhin are represented less formally through the druid community. |
| 10 | **The Shackles** | The run-down headquarters of the Somnolent guard. |
| 11 | **Eastern Gatehouse** | A stone gate with a portcullis and small garrison, intended more to delay attackers than defeat them. |
| 12 | **The Silent Court** | A cramped, orderly office registering trade licences, property, marriages and grievances. |
| 13 | **The Scrappers' Yard** | Part arena and part training pit, used for sanctioned disputes and martial practice. |
| 14 | **Bramblehorn Stables** | Stables serving local residents and a small number of town-guard mounts. |
| 15 | **The One Ball Inn** | A rougher local inn known for gambling dice, pickled eggs, laughter and relaxed standards of behaviour. |
| 16 | **Sixhalls** | Six stone-lined market halls run cooperatively for local tradespeople. |
| 17 | **Northbend Farm** | A major local producer of crops and livestock. |
| 18 | **Lowfold** | A working district of narrow paths, garden plots and weathered cottages. |
| 19 | **Festival Square** | A cobbled plaza used for celebrations, public meetings and sanctioned market stalls. |
| 20 | **Shrines of Flow** | Modest shrines to the Sea-Mother and Moon-Father beside a spring. |
| 21 | **The Circle of Root and Stone** | An earth shrine and grove district where druids of many traditions live among stones and living huts. |
| 22 | **The Arcades** | A prosperous merchant district of artisans, traders, shingled houses and walled gardens. |
| 23 | **Lamplight Ward** | The civil district of scribes, officials and clerks, named for the lamps burning over records late into the night. |

# Tace Nox

> [!summary] At a glance
> An Ardynian ruin accessed through [[02 Places/Rakara/Thistlewound|Thistlewound]], containing temporal machinery, sacrificial research, preserved creatures and the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Staff of Broken Time|Staff of Broken Time]].

| | |
|---|---|
| **Region** | Rakara |
| **Known builders or users** | Ardynian researchers and Chronarchs |
| **Explored** | Sessions 3â€“4 |
| **Known recovered objects** | Ring of Mind Shielding, Gem of Brightness, sealed scroll |
| **Relic retained by the ruin** | Staff of Broken Time |

## Entrance and light puzzle

A sealed stone threshold at Thistlewound opened only after nightfall when the command **â€œLights Outâ€** was spoken in darkness.

Beyond the entrance, the party found chambers illuminated by sickly green light, dead robed figures and an Ardynian soldier statue. A mosaic indicated that the ruin's first mechanism required an **arrow of light** directed towards an altar. Solving the puzzle opened a portal into the deeper site identified as Tace Nox.

## Chambers of stasis

Within Tace Nox, the Defracturers encountered:

- butterflies suspended within a column of emerald temporal energy;
- two indifferent chwinga resting on stone ottomans;
- a [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Ring of Mind Shielding|Ring of Mind Shielding]];
- a Gem of Brightness;
- the Staff of Broken Time.

The ring later revealed that it contained a soul calling itself [[03 People/Notable Figures/Din|Din]].

## The laboratory

A deeper laboratory contained three hags held within magical cages, a dais sized for the **Orb of Time**, and a note describing the site's principle:

**Life, traded for progress.**

Using the Orb activated a siphoning process that restored members of the party while destroying the imprisoned hags. A creature summoned from Ballar's Bag of Rust Tricks was also petrified during the process.

The party removed a rune-sealed Ardynian scroll from the laboratory. The scroll later became an important reason for their meeting with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Elmwood Bluevine|Elmwood Bluevine]].

## Guardian and corrupted relics

A stone guardian initially demanded the return of the Staff of Broken Time. When Ieuan placed the staff into its hand, the construct aided the party against corrupted hag and dwarven relics.

The guardian was destroyed during the battle, but the Defracturers defeated the remaining creatures and recovered the staff from the rubble.

## The ruin's claim

When the party crossed the threshold to leave:

- the Staff of Broken Time vanished and returned to the ruin;
- emeralds removed from the guardian disintegrated;
- the Ring of Mind Shielding and sealed scroll remained with the party.

Tace Nox therefore appears able to reclaim at least some objects removed from its interior.

The party emerged into the aftermath of the [[02 Places/Rakara/Thistlewound|Thistlewound massacre]]. It is unclear how much time passed outside while they explored the ruin.

## Current state

- The entrance puzzle has been solved.
- The principal guardian was destroyed.
- The Staff of Broken Time remains somewhere within the site.
- The laboratory's imprisoned hags were destroyed.
- The site may still contain active temporal mechanisms and unexplored chambers.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Why does Tace Nox reclaim some objects but permit others to leave?
> - What was the laboratory attempting to achieve?
> - Who were the dead researchers found throughout the ruin?
> - What is the Orb of Time's complete function?
> - Does time pass differently inside the site?
> - Can the Staff of Broken Time be removed safely?

## Campaign appearances

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 03 - Into Buggie's Den|Session 3]]
- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 04 - Tace Nox|Session 4]]
- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 07 - Coffee and Contraband|Session 7]]

# Thistlewound

> [!summary] At a glance
> A ruined forest site several hours from Somnolent that became a refuge for Buggie's displaced goblins, bugbears and bullywugs before a massacre destroyed the camp.

## Approach

The route from [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Somnolent|Somnolent]] to Thistlewound crosses a river and waterfall using a concealed rope swing. [[03 People/Notable Figures/Terrick Lowgins|Terrick Lowgins]] guided the Defracturers there after reporting goblin activity in the area.

The outer ruins were overgrown, quiet and protected by traps and sentries.

## Buggie's refuge

Thistlewound was occupied by [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Buggie|Buggie]] and a mixed group of goblins, bugbears and bullywugs displaced from Minir.

Although members of the camp had taken part in the Emberwake attack, Buggie described his people as refugees who had entered a mercenary arrangement rather than a force seeking open war with Somnolent.

Buggie negotiated with the Defracturers and exchanged information concerning the Loyal Crow who had hired his group, the attempted abduction of Alistair Prusset and a House Bromere favour coin.

## The Ardynian threshold

A sealed stone door within the ruins bore a riddle concerning darkness, stars and extinguished light. Speaking **â€œLights Outâ€** at night opened the threshold.

The chambers beyond led to the Ardynian site known as [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]].

## The massacre

When the party returned from Tace Nox, the clearing had become a slaughterfield.

Known details include:

- Buggie and most of the camp's inhabitants had been killed.
- The victims showed wounds caused by both blades and claws.
- An unidentified drow corpse was found among the dead.
- Survivors said the attackers had been searching for a note stolen by Buggie's group.
- Buggie had torn an insignia from one attacker that Erelmor recognised as belonging to the [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]].
- Terrick and one terrified goblin survived the immediate attack.

The party searched the caves, recovered the strange silver coin from beneath Buggie's stone throne and then set fire to the camp.

## Current state

Thistlewound was left burned, blood-soaked and apparently abandoned. Its later condition is unknown.

The threshold leading to Tace Nox may remain accessible to anyone who knows how to open it.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Who ordered the attack on Buggie's camp?
> - Who was the unidentified drow among the dead?
> - Were all the attackers Rootbound?
> - What exact document were they trying to recover?
> - Did anyone return to occupy or investigate the ruins?
> - Does the route into Tace Nox remain open?

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 04 - Tace Nox|Session 4]]

# Rhul Slowdrift

> [!summary] At a glance
> A name connected to the upper ranks of the Broken Fang. Rhul Slowdrift has not been seen or directly encountered by the Defracturers.

## What is known

- Rhul is a **high-ranking member** of the [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]].
- His name has emerged during the party's investigation of the organisation.
- He is considered a possible link in the chain leading towards Broken Fang leader **Prodosius**.

## What is not known

- The Defracturers have not met Rhul.
- His appearance, ancestry and abilities are unknown.
- His exact title, territory and responsibilities have not been established.
- His current location and relationship with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] are unknown.
- There is not yet enough evidence to state that he commands all Broken Fang activity in Rakara.
