updated: 2026-07-02
---

# Anthea Bloodclot

> [!summary] At a glance
> A powerful Rakaran unifier who revived the mythic title **Lord of the Five Clans**, built a ten-year political following and was murdered shortly before she was due to speak at Emberwake.

## Rise to power

Anthea rose to prominence in the lands of [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Khaali|Clan Khaali]]. This led to the widespread but incorrect assumption that she was Khaali by origin. Her actual birthplace and early origins were never publicly established.

The title **Lord of the Five Clans** was not a recognised office before Anthea used it. She drew upon an old myth to create a personal following and spent ten years acting as mediator, war-broker and political anchor between the clans.

## Death

Anthea was found dead inside her caravan approximately two miles east of [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Somnolent|Somnolent]] on the morning of the [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Emberwake Festival|Emberwake Festival]].

The public initially knew only that she had died by unknown means. The Defracturers later obtained [[06 Library/Letters and Documents/Report on the Death of Anthea Bloodclot|the official report]], which identified likely Bloodspore poisoning administered in wine. The caravan and crime scene disappeared before a comprehensive examination could be completed.

## What the Defracturers uncovered

The Defracturers gathered evidence that:

- The [[04 Organisations/Church of Elgira|Church of Elgira]] ordered Anthea's death.
- [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Alistair Prusset|Alistair Prusset]] arranged contact with a [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]] cell.
- Rootbound agents have taken quiet credit without openly confirming responsibility.
- Perfidian Church leaders believed, or claimed to believe, that Anthea was **Saint Marra** and feared she intended to restore Ardynia.

> [!important] The Saint Marra claim
> The belief that Anthea was Saint Marra is a **Perfidian conclusion**, not an established fact. The Defracturers have found evidence that the Church acted upon this belief, but they have not independently substantiated Anthea's identity.

The message recovered at [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]] confirmed that conflict existed among figures associated with the ancient saints. It did not prove that Anthea was Marra.

## Legacy

Anthea's death fractured the agreements she had built. [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Voruun|Clan Voruun]] publicly claims the right to succeed her, with [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Khaali|Clan Khaali]] alone offering open support.

# FILE: C:\Users\ellis\Documents\vaelond-wiki\content\03 People\Notable Figures\Din.md
---

---
title: Din
type: npc
status: published
player_facing: true
aliases:
  - The Soul in the Ring
  - The Voice in Ballar's Ring
tags:
  - people
  - npc
  - campaign
  - tace-nox
  - ardynia
ancestry: Unknown
role: Soul bound within a Ring of Mind Shielding
current_status: active
last_known_location: With Ballar Novis
source: Sessions 4 and 17
updated: 2026-07-03
---

# Din

> [!summary] At a glance
> A soul preserved within [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Ring of Mind Shielding|a Ring of Mind Shielding]] recovered from Tace Nox. Din has offered Ballar guidance in exchange for help obtaining a body.

## What is known

- Din communicates privately with [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar Novis]], the ring's current wearer.
- Din has asked where the Defracturers are travelling and why they are entering Ardynian ruins.
- Din wants a body and believes Ballar may be capable of finding one.
- In return, Din has offered to guide the party through ruins where its knowledge is useful.
- Din's original ancestry, appearance, life and cause of death are unknown.

## Possible connection to Vaelen

A preserved message found at [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]] records Saint Vaelen's claim that he had made preparations to preserve his soul.

Din inhabits a ring recovered from an Ardynian site and claims knowledge that may be useful in similar ruins. This creates a possible connection between Din and Vaelen, but the party has not confirmed that they are the same being or otherwise connected.

Din's identity should therefore remain an open question rather than established history.

## Relationship with Ballar

Din's conversations with Ballar have been cautious and transactional. Din wants Ballar's help, while Ballar has demanded evidence that Din's promised guidance will be useful before committing to any bargain.

Their arrangement remains unresolved.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Is Din the name the soul used in life?
> - Is Din connected to Saint Vaelen?
> - Why was the ring stored in Tace Nox?
> - What kind of body would be suitable?
> - Would placing Din in a body free, restore or transform the soul?
> - What does Din know about the Chronarchs and their ruins?

## Campaign references

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 04 - Tace Nox|Session 4]]
- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 17 - Shrieks, Widows and Time Juice|Session 17]]

## Related

- [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Ring of Mind Shielding|Ring of Mind Shielding]]
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar Novis]]
- [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]]
- [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]]
- [[06 Library/Letters and Documents/Vaelen's sending stone|Vaelen's sending stone]]

# FILE: C:\Users\ellis\Documents\vaelond-wiki\content\03 People\Notable Figures\Prodosius.md
---

---
title: Prodosius
type: npc
status: published
player_facing: true
tags:
  - people
  - npc
  - campaign
  - broken-fang
  - minir
ancestry: Bugbear
role: Leader of the Broken Fang
current_status: active
last_known_location: Unknown
source: Sessions 3 and 7
updated: 2026-07-03
---

# Prodosius

> [!summary] At a glance
> A bugbear warlord who leads the [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]], enslaved [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong]] and remains the central target of n'Dong's pursuit.

## Known details

- Prodosius is identified as the leader of the Broken Fang.
- n'Dong has stated that Prodosius enslaved him before his escape from the organisation.
- Buggie said that his displaced tribe had fled Minir to escape the Broken Fang and knew Prodosius as a dangerous figure.
- The Broken Fang was already expanding from the Miniri borderlands towards [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Erzhin|Clan Erzhin]] when the Defracturers first learned Prodosius's name.
- During a vision caused by the mask of Verei, Prodosius appeared before n'Dong, boasted of the deaths of n'Dong's parents and threatened that n'Dong would soon die as well. Whether this vision reproduced a memory, revealed a truth or manipulated n'Dong remains uncertain.

## Place within the Broken Fang

Prodosius appears to sit above the commanders currently known to the party:

- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] once commanded the unit in which n'Dong was forced to serve.
- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Rhul Slowdrift|Rhul Slowdrift]] has been identified as a high-ranking Broken Fang figure connected to its Rakaran operations.

The precise responsibilities and relationships between Prodosius, Ulsar and Rhul have not yet been established.

## Current significance

Finding Prodosius remains one of n'Dong's principal personal objectives. The Broken Fang's interest in Ardynian ruins and relics has brought the wider party closer to that confrontation, but the Defracturers have not yet encountered Prodosius directly.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Where is Prodosius currently based?
> - Did he personally kill n'Dong's parents?
> - What role did he play in n'Dong's expedition into an Ardynian ruin?
> - Why is the Broken Fang searching for Ardynian relics?
> - How much authority do Ulsar Quinn and Rhul Slowdrift hold beneath him?

## Campaign references

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 03 - Into Buggie's Den|Session 3]]
- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 07 - Coffee and Contraband|Session 7]]

## Related

- [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]]

# FILE: C:\Users\ellis\Documents\vaelond-wiki\content\03 People\Player Characters\Ballar Novis.md
---

---
title: Ballar Novis
type: player-character
status: published
player_facing: true
aliases:
  - Ballar
tags:
  - people
  - player-character
  - defracturers
  - doro
ancestry: High elf
class: Paladin
subclass: Oath of Glory
homeland: Doro
mount: Kingcup
current_status: active
source: Sessions 1â€“20
updated: 2026-07-04
---

# Ballar Novis

> [!summary] At a glance
> A proud, conspicuous high-elf paladin from Doro; the third son of House Nirrell, whose hunger for glory, public recognition and heroic purpose often conceals the uncertainty of a noble child raised without a clear inheritance.

## Established background

Ballar is the son of the head of **House Nirrell**, one of Doroâ€™s ten lesser houses.

Although noble, wealthy and well connected, Ballar is the **third child of five** and the **third son**. His older brothers were raised as the meaningful heirs to the house, leaving Ballar with status, expectations and opportunity, but no obvious dynastic purpose.

His arrogance, peacocking and hunger for spectacle often mask that deeper uncertainty. Ballar presents himself as a natural hero because the role gives him something inheritance has not: a story in which he is necessary.

His paladin oath is associated with **glory**, public deeds and memorable achievement. In practice, this makes him both inspiring and difficult. Ballar wants to be seen doing the right thing, but he also wants that right thing to look impressive.

## Personality and purpose

Ballar is bold, theatrical and deeply conscious of how others perceive him. He is often the first to step forward, issue a challenge or present himself as the groupâ€™s most obvious champion.

That confidence can be genuine, but it is also defensive. Ballarâ€™s noble birth gives him access to rooms, people and authority that others lack, yet his place within House Nirrell remains undefined. He is important enough to carry the family name, but not important enough to inherit its future.

This tension appears in several recurring habits:

- He seeks public validation through contests, duels, speeches and visible heroics.
- He reacts badly when mocked, diminished or made ridiculous.
- He is drawn to symbols of prestige, strength and display.
- He often treats danger as an opportunity to prove he belongs in the centre of the story.

Ballarâ€™s better qualities are tied to the same instincts. He is brave, difficult to ignore and capable of putting himself between danger and his companions. He has repeatedly restored fallen allies and fought in the centre of the partyâ€™s most dangerous encounters.

## Campaign role

Ballar often acts as the Defracturersâ€™ visible champion, front-line combatant and negotiator. His confidence gives the party weight in public, especially around Doroan figures and formal authority, but it can also create complications when his personal pride overtakes the groupâ€™s shared priorities.

He helped capture [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Alistair Prusset|Alistair Prusset]], fought through the Perfid embassy operation and has repeatedly used healing magic to bring companions back from the edge of death.

During the pursuit in Session 20, Ballar captured Salla, placed him in the bag of holding and later revived [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong]] during the Egg Hunter attack.

## Doroan ties

Ballarâ€™s strongest political connection is [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ouin Brond|Ouin Brond]], Doroâ€™s ambassador in Somnolent. Ouin has served as handler, adviser and truth-teller: someone who understands both Doroan politics and Ballarâ€™s tendency to mistake attention for achievement.

Ballar has reported sensitive discoveries to Ouin, including evidence connected to the House Bromere favour coin, the Rootbound and Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s murder. Ouin has also directed him towards wider Doroan threads, including the One Ball Inn dice game and the figure known as the Old Grey Pike.

Their relationship has become increasingly personal as well as political. Ouin has challenged Ballar to consider what he is actually serving: Doro, House Nirrell, his familyâ€™s secrets, his own pride or something more meaningful.

Ballarâ€™s acceptance of [[03 People/Notable Figures/Romayne Drayton|Romayne Draytonâ€™s]] patronage complicated his Doroan status. In choosing to become one of Somnolentâ€™s agents, Ballar stepped further away from a simple role as a Doroan representative and towards a less certain, but potentially more self-directed, future.

## The Prusset fracture

The capture of Prusset exposed Ballarâ€™s divided loyalties.

After Prusset was subdued, Ballar wanted him delivered into Doroan custody. Other members of the party resisted, preferring Somnolent authority. When overruled, Ballar split from the group, acting according to his Doroan instincts rather than the emerging unity of the Defracturers.

The fracture became personal when Ballar later crossed paths with Ieuan and struck him. It remains one of the clearest moments where Ballarâ€™s pride, loyalty to Doro and frustration with his companions overcame his better judgement.

## Din and the ring

Ballar carries a [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Ring of Mind Shielding|Ring of Mind Shielding]] recovered from the Ardynian ruin of [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]].

The ring contains a speaking consciousness known to the party as [[03 People/Notable Figures/Din|Din]]. Ballar initially experienced Din as a private voice and did not immediately make the full situation transparent to the rest of the group.

Din has offered guidance in exchange for the promise of a body. The offer places Ballar in a familiar position: entrusted with something secret, potentially important and potentially dangerous. For someone uncertain of his purpose, Dinâ€™s attention may be tempting. The ring gives Ballar private significance beyond birth order, noble expectation or Doroan approval.

Dinâ€™s identity, motives and limitations remain unresolved.

## Public image and Ersil

Ballarâ€™s hunger for glory does not mean he accepts every story told about him.

When [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ersil Loquella|Ersil Loquella]] performed songs about the party, Ballar reacted badly to being turned into entertainment outside his control. He followed Ersil to his room, attacked him and held him over a balcony, demanding that the bard stop using his name.

Ersil escaped with magic and left Ballar incapacitated by Hideous Laughter.

The incident remains one of the clearest examples of Ballarâ€™s fragile pride. He wants fame, but he wants fame on his terms: heroic, flattering and controlled. Being made ridiculous cuts deeper than he likes to admit.

## Balur, chains and fighting-pit bravado

Ballarâ€™s relationship with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Balur Moonclaw|Balur Moonclaw]] reflects another side of his search for purpose.

At Emberwake, Ballar earned Balurâ€™s respect through physical contest and was offered either blacksmith training or an introduction to the fighting pits. Ballar chose the latter.

He later bought chains and manacles from Balur, leading to a display of mutual peacocking and strength-based bravado. The fighting-pit invitation remains a dormant personal hook: less a grand quest than a perfect expression of Ballarâ€™s desire to prove himself somewhere public, physical and impossible to ignore.

## Mount

[[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses#Kingcup|Kingcup]] is Ballarâ€™s recorded mount.

Beautiful, vain, difficult and fond of attention, Kingcup is an obvious match for Ballar. The choice says as much about the rider as the horse. Ballar was warned not to be foolish when choosing a mount and naturally gravitated towards the most dramatic option available.

Kingcupâ€™s elegance and temperament mirror Ballarâ€™s own self-image: impressive, stubborn, high-maintenance and convinced that being admired is part of the job.

## Active personal threads

- Decide what purpose Ballar has beyond being the third son of House Nirrell.
- Define whether his future lies with Doro, Somnolent, the Defracturers or some more personal ideal of glory.
- Understand [[03 People/Notable Figures/Din|Din]] and determine whether he can safely be given a body.
- Uncover the full relationship between House Nirrell, the Old Grey Pike and the destruction of Ashenhom.
- Decide what responsibility Ballar bears for the choices and secrets of his family.
- Resolve his strained loyalties between Ouinâ€™s Doroan guidance and the Defracturersâ€™ collective judgement.
- Determine whether his attraction to spectacle can become genuine leadership rather than performance.

> [!warning] Gaps in the current record
> - The exact duties, resources and internal politics of House Nirrell remain incomplete.
> - Ballarâ€™s older siblings have not yet been fully named or described.
> - The exact actions of each Nirrell family member during the events surrounding Ashenhom remain unresolved.
> - Dinâ€™s original identity, limitations and long-term objective have not been confirmed.
> - Ballarâ€™s life and accomplishments before the campaign are only lightly described.
> - The consequences of Ballar stepping away from formal Doroan authority have not yet fully played out.

---

specialism: Fire magic
homeland: Clan Khaali
mount: Bracken
current_status: active
source: Sessions 1-20 and original character notes
updated: 2026-07-04
---

# Enrak Kiran

> [!summary] At a glance
> A flamboyant half-elf fire mage from [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Khaali|Clan Khaali]] whose ambition, appetite for excess, ancient book and devotion to Anthea Bloodclot's legacy have drawn him away from the formal path of Khaali fire-magehood and into the dangerous search for Ardynian secrets.

## Established background

- Enrak is associated with [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Khaali|Clan Khaali]] and the fire-rich traditions surrounding Mount Khal.
- His magic is strongly focused on fire, creativity and destructive spectacle.
- He possesses an ancient book whose full significance has not yet been established.
- He has expressed a desire to become Khaali's foremost fire mage, linking that ambition to solving [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot's]] murder.
- Anthea was one of Enrak's idols, and her death appears to have shaken both his personal ambitions and his understanding of the future of the Five Clans.
- Enrak left the security of the Khaali heartlands to seek recognition through adventure, believing he could make a greater mark on his clan's future by following his own path.
- He sometimes claims that [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/Religion/Rakaran Faiths#Ashka the Ember-Dancer|Ashka]] herself encouraged this course through dreams. Whether this is sincere belief, dramatic self-mythology or a useful story depends heavily on Enrak's mood and audience.

## Personality and reputation

Enrak is intelligent, perceptive and magically talented, but his flamboyance and appetite for debauchery have repeatedly complicated the expectations placed upon him.

The formal road to fire-magehood in Khaali appears to have frustrated him. Its creative, artistic and scholarly demands offered prestige, but also constraint. Enrak sought adventure partly as escape and partly as proof that greatness could be seized outside the approved channels of clan recognition.

He is drawn to performance, praise, drinking and dramatic declarations. This has served him well when boldness, charm or spectacle are useful, but it has also made him difficult to predict. His chaotic nature can inspire allies one moment and leave them managing the consequences the next.

This pattern has become part of his reputation within the Defracturers: Enrak wants greatness, but often pursues it through instinct, appetite and fire rather than discipline.

## Campaign role

Enrak provides destructive and utility magic, with fire serving as his most recognisable answer to danger. His curiosity about ancient material has made him a natural participant in the exploration of [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]] and [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]], although his handling of dangerous knowledge has not always been cautious.

During [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 01 - Die Dog, Die|Emberwake]], Enrak killed the goblin warchanter and helped halt the attack on Somnolent. [[03 People/Notable Figures/Father Zantos|Father Zantos]] named him the hero of the hour, establishing an early public reputation that Enrak has not discouraged.

In Somnolent, Enrak developed an enthusiasm for bardic attention. He enjoyed [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ersil Loquella|Ersil Loquella's]] praise and the growing legend of the "Brazen Flame", even while some of his companions worried that songs about the party made them easier to identify. During a later conversation, Enrak told Ersil of his ambition to rise in Khaali and uncover Anthea's killer. Ersil's bitter song about Anthea, and his admission that he and Anthea were childhood friends who were not born in Khaali, gave Enrak a more personal and unsettling lead into Anthea's past.

The vision caused by [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Virei's Shattered Mask|Virei's Shattered Mask]] placed Enrak before Ashka, goddess of fire, chaos, inspiration and renewal. Ashka asked him who he was. The meaning of that question remains unresolved, but it sits at the centre of Enrak's self-image: whether he is a failed scholar, a chosen flame, a self-made legend or something stranger.

Enrak also has a personal link to [[03 People/Notable Figures/Summer Quaresh|Summer Quaresh]], a Khaali liaison in Somnolent and apparent member of Anthea's Crows. Summer was present when [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Alistair Prusset|Alistair Prusset]] was burned alive in custody and later vanished. Enrak's belief that Summer would not willingly have helped murder Prusset makes her disappearance a personal and political thread.

Within Tace Nox, Enrak helped uncover the ruin's mechanisms and recovered important Ardynian material, including a rune-sealed scroll and evidence of the Chronarch principle: life traded for progress. Later, while drunk, he proved a questionable custodian of dangerous Ardynian knowledge, forcing Ieuan to intervene and secure the material more carefully.

At Rana Viam, Enrak took part in the ruin's movement trials, yielded power to the chamber, investigated its green flames and fought in the disastrous first confrontation with the golem guarding the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]]. In the crossing chamber, his confidence repeatedly carried him into danger, including an incident in which he was petrified by a cockatrice before being restored by Ieuan's magic.

During Session 20, Enrak pursued Salla alongside Ballar, resisted the bog siren's song and was webbed during the giant-spider attack before helping to defeat the creatures.

## Mount

[[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses#Bracken|Bracken]] is Enrak's recorded mount. Bracken is slow but exceptionally steady, a useful counterpoint to Enrak's volatility. Bracken has stood firm through danger more than once, including protecting Enrak during the owlbear attack.

## Active personal threads

- Determine the origin, purpose and danger of his ancient book.
- Solve the remaining questions surrounding [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot's]] murder.
- Understand Anthea's true origin, legacy and relationship to Khaali.
- Decide what it means to become Khaali's foremost fire mage outside the formal path of clan scholarship and artistic discipline.
- Discover whether Enrak's claimed dreams of Ashka were divine guidance, useful theatre or something between the two.
- Interpret Ashka's question from the vision: **Who is Enrak?**
- Find or account for [[03 People/Notable Figures/Summer Quaresh|Summer Quaresh]] and determine what role she played in Prusset's death.
- Establish whether fame helps Enrak achieve his ambitions or simply paints a brighter target on him.
- Understand the relationship between his fire magic and the Ardynian relics or ruins now being uncovered.

> [!warning] Gaps in the current record
> - The ancient book's title, provenance, contents and current abilities are not clearly recorded.
> - Enrak's family, teachers and formal standing within Clan Khaali have not been established in detail.
> - The office, rank or recognition implied by "foremost fire mage" has not been formally defined.
> - The exact nature of his original training in Khaali fire-magehood remains unclear.
> - It is not yet established whether Enrak's Ashka dreams were genuine divine contact, personal belief or deliberate self-mythology.
> - His deeper feelings about Anthea as an idol, political figure and symbol of the Five Clans have only partly surfaced.
> - His longer-term goals beyond recognition, magical power and the Anthea investigation are not yet clear.

---
title: Erelmor

  - drow

class: Rogue

source: Sessions 1â€“20
updated: 2026-07-04
---

# Erelmor

> [!summary] At a glance
> A drow rogue and survivor of Ashenhom whose search for those responsible for the deaths of his wife and children is complicated by grief, greed, guilt and a tendency for violent solutions to create problems for everyone nearby.

## Established background

- Erelmor survived the destruction of **Ashenhom**.
- His wife and children were killed there.
- He has past knowledge of, or contacts among, the [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]].
- His central personal objective is to identify and punish those responsible for his family's deaths.
- His grief is not distant or settled. Visions, songs and memories have repeatedly dragged Ashenhom back into the present.

## Personality and patterns

Erelmor is quiet, dangerous and often difficult to read. He is capable of patience, stealth and careful observation, but that restraint can collapse quickly when Ashenhom, the Rootbound or the Old Grey Pike are involved.

He is also notably motivated by money, treasure and useful objects. His willingness to pursue valuables has drawn him into risky or morally questionable choices, including the failed attempt to rob Alaaric Hammerfall's safe in the Silent Court and his repeated willingness to accept or experiment with [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Eula's Red Coins|Eula's marked coins]].

In battle, Erelmor is effective but not always controlled. His actions can end threats quickly, but his positioning, timing and improvisation have sometimes created problems for his allies. Ballar, in particular, has often found himself suffering the consequences of the chaos around Erelmor's interventions.

## Campaign role

Erelmor is the party's scout, infiltrator and most direct practitioner of covert violence. His experience with the Rootbound and the history of Ashenhom frequently place him at the centre of investigations involving Doro, House Nirrell and the Old Grey Pike.

During the exploration of [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]], Erelmor's eye for treasure was already clear. He attempted to reach the jeweled eyes of a statue and later reacted badly when n'Dong pocketed jade crystals from the ruin. His practical skill also came through when he solved the light-arrow puzzle after the others struggled with the mechanism.

After the massacre at [[02 Places/Rakara/Thistlewound|Thistlewound]], Buggie was found dead among his slaughtered people. Buggie had torn an insignia from one of the attackers, which Erelmor recognised as belonging to the [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]]. This gave the party one of its clearest early signs that the movement was involved in the wider conspiracy surrounding Anthea Bloodclot's death.

Erelmor's sleep has repeatedly been disturbed by nightmares and by a song connected to **the Pike**. The song stirred memories of smoke, ash and the destruction of his home. In Somnolent, this led him to violently question a former soldier after hearing a connection between the song, the Maw and the One Ball Inn. The party later established that [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ervain Megbanas|Ervain Megbanas]] is the Old Grey Pike, but the full Ashenhom conspiracy remains unresolved.

The Pike thread has narrowed in some ways without being closed. Ervain once owned the House Bromere favour coin later used to pay Buggie, but lost it to Eden Carrion during a dice game. This weakened the idea that Ervain was directly responsible for the immediate Bromere-coin trail, while leaving his deeper connection to the Maw, Ashenhom and Erelmor's family unresolved.

In the basement of the [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Drowsy Coin|Drowsy Coin]], [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Virei's Shattered Mask|Virei's Shattered Mask]] forced Erelmor into a vision of his dying wife, who begged him to explain why he had not saved her. When the visions fractured, an apparition of his wife remained and attacked the party. Whether this reflected memory, guilt, manipulation by the mask or something more supernatural is unknown.

Erelmor later joined n'Dong in breaking into the Silent Court. The attempted heist was poorly controlled: they entered without a clear plan, found Alaaric Hammerfall's warded safe, failed to open it and were magically branded by its defences. The incident remains one of the clearest examples of Erelmor's appetite for valuables overriding caution.

Erelmor has also been repeatedly involved with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eula|Eula]] and her corpse-money. He accepted marked coins in payment for bodies, attempted to pass one of those coins to a farmer, and later placed a marked coin on an already dead body as an experiment. The full consequences of carrying, spending or activating Eula's coins remain unresolved.

During Session 20, Erelmor used psychic communication to keep the divided pursuit groups in contact while the party chased Broken Fang scouts from Rana Viam. He also killed the restrained first scout before the party could interrogate him, reinforcing a long-running pattern: when Erelmor sees a threat, his first instinct is often to end it, even when answers might be more useful.

## Mount

[[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses#Vesper|Vesper]] is Erelmor's recorded mount. Vesper previously carried an unconscious Erelmor away from danger during the scorpion battle.

## Active personal threads

- Identify the people and institutions responsible for Ashenhom.
- Determine the Old Grey Pike's true role in the destruction of Erelmor's home and family.
- Understand whether the vision of his wife reflected memory, guilt, manipulation by [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Virei's Shattered Mask|Virei's Shattered Mask]] or something more supernatural.
- Reconcile Erelmor's former Rootbound associations with the movement's involvement in Anthea's murder and the slaughter at Thistlewound.
- Decide how far he is willing to go in pursuit of revenge.
- Decide whether revenge, wealth or survival is now the stronger pull on his decisions.
- Understand the consequences of carrying, spending or experimenting with [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Eula's Red Coins|Eula's Red Coins]].

> [!warning] Gaps in the current record
> - The names, personalities and histories of Erelmor's wife and children are not recorded in the supplied notes.
> - His precise role and life in Ashenhom before its destruction remain unclear.
> - The nature and duration of his past Rootbound involvement are only partially established.
> - The source and meaning of the song and nightmares connected to the Pike remain unresolved.
> - The truth of the vision caused by Virei's Shattered Mask has not been established.
> - The extent of Erelmor's appetite for treasure, and whether it is simple greed or a survival response, remains open to interpretation.
> - The exact pattern of battlefield accidents and collateral harm involving Ballar is not fully recorded in the session chronicle.

# Ieuan

> [!summary] At a glance
> A wood-elf Circle of Stars druid from Clan Orvak whose reverence for the natural world is matched by deep curiosity, quiet evasiveness and an alarming willingness to bargain with dangerous living things.

## Established background

- Ieuan is from [[02 Places/Rakara/Clans/Clan Orvak|Clan Orvak]] and was regarded as a gifted young druid.
- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Father Zantos|Father Zantos]] is an important mentor, both spiritually and politically.
- His beliefs particularly involve **Orun the Verdant Kin**, **Ezaa of the Whispering Gale** and **Khurmog the Silent Deep**.
- His sister **Daear** is connected to his unresolved history with Orvak and the [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]].
- His childhood friend [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Tranwyl Bruun|Tranwyl Bruun]] later fell in with the Rootbound, making the movement a personal matter as well as a political one.

## Campaign role

Ieuan serves as the party's wilderness guide, healer and interpreter of natural or magical phenomena. His transformations, tracking and connection to the land have repeatedly enabled the group to travel, pursue targets and survive dangerous terrain.

During Emberwake, Ieuan played for subtler stakes than some of his companions. He won a magical key, chose a private follow-up with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Elmwood Bluevine|Elmwood Bluevine]] over a simple prize and was drawn into assisting [[03 People/Notable Figures/Father Zantos|Father Zantos]] with the festival ritual. When the goblin attack began, his entangling magic helped protect fleeing villagers, though his decision to ignite the festival bonfire also triggered the next stage of the assault.

Ieuan's later meeting with Elmwood revealed a more guarded side to him. When Elmwood spoke about Ardynian ruins and described details that matched [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]], Ieuan realised the party had already entered the very site Elmwood was concerned about. Rather than immediately reveal this, Ieuan initially withheld the truth and tried to manage the conversation carefully. He eventually admitted what the party had found after Elmwood pressed him on the responsibility of handling Ardynian scrolls and dangerous knowledge.

Ieuan also played a quiet but important role in the interrogation of [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Alistair Prusset|Alistair Prusset]]. After Prusset was captured, Ieuan brought [[03 People/Notable Figures/Romayne Drayton|Romayne Drayton]] to the cells and was caught within the Zone of Truth used during questioning. While the interrogation focused on Anthea Bloodclot's murder and the Church of Elgira, Ieuan asked after [[03 People/Notable Figures/Terrick Lowgins|Terrick Lowgins]], revealing that Terrick had been taken to the Perfid embassy.

Father Zantos later entrusted Ieuan with a [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Star Seed|Star Seed]], linking Ieuan's Circle of Stars path to deeper questions of faith, growth and responsibility. During the rest inside [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]], the seed produced a leaf bearing the constellation associated with Ieuan's Starry Form.

At Rana Viam, Ieuan's insight proved important after the defeat of the golem guarding the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]]. He investigated the remains and determined that the Bands were linked to the construct through a heart stone, helping the party understand how the relic had been bound and how it might be safely recovered.

In Session 20, Ieuan used **Speak with Plants** to communicate with [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Plague|Plague]], a sentient poisonous fungus. He promised to spread five cuttings in exchange for safe passage and planted the first in Salla's corpse. His later proposal to use Plague against the Broken Fang camp was designated **Plan E**.

## Father Zantos

[[03 People/Notable Figures/Father Zantos|Father Zantos]] acts as more than a mentor or source of druidic guidance. His conversations with Ieuan connect several of Ieuan's central tensions: the duties of Orvaki faith, the danger of Ardynian ruins, the burden of ancient knowledge and the compromised legacy of the Rootbound.

Zantos praised Ieuan's path as a Circle of Stars druid and linked it to Orun's wider mysteries, but he also warned him against reckless involvement with Ardynian sites. His own former affinity with the Rootbound, and his distress at their cooperation with the Church of Elgira, make him a living warning about how ideals can be twisted by fear, secrecy and foreign influence.

## Mount

[[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses#Cobb|Cobb]] is Ieuan's recorded mount: dependable, calm and sturdy.

## Active personal threads

- Understand the purpose and development of the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Star Seed|Star Seed]].
- Find Daear and establish her current relationship with the Rootbound.
- Understand what became of [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Tranwyl Bruun|Tranwyl Bruun]] and how deeply he is involved with the Rootbound.
- Decide how much trust to place in Father Zantos's warnings about Ardynian ruins, forbidden knowledge and compromised causes.
- Determine whether carrying and spreading Plague conflicts with his duties to Orun and the forest.
- Reach [[02 Places/Rakara/Hushvale|Hushvale]] and investigate the guarded state of the Orvaki settlement.

> [!warning] Gaps in the current record
> - Ieuan's early life, family structure and formal place within Orvak's druidic traditions are only partly described.
> - Daear's full history, motives and present role have not yet been established at the table.
> - Tranwyl Bruun's current location, allegiance and relationship with Ieuan remain unclear.
> - The intended stages and powers of the Star Seed remain unknown.
> - The consequences of Ieuan's bargain with Plague have not yet occurred.
> - His personal interpretation of the duties owed to Orun, Ezaa and Khurmog has not been recorded in detail.
> - It is unclear whether Ieuan's caution around Elmwood reflects distrust, responsibility, fear of consequences or a broader instinct for secrecy.

# n'Dong

> [!summary] At a glance
> A dragonborn wizard from Minir, trusted by Romayne Drayton but driven by trauma, whose enslavement by the Broken Fang, transformation in an Ardynian ruin and reckless pursuit of Prodosius have placed the mercenary organisation at the centre of his story.

## Established background

- n'Dong's parents were killed by the [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]].
- He was enslaved or forced into service by the organisation.
- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] once commanded the unit in which he served.
- An expedition into an Ardynian ruin ended in disaster and was connected to n'Dong's transformation and eventual escape.
- The memory of that ruin remains personally traumatic. When the party first identified the site beneath Thistlewound as Ardynian, n'Dong admitted that ruins like it had twisted him into what he is.
- His personal objective is to reach Broken Fang leader [[03 People/Notable Figures/Prodosius|Prodosius]].
- His magic is strongly associated with acid.
- Romayne Drayton introduced n'Dong to the others as one of his trusted agents during the first investigation after Emberwake.

## Campaign role

n'Dong is a volatile but powerful arcane combatant whose knowledge of Broken Fang procedures and personnel has repeatedly advanced the party's investigation. He is also one of the party's clearest examples of reckless improvisation: when a Broken Fang lead appears, he tends to pursue it immediately and worry about consequences afterwards.

His pursuit of Buggie's trail was not only investigative. After pressing Buggie about [[03 People/Notable Figures/Prodosius|Prodosius]], n'Dong revealed that he had followed the clue because the word "bugbear" might lead him closer to vengeance against the Broken Fang leader who had enslaved him.

He later uncovered a Broken Fang smuggling route in Somnolent, destroyed one of its whiskey shipments and recovered the [[06 Library/Letters and Documents/Broken Fang Contraband Manifest|Broken Fang Contraband Manifest]]. This investigation showed both his usefulness and his recklessness: n'Dong climbed inside a whiskey barrel to follow the shipment, discovered a drop site, sampled an unknown substance that was later identified as **Agony**, and returned with evidence of a wider network reaching Somnolent, Orvak, Voruun and Khaali.

His pursuit of the same network later led him and Erelmor to Broken Fang agents in the woods. After the agents burned n'Dong's crude shelter, the pair attacked them, extracted the name [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Rhul Slowdrift|Rhul Slowdrift]] from a man called Sunshine, and then burned the bodies to disguise what had happened. This gave n'Dong a new name in the Broken Fang hierarchy, but also reinforced his willingness to destroy evidence when cornered.

n'Dong also recovered [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Virei's Shattered Mask|Virei's Shattered Mask]] beneath the [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Drowsy Coin|Drowsy Coin]]. The mask forced the party into private visions; n'Dong saw Prodosius boasting of his parents' deaths and warning that n'Dong would soon die. After the resulting apparitions were defeated, n'Dong recited a forgotten prayer to Virei, the Shattered Mask, lifted the curse and claimed the mask. The precise meaning of the mask, the prayer and n'Dong's connection to Virei remain unresolved.

His later use of the mask as a disguise after the failed Silent Court safe robbery suggests a recurring pattern: n'Dong often responds to exposure or danger with immediate magical improvisation, even when the solution may create new risks. He concealed his identity behind Virei's visage despite being one of the only dragonborn in Somnolent.

n'Dong has also repeatedly helped erase dangerous scenes once the party has taken what it needs. He helped burn Buggie's lair after the massacre at Thistlewound, then later burned Broken Fang bodies after the woodland confrontation connected to Rhul Slowdrift. Whether this reflects practicality, panic, vengeance or habit remains unresolved.

During Session 20, n'Dong performed aerial reconnaissance, located Malt and attacked the fleeing scout from above. He was the first Defracturer knocked unconscious by the Egg Hunter Hatchlings and was revived by Ballar.

n'Dong's bluntness also colours his role in the group. He can be direct to the point of social disaster, as seen when he helped rescue Mireya and informed her of her husband's death with very little softness.

## Mount

[[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses#Nail|Nail]] is n'Dong's recorded mount: a quick, enduring former courier horse that becomes shy around battle.

## Active personal threads

- Find [[03 People/Notable Figures/Prodosius|Prodosius]] and decide what justice or revenge would actually mean.
- Confront [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] and establish his current role in the Broken Fang.
- Determine the significance of [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Rhul Slowdrift|Rhul Slowdrift]] and whether he can lead n'Dong closer to Prodosius.
- Learn why the Broken Fang is searching Ardynian ruins and relics.
- Understand the transformation connected to the ruin expedition.
- Understand why [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Virei's Shattered Mask|Virei's Shattered Mask]] responded to him, why he knew the prayer and whether the mask is safe.
- Decide whether his habit of reckless escalation is survival instinct, vengeance, trauma or something more dangerous.

> [!warning] Gaps in the current record
> - The exact chronology of n'Dong's capture, forced service, ruin expedition, transformation and escape is incomplete.
> - His original home, family life and status in Minir are not fully described.
> - It is not clear whether Prodosius personally killed n'Dong's parents or whether the mask vision showed truth, manipulation, memory or symbolic fear.
> - The nature of Virei and the mask's relationship to n'Dong remain uncertain.
> - The responsibilities of Ulsar, Rhul and others within the Broken Fang hierarchy are not yet fully known.
> - It is unclear what n'Dong intends to do if he reaches Prodosius.
> - It is unclear whether n'Dong recognises his own pattern of reckless escalation, or whether the party will challenge it before it causes greater harm.

# Broken Fang

![[Broken Fang Symbol.jpeg|240]]

The **Broken Fang** is a Miniri mercenary organisation led by [[03 People/Notable Figures/Prodosius|Prodosius]].

It is personally tied to [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong's]] enslavement and the murder of his parents, and has become one of the party's most persistent enemies.

## Known activity

- The organisation maintains a smuggling operation in Rakara, including a supply chain through the [[03 People/Notable Figures/Antoinne Cassan|One Ball Inn]].
- n'Dong destroyed a whiskey shipment and uncovered a small drop site and the [[06 Library/Letters and Documents/Broken Fang Contraband Manifest|Broken Fang Contraband Manifest]].
- [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Rhul Slowdrift|Rhul Slowdrift]] has been identified only as a **high-ranking Broken Fang member**. His precise role and area of responsibility are not yet known.
- Members searched Ardynian ruins and died within [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]].
- The organisation is seeking Ardynian relics, though its final purpose remains unknown.
- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]], known as the Glass Fang, once commanded the unit in which n'Dong was forced to serve.
- Scouts associated with Ulsar confronted the party as they emerged from Rana Viam.
- Ulsar's forces took the Defracturers' horses while the party explored the ruin.
- One scout was killed by Erelmor and two others, Salla and Malt, were captured during the subsequent pursuit.
- Salla and Malt produced [[06 Library/Letters and Documents/Broken Fang Camp Sketches|crude but broadly consistent maps]] of the nearby camp, including the approximate position of the stolen horses.
- Salla was killed during an attack by Egg Hunter Hatchlings.
- Malt survived and remained in the Defracturers' custody at the end of Session 20.
- It is not yet known whether the camp has learned what happened to the missing scouts.

# Chronarchs

![[Chronarchs Symbol.png|240]]

The **Chronarchs** were Ardynian practitioners who meddled with time magic. The partyâ€™s encounters suggest their work combined extraordinary temporal effects with a willingness to spend lives as fuel.

## Campaign record

- Tace Nox contained a device that siphoned life energy from imprisoned hags to extend or restore the lives of others.
- Other sites have contained time stasis, resetting challenges, temporal residues known as Time Juice and relics associated with the Sundering.
- The motives, hierarchy and ultimate fate of the Chronarchs remain uncertain.

# Church of Elgira

![[Church of Elgira Symbol.png|240]]

The **Church of Elgira** is Perfid's dominant religious institution. Church of the Saviour, Saviour's Church and Faith of Elgira are recognised aliases, though Church of Elgira is the most common name.

## Leadership

- **High Illuminant Derathel** is the head of the Church.
- **Arch-Illuminant Serathiel** holds a separate senior office and signed the recovered instructions sent to Alistair Prusset.

The two titles refer to different people and different positions, not an alias or continuity error.

## Known campaign position

- The Church ordered [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot's]] murder and used [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Alistair Prusset|Alistair Prusset]] to contact a [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]] cell.
- Its leadership believed, or claimed to believe, that Anthea was **Saint Marra**, one of Elgira's surviving followers, and feared she intended to restore Ardynia.
- The Defracturers have not independently proven that Anthea was Marra.
- Perfidian agents abducted and tortured [[03 People/Notable Figures/Terrick Lowgins|Terrick Lowgins]].
- Church agents escaped Somnolent with a [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Corona of the Ashen Choir|Corona of the Ashen Choir]].

## Doctrine and history

The Church teaches that Elgira deliberately sacrificed herself at the Battle of the Vale. Other surviving records and reconstructions present a different account of the Sundering.

# Loyal Crows

The **Loyal Crows** were Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s hidden agents and confidants. Their identities and structure were deliberately concealed, leaving the party to distinguish genuine loyalists from those merely invoking the name.

## Known members and claims

- [[Eden Carrion]] is identified as the Loyal Crow who hired [[Buggie]] to abduct Alistair Prusset.
- [[Summer Quaresh]] is recorded as another of Antheaâ€™s Crows.
- Antheaâ€™s note from Tace Nox says three Crows had already died and that only Anthea and Eden remained at that time.
- The groupâ€™s current structure, resources and surviving membership are unknown.

# The Rootbound

> *â€œBreak the roots, free the forest.â€*

| | |
|---|---|
| **Origin** | Dissent within [[Clan Orvak]] |
| **Structure** | Independent cells with no accepted central command |
| **Symbol** | An inverted evergreen with chained roots |
| **Shared cause** | Resistance to imposed hierarchy and outside control |

## Symbol

![[Rootbound Sigil.png]]

The Rootbound use an **inverted evergreen whose roots terminate in chains**.

- The evergreen recalls Orvak's endurance.
- Its inversion rejects the idea that tradition must always be obeyed.
- The chains represent obligations to chiefs, foreign patrons and distant rulers.

The mark is carved into wood, stone and sometimes flesh. Depending on the cell, it may be a warning, a threat or a rallying sign.

## Origins

The movement emerged as Orvaki communities were pressed to accept stronger tribal leadership and greater participation in Anthea Bloodclot's project of clan unity. Many Orvaki considered this a practical response to a changing world. The Rootbound regarded it as the surrender of local autonomy.

Their central argument was simple: Orvak drew its strength from small, resilient communities bound by kinship and custom, not from obedience to a distant chieftain, a foreign patron or a self-appointed Lord of the Five Clans.

Anthea became the movement's defining enemy. Rootbound songs described her as a **â€œpoisoned seedâ€**, an outsider whose alliances would reshape Orvak until little of its independence remained.

## One name, many movements

The Rootbound's contradictions are not separate discarded versions of the organisation. They are a consequence of its design.

Cells act independently and often pursue rival ambitions. They share symbols, coded songs and a broad hostility to imposed authority, but no leader can enforce a single interpretation of the creed. The movement's decentralisation protects it from destruction while repeatedly undermining its own message.

Some cells are **radical decentralists**. They welcome outsiders who accept local autonomy and imagine a network of self-governing communities linked by custom rather than law.

Other cells are **isolationist and exclusionary**, presenting themselves as guardians of Orvaki purity. They treat trade, migration and foreign alliances as cultural erosion.

Still others are primarily violent opportunists who use Rootbound language to justify sabotage, private vendettas or paid work. Two cells may use the same symbol while considering one another traitors.

## Methods

Actions attributed to Rootbound cells include:

- Sabotage of trade routes and foreign supply lines.
- Threats against leaders judged to be compromised.
- Poisoning and targeted assassination.
- Attacks on caravans, officials and political intermediaries.
- Use of fear to make cooperation with outsiders costly.

Because responsibility is deliberately fragmented, an act committed by one cell does not necessarily reflect the plans or approval of another.

## Anthea Bloodclot

The Rootbound have taken **quiet credit** for Anthea's death through private boasts, songs and coded claims, but the movement has never issued an open confirmation. Some cells call the killing an act of purification; others deny responsibility while celebrating the result.

The public case remains uncertain. The party's investigation has gone further.

> [!info] What the Defracturers uncovered
> The Defracturers found evidence that Perfid, acting through [[Alistair Prusset]], hired a Rootbound cell to kill Anthea Bloodclot. They have also directly observed Rootbound agents cooperating with Perfidian interests and found a Rootbound insignia among those responsible for the destruction of Buggie's gang.

## Perfid and the Rootbound

Perfid funds, shields or employs parts of the movement when convenient. This is a pragmatic relationship rather than a true ideological alliance. Rootbound rhetoric condemns foreign interference, yet individual cells accept foreign coin and protection when it advances their own goals.

That hypocrisy is not hidden by a central leadership because no such leadership exists. One cell may work with Perfid while another considers that cooperation proof of betrayal.

# The Defracturers

The adventurers were informally called the **Squishy Boys** during their earliest exploits. They formally adopted the name **The Defracturers** when they accepted [[03 People/Notable Figures/Romayne Drayton|Romayne Draytonâ€™s]] patronage in Session 11.

## Members

- [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar Novis]] â€” high-elf paladin connected to Doro and House Nirrell
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Erelmor|Erelmor]] â€” drow rogue pursuing those responsible for the destruction of his family
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Ieuan|Ieuan]] â€” wood-elf Circle of Stars druid with ties to Clan Orvak
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Enrak Kiran|Enrak Kiran]] â€” half-elf fire mage from Clan Khaali
- [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong]] â€” dragonborn wizard and former captive of the Broken Fang

## Formal status

Following Perfid's actions in Somnolent, the group entered formal patronage under Romayne Drayton. They were granted resources, political protection and use of the former Perfid embassy as a bastion.

## Household and mounts

- [[The Defracturers' Bastion|The Defracturersâ€™ Bastion]]
- [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Horses|Horses]]
- [[content/03 People/index#Bastion staff|Bastion staff]]

## Current campaign position

After Session 20:

- The party holds the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]] and their heart stone.
- Their horses remain in the hands of forces associated with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]].
- They possess two crude but broadly consistent sketches of the nearby Broken Fang camp.
- [[03 People/Player Characters/Ieuan|Ieuan]] carries four remaining cuttings of [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Plague|Plague]].
- One cutting was planted in Salla's corpse.
- Malt, a captured Broken Fang scout, remained alive and in the party's custody.
- The group had not yet agreed how to infiltrate the camp and recover the horses.

# Session 01: Die Dog, Die

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The party meets during Emberwake and defends Somnolent from a coordinated goblin attack.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 1 |
| **Recorded** | 22 July 2025 at 21:22 |
| **Arc** | Emberwake |

The quiet town of [[Somnolent]] stirred to life with the opening of the [[Emberwake Festival|Emberwake festival]], a celebration of fire, unity, and remembrance. Though joy permeated the air, a sombre undercurrent lingeredâ€”the memory of Anthea Bloodclot, the former leader of the Five Clans, whose passing the year prior cast a long shadow over the festivities.

Three voices opened the day:

- **Romayne Drayton**, the village headman, spoke first. His words were carefully chosenâ€”earnest, charismatic, but subdued, honoring Anthea's memory with the dignity it deserved.
- **Alistair Prusset**, the [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] ambassador, followed. He was formal, distant, and faintly dismissive of the festivalâ€™s spiritual elements, though he managed to deliver his remarks with a minimal degree of respect.
- Then came **Callum Redmayne**, landlord of the Drowsy Coin, whose raucous charm and infectious energy kicked off the revelry with cheer and drink.

The party, strangers still, spread across the fairgroundsâ€”each drawn into different distractions, unwittingly threading their fates closer together.

## Festival Highlights

- **Erelmor** stepped boldly into competition, losing a drinking contest to the local legend Billy Hill with no loss of dignity. He later tried his hand at archery with the hunter **Terrick**, who noted Erelmorâ€™s latent potentialâ€”and shared a troubling rumour of a goblin camp spotted near the forest edge.

- **Enrak** found himself the recipient of a warm gesture from the kind-hearted **Xene**, who offered him festival food with a touch of flirtation. Later, he gambled with the mysterious wizard **Elmwood**, but misread the gameâ€”watching the old manâ€™s hands, not the ghostly mage hand that shuffled the cups.

- **Ballar** thundered his way through the games. He bested **Alaaric Hammerfall**, the town treasurer, at diceâ€”perhaps sparking the ember of rivalry. He then crushed the **Hammertoss**, earning the respect of **Balur Moonclaw**, who offered to introduce him to a local fighting pit. Ballar left with two prizes: a **Bag of Rust Tricks** from **Sable**, and a **Hewardâ€™s Handy Haversack**.

- **Ieuan** played for subtler stakes. After pinning the tail perfectly on a donkey (to everyone's surprise), he was rewarded with a **magical key**. He won again at a cup shuffle game, but instead of a prize, chose a private follow-up with Elmwood. Before long, **Mother Joygrove** found him and requested his aid for **Father Zantos** during the coming ritual.

## The Goblin Attack

Just as Father Zantos began the Emberwake invocation, celebration turned to chaos. A dogâ€™s chilling death rattle shattered the ceremony, followed by goblin war-songs. Where the crowd fled, four soon-to-be heroes stood firm.

Erelmor and Ballar surged forward, gleefully trying to outdo one another with cries of â€œwatch this!â€ as they felled goblins in a deadly display of theatre and pride. Meanwhile, Ieuan and Enrak found their fire magic sputtering, failing to ignite fear in the goblinsâ€”but **Ieuanâ€™s Entrapment spell** held the line, ensuring the villagers could flee in safety.

Sensing a greater threat, **Ieuan ignited the bonfire**, intending to warn the townâ€”but instead triggered the next wave. The magical phoenix flare, meant to symbolize rebirth, was in fact a **signal for the goblin warchanter** and her kin to join the fray and burn it all.

Still, the party fought on. Enrak, burning with frustration and flame, stepped into the breach to deliver the **final blow**, incinerating the goblin warchanter and halting the assault. As Father Zantos rushed to tend to the wounded, he declared **Enrak the hero of the hour**â€”a pronouncement that didnâ€™t sit well with his more flamboyant allies.

## Second Wave: Bridge Ambush

No rest for heroesâ€”**a scream rang out** from the bridge. The ambassador, Alistair Prusset, and his loyal hound were under attack. The party sprinted to the scene, but arrived just in time to see the houndâ€™s **throat torn open** by a goblin bossâ€™s dogslicer.

What followed was a mess of half-planned heroics:

- Erelmor finished the goblin dog with efficient violence, before finding the sharp end of a dogslicer and retreating.
- Ballar, ever the opportunist, moved to protect Prusset attempting to slyly curry favour with the ambassador.
- Ieuan and Enrak hurled fire spells againâ€”but the goblin boss seemed warmed at most by the flame, shrugging off their assaults.

A second goblin wave surged from the treeline, surrounding Ballar. From a distance, the others peppered the attackers as best they could.

Eventually, **Erelmor brought down the goblin boss**, and the tide turned. As one last goblin fled and dove into the river to escape, **Ballar drew from his bag of tricks** â€” and a **giant boar** splashed into being, chasing and crushing the fleeing goblin beneath the water.

## Session End

As the blood settled into the earth and the villagers crept cautiously back to assess the wreckage, the four adventurers found themselves **changed**â€”strengthened by danger, tempered by fire, and perhaps for the first time, connected by shared purpose.

They **rose to level 2**, not just as survivors, but as the first sparks of something greater.

# Session 02: The Ashes of Emberwake

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The first clues point towards Buggie and a Crow as the party follows Terrick towards Thistlewound.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 2 |
| **Recorded** | 12 August 2025 at 22:12 |
| **Arc** | Emberwake and Thistlewound |

The firelight had faded, but the embers still glowed. In the aftermath of the goblin attack, *[[Somnolent]]* stirred with caution. The party, newly forged in battle, now stood beneath smoke-choked skies unsure of what came next.

**Ambassador Prusset**, still reeling from the death of his faithful hound, met the group in grim silence. **Ballar** stepped forward with a gesture of respect, offering to see to the dogâ€™s burial personally. Prusset, ever the diplomat, declinedâ€”keeping his grief neatly folded behind icy eyes.

Before tensions could rise further, **Romayne Drayton** arrived to reclaim control. Measured and authoritative, the village headman called for calm, asked the party to visit him in the morning, and promised to speak of next steps.

The group searched the goblin dead and found two strange clues:

- A **note** scrawled in barely-legible goblin:
  *"No kill, just scare. Get Pruzet. Crow want alive. Buggie."*
- A **large silver coin**, odd in make and unmarked by any known mint.

Prusset denied knowledge of the name "Buggie," and Drayton, while unfamiliar with the goblin, noted with unease that "Crow" had once referred to **Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s hidden agents**, the quiet, loyal few who operated in her shadow.

The heroes took their leave, agreeing to regroup at the Drowsy Coin in the morning. Each went their own wayâ€”with Ballar returning to the **[[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] consulate**. There, he encountered **Ambassador Ouin**, a woman of silk and sharp instincts, caught off guard by the muddy menagerie that trailed behind Ballar. Upon hearing of Ballarâ€™s involvement in the ambassadorâ€™s rescue, Ouin gave an approvingâ€”if wearyâ€”nod, but warned: *â€œRemember where your loyalties lie. Doro first. Always.â€*

## Picking up Threads and Receipts

Morning broke with grease and gossip. Over **breakfast boar baps** at the Drowsy Coin, the group reunited and learned that **Elmwood**, the local wizard, had performed powerful magic to protect the tavern and its patrons during the attackâ€”a fact shared with no small amount of admiration by **Callum Redmayne**, which seemed to inspire some jealousy in **Enrak**.

They then returned to **Romayne Drayton**, who spoke openly and plainly. With the village still rattled and official aid slow to arrive, he asked for the partyâ€™s support in uncovering the truth behind the attack. To assist, he offered one of his trusted agentsâ€”**nâ€™Dong**.

Drayton laid bare the fragile unity left in Antheaâ€™s wake. She had been more than a leaderâ€”she was the very thread holding the Five Clans together. Her â€œCrowsâ€ were real, though anonymous. Now, only the **Voruun** and **Khaali** still marched toward her dream. **Erzhin** had withdrawn, and **Orvak**, plagued by its **[[Rootbound]]** movement, teetered on the edge of internal collapse. Surprisingly, he also admitted that Bloodclot had very few loyal to her in Somnolent, a town that prided itself on its neutrality and ability to stay out of clan skirmishes, but if anyone was still loyal to Anthea then **Eden**, the Voruun liaison, would know.

With no clear trail to follow but the goblin note, Drayton suggested seeking out the hunter **Terrick** or reviewing the **report** he had submitted.

Thus, the party sought out **Alaaric Hammerfall**, treasurer of Somnolent and a man not easily parted from his ledgers. After some bartering, Alaaric sold them **Terrickâ€™s report** for a single gold coin. Ballar, ever the dealmaker, pressed furtherâ€”inquiring about a report on **Antheaâ€™s death**. Alaaric confirmed its existence, but explained the delicate material would take some time to procure.

## Chasing Waterfalls

With the trail confirmed, the party visited Terrick himself in the **village arcade**. He was eager to helpâ€”though **Erelmor**, in an almost performative show of suspicion, accused the man of failing to hunt his own rabbits. Only **Ieuan** and **nâ€™Dongâ€™s** timely intervention calmed the rising tension. Terrick offered to guide the group to **Thistlewound**, the site of his goblin sighting, but warned they should arrive before nightfall.

Their journey was swift but not uneventful. Hours into the trip, they arrived at a river, where a **waterfall roared across their path**. Terrick, showing surprising agility, demonstrated a hidden **rope swing** and leapt across with ease.

**Ieuan**, inspired by the display, attempted the sameâ€”and promptly botched it. With a *sickening splash*, he slammed into the river and immediately drew the attention of a **crocodile**. As the beast snapped its jaws around him, **Ballar dove in** to assist, slashing at thin air as the rest of the party fired spells and arrows.

With death looming, **Ieuan** thrust his hands into the beastâ€™s mouth and unleashed a toxic blast of **poison spray** down its throat, felling it from within. Terrick pulled him to safety as the other crocs were dispatched.

Amid the aftermath, **Ieuan spotted something behind the waterfall**. Working with **Enrak**, the two used their **mage hands** to retrieve a waterlogged **wooden cache**, which contained **eight healing potions**â€”a windfall they would soon be grateful for.

## Obvious Riddles & Unexpected Bullywugs

The final leg of their journey led them to the crumbling edges of **Thistlewound Ruins**, overgrown and silent.

**Ieuan**, attuned to whispers on the wind, caught goblin voices murmuring in the distance. Soon after, the group encountered a **sealed stone door** bearing an inscription:

*The moon and stars may show the way, To knowledge lost, kept sealed away.*

*I guard what sleeps where silence grows, No flame may flicker, no lantern glow.*

*In still of dark, speak sleep's last shout, Snuff the candles and call lights out.*

Ieuan instantly divined the meaning: *Return at night, in darkness, and speak â€œLights Out.â€* It was, in his own words, *â€œobvious.â€*

With time to kill, the party scouted furtherâ€”**Erelmor**, perhaps too eager, **triggered a trap**, alerting a mix of **goblin and bullywug guards**. The ensuing battle was dramatic, chaotic, and deeply pyrotechnic.

- Spells flew with reckless abandon.
- **Erelmor** doused a bullywug in oil just to see it burn.
- **nâ€™Dong** and **Enrak** emptied their arcane reserves in wild displays of poison and flame.

More enemies surged from the shadowsâ€”but just before bloodshed could resume, a massive **bugbear** emerged from the cave entrance, barking a command:

> **â€œEnough! If you want answers, we talk. Not fight.â€**

The battlefield fell still.

# Session 03: Into Buggie's Den

> [!abstract] Session summary
> A tense parley with Buggie reveals new enemies before the party enters the Ardynian ruins beneath Thistlewound.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 3 |
| **Recorded** | 06 September 2025 at 14:19 |
| **Arc** | Thistlewound |

## Into the Bugbearâ€™s Den

In the damp throat of the wilds, beneath the looming stone of Thistlewound and the weight of unseen eyes, the party stood face-to-face with a monster.

**Buggie**, towering and scarred, was not the frothing warbeast they expected, but a warlord weary of blood. Behind him, goblins and bugbears watched in silenceâ€”not the frenzied swarm that had assaulted [[Somnolent]], but sullen, displaced kin, licking old wounds beneath mossy banners.

The tension was immediate and knife-sharp. The party, never ones to flinch, **openly admitted to killing Ryzdak**, Buggieâ€™s commander, and **Erelmor** in particular seemed to savour the admission. Buggie, to his credit, did not rise to anger. Instead, he spoke of what truly mattered: **a coin** Ryzdak carriedâ€”an insurance policy.

Feigning ignorance, the party offered a deal. In return for information on the **Crow** who had hired them, they would help recover the coin. But patience was wearing thin. Words stretched taut. Finally, an accord was struck.

**Buggie would give them everything he had**: names, fragments, and a **document** smuggled at the Crowâ€™s requestâ€”her last order before she disappeared. The failure to capture Ambassador Prusset meant, Buggie admitted with cold certainty, *they would likely never see her again.*

With all eyes watching, **Erelmor clumsily fumbled the handoff**, forcing **Ballar** to complete the transaction. As his fingers closed around the object, he realized the truth: this was no ordinary trinket. It was a **ceremonial coin**, minted in **[[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]]**, bearing the unmistakable crest of **House Bromere**.

Before they could leave, a **goblin warchanter demanded retribution** for her sisterâ€™s death. **Erelmor**, quick-witted even under threat, wove a half-truthâ€”claiming they saw her die, yes, but not by their hand. The lie held. Barely.

As they turned to leave, **nâ€™Dong**, silent till now, **pressed Buggie about a name from his pastâ€”Prodosius**. The bugbear's answer was chilling. His tribe had **fled [[The Powers That Be#Minir|Minir]]** to escape the Broken Fang, a ruthless force growing bolder, expanding from their borderland camp near **[[Clan Erzhin]]**. Of Prodosius, Buggie knew littleâ€”but enough to stoke a fire in nâ€™Dongâ€™s heart.

## Putting the pieces together

Smoke still clung to their clothes as the party emerged from the goblin hideout, the echoes of parlay with **Buggie the bugbear warlord** lingering in the air. The uneasy truce held, but beneath the surfaceâ€”suspicion festered.

As the group trudged through the underbrush, **Ieuanâ€™s jaw tightened**, eyes fixed on **nâ€™Dong**. Without warning, he pulled the towering dragonborn aside, voice low and sharp.

**nâ€™Dong didnâ€™t flinch.** Instead, he exhaled a truth that hung heavy in the forest air. He had once been **enslaved by Prodosius**, a bugbear warlord whose name still made his fists curl. He hadnâ€™t joined this quest for coin or glory. Heâ€™d seen the word *bugbear* in that crude goblin note and followed it like a bloodhound, knowing vengeance might lie at the end of the trail. Trust, once strained, began to realignâ€”though not fully heal.

With spell slots empty, the party finally took some time to breathe â€” and think. They turned over what they had learnt like bones in a fire, watching the shadows shift and dance, and piece by bitter piece, a story began to form.

Theories became conviction as **Ieuan**, **Enrak**, **Ballar**, **Erelmor**, and **nâ€™Dong** laid the web bare:

- Anthea Bloodclot didnâ€™t die by fate. She was silenced.
- The [[Perfidian Faith|Church of Elgira]], threatened by her influence and unity, whispered the command.
- And it was Prusset, the [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] ambassador, who gave the orderâ€”using a shadowy Orvaki faction called the [[Rootbound]] to do the deed.

The assassination of Anthea was not just a murderâ€”it was a **betrayal of the Five Clans**, a scalpel wound to their fragile alliance. Now, at least one of her hidden **Crows** was calling in old debts, with **Prusset** in her sights.

## Into the dark

Before them loomed the sealed stone threshold of **Thistlewound**, the ruins whispering secrets older than the clans themselves. *"Call Lights Out"* The words had lingered since Ieuan had first deciphered the ancient riddle, and now, as twilight clung to the edges of the forest, the moment had come.

There was hesitation. Some advised caution. Others waited for a sign.

**Erelmor** did not.

With the recklessness of a man chasing ghosts, he stepped into the dark without waiting for his allies. The others followed, some with blades drawn, some with prayers muttered. **Ballar**, bringing up the rear, exhaled a long-suffering sigh that echoed faintly into the depths.

The air shifted the moment they crossed the threshold. **Suffocating pressure**, oppressive and humid, settled over them like a damp shroud. The ruins pulsed with a sickly **green light**, and something unnatural stirred in the walls.

They crept through narrow halls when a **Chwinga**â€”a flickering, fey-like spiritâ€”darted across their path and vanished through solid stone. A harmless omen, perhaps. But to nerves already taut, it felt like a warning.

In the first chamber, a flash of light drew their eyes to a grim statue of an **[[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] soldier**. A staff vanished from its grasp, swallowed by unseen forces. Instinct screamed at them to turn back. Instead, they doubled down.

**Ballar**, growing suspicious, cast forth a **brown bear** from his Bag of Rust Tricks, using the creature as a trap tester.

Meanwhile, **Ieuan** shifted into the form of a rat to scout ahead. Neither beast found any trapsâ€”but what they did find was worse: **bodies**. Dozens. Strewn across rooms and corridors, decaying in robes of faded glory. The stink of **death** clung to the stone like moss.

And yet, amidst this desecrated sanctum, **Erelmor** looked up at the jeweled eyes of the statue and thought, *I want those.* He attempted to scale itâ€”failedâ€”and fell with a graceless thud. Rat-Ieuan bit him on the neck in reprimand.

Their path led them to what remained of a **living quarter**, aged and dust-choked but intact. Among cracked tomes and shattered furniture, they uncovered:

- An **Orb of Time**
- Books cataloguing **creatures of immense lifespans**â€”liches, dragons, phoenixes.
- And most damning of all, a **discarded letter** penned by **Anthea Bloodclot** herself, revealing she had once entered these ruins and encountered strange puzzles and deadlier things still.

Anthea's letter confirmed the origin of the ruins. **Ardynian.** The name alone was enough to chill **nâ€™Dong**, who confessed in a rare moment of openness: *It was ruins like theseâ€¦ Ardynianâ€¦ that twisted me into what I am.* The others could only wonder what had once happened to him in the dark.

In a tomb-like chamber, the bearâ€”still loyally blundering aheadâ€”**kicked open a door**, revealing three monstrous **giant centipedes**. Acting quickly, Erelmor turned the ground into chaos, flinging a sack of **ball bearings** to slow their advance.

It didnâ€™t work.

The pedes simply climbed the walls and descended upon the party from above.

The fight was fast and vicious, but the party stood strong. **Ballar**, ever the finisher, struck the final blowâ€”again.

While Erelmor grudgingly gathered up his scattered bearings, the others investigated:

- **Ballar and Enrak** retrieved more **healing potions** from near the sarcophagi.
- **nâ€™Dong** found three **jade crystals** embedded in an altar, which he pocketed swiftlyâ€”drawing a glare from Erelmor.
- A **mosaic** confirmed the earlier noteâ€™s clue: the solution to the ruinâ€™s puzzle lay in creating an **arrow of light** pointing toward the altar.

**Ballar**, seized by a sudden burst of industrious madness, ran through the ruins **tearing sconces from the walls**, hoping to piece together the arrow manually.

While Ballar made his frantic rounds, **nâ€™Dong, Ieuan, and Enrak** discovered the real puzzle: an ancient mechanism already half-triggered by the bearâ€™s heavy paws.

Panic set in. Had they already failed?

They regrouped, tensions high. Ballar scolded his bear. The others argued. Then, without warning, **Erelmor and nâ€™Dong** stepped forward â€” and triggered the puzzle prematurely.

Green light **flickered** violently through the chamber. The ruins screamed. From the shadows emerged a **skeletal warhorse**, its hooves crackling with unnatural energy. The battle was fierce, but fleeting. The party, galvanized by fear, fell upon it. **Ballar**, resolute and righteous, delivered the final strikeâ€”again.

Not wanting to tempt fate a second time, they approached the puzzle with care. **Ieuan** scratched diagrams into the dust, frantically testing combinations. **Ballar** declared it impossible. **Erelmor**, tired of the noise, **found the answer**.

With a shudder and a groan, the puzzle accepted their resolution. The green light pulsed, focused, and coalesced into a swirling **portal above the altar**.

It opened. But what lay beyond?
