# Session 04: Tace Nox

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The party confronts time magic, sacrificial machinery and corrupted relics within Tace Nox.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 4 |
| **Recorded** | 23 September 2025 at 21:20 |
| **Arc** | Tace Nox |

## Relics and Ruin

The puzzle was solved, the path was open â€” but beyond dark flickering with green light, only deeper shadow waited. A narrow tunnel beckoned, too much of a squeeze for Snippy the bear. As the loyal creature let out a low, uncertain huff, Ballar gave him a gentle pat and a quiet word: â€œWait here. Weâ€™ll be back.â€

They would not.

The next chamber felt colderâ€”not in temperature, but in spirit. At its center rose a column of flickering emerald light, strange and pulsing like a heartbeat. Suspended within were two butterflies, motionless in stasis. To either side, a Chwinga lounged upon stone ottomans, unmoving, unblinking, uncaring.

The party approached gently this timeâ€”offering food, soft words, and hesitant hands. The chwinga remained indifferent. At least they werenâ€™t hostile.

The ottomans, however, held more interest. nâ€™Dong stepped up to open oneâ€¦ and failed spectacularly, slipping backward with a thud as if repelled by the stone itself. Ballar, with a grunt and a flex, opened it with easeâ€”finding 100 gold and a Ring of Mind Shielding, which Ballar quietly equipped and hid without mention.

The other ottoman held more goldâ€”shared more freelyâ€”and a hagâ€™s eye, which vanished swiftly into Erelmorâ€™s possession.

They turned at last to the green column. An inscription lay etched at its base:

â€œSlowed by radiance and trapped in time,

Release the static from their binds.

One to protect you from the relics,

One to illuminate the exit.â€

They tried everything: brute force, magic, cleverness. But it was nâ€™Dong and Ballar, acting in tandem, who reached into the stream of power and retrieved their rewards:

- A mysterious magical staff, humming with latent time magic.
- A gem of brightness, blazing with inner light.

The moment they were freed, the portcullis ahead slid open, beckoning them onward.

## The crucible of stolen time

They descended into a narrow corridor, each step heavy with tension, until it opened into a long-abandoned laboratory, its very air vibrating with arcane pulses.

There, they found three caged hags, suspended in twisted magical stasis, each one a flickering shell of its former self. In the centre: a dais and plinth, the perfect size for the Orb of Time.

Erelmor, ever impulsive, plunged his hand into one of the magical cages â€” nearly losing it in the process. Ballar threw himself at a sealed door, to no avail. But Enrak, combing the desk, found:

- A rune-sealed scroll bearing the sigil of a sand timer.
- A note, left by an unknown [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] researcher, explaining the ritualâ€™s purpose: Life, traded for progress.

So, with no other option presenting itself, they began. Erelmor, first to act, placed the orb and initiated the siphoning ritual. As arcane light wrapped around him, one hag crumbled to ashâ€”and he felt restored, empowered, like after a long rest. A giftâ€¦ bought with a life. nâ€™Dong followed, then Ieuan, who watched as the second hag crumbled to dust. The sealed door opened, allowing further passageâ€”but the final hag remained, twitching in half-life.

They hesitated. Morality battled pragmatism. Then Ballar, in an act of grim compromise, tossed a goat from his Bag of Rust Tricks onto the dais. The goat bleated once, then froze in mid-scream as the ritual drained it. The third hag died. The goat turned to stone.

Before they could reflect, a chilling sound cut through the ruins. Snippyâ€”their stalwart bearâ€”was dying.

## The guardian and the relics

Racing through the newly opened path, the party arrived at a grand stone chamber where the Guardian awaitedâ€”its massive form carved in Ardynian fashion, animated, and now barring their exit.

In silence, it extended a hand, beckoning the party to return the staff. nâ€™Dong refused. Erelmor fired.

The guardian reeled back â€” but not in retreat. It stepped aside, revealing the true threat: relics of hags and dwarves, corrupted by the same magic that had defiled these ruins, now rising to attack. Ieuan, with sudden clarity, darted forward to yank the staff from nâ€™Dongâ€™s grasp and place it in the guardianâ€™s hand.

The construct nodded â€” and responded with power. A protective aura washed over the party, granting bonus vitality and resistance for the battle ahead. Then the room erupted into war.

## The time war

The Relics were monsters of another age â€” screeching, relentless, forged from bone and corruption. The party met them head-on.

- Ieuan, now a hulking elk, gored and slammed his way through the battlefield.
- Ballar, as ever, stood unflinching in the storm, holding the line against four dwarven relics â€” all weakened by Snippyâ€™s final stand.
- Erelmor and Enrak flung spells like fire and daggers, dancing between foes.
- nâ€™Dong, cold and calculating, let loose his arcane fury.

The guardian fought beside themâ€¦ but was the first to fall. Its stone frame cracked and shattered under the hagsâ€™ savage assault, the time-magic staff falling to the floor. The tide turned.

Ieuan, desperate to shift momentum, cast Faerie Fire, lighting the enemies like candles â€” despite catching Ballar in the blaze. It wasnâ€™t enough. Ballar went down, blood soaking the ruin floor. Ieuan followed, cut down by a hagâ€™s screeching magic.

Now just three remained. Outnumbered, outmatched, and nearly out of spells, Enrak, Erelmor, and nâ€™Dong made their final stand. There was no glory â€” only grit. They fought with the cold knowledge that failure here meant dying forgotten in an ancient tomb.

And then, through the smoke and blood, nâ€™Dong raised his hand. A Magic Missile, three bolts of pure force. The chamber fell silent.

## The Price of Power

The remaining few still standing dragged their allies back from the brink with potions and trembling hands. Together, they searched the rubble â€” reclaiming the Staff of Broken Time, and prying free emeralds from the guardianâ€™s shattered face.

Victory. At last.

But as they stepped across the threshold to leaveâ€¦ everything changed.

The staff vanished. The gems disintegrated. The Ardynian ruin, ever greedy, took back its gifts. What it left themâ€¦ was horror.

The quiet clearing of Thistlewound was gone. In its place, a slaughterfield. Blood soaked the earth, limbs and steel lay scattered, the grass torn to shreds.

And among the carnage, the party stood blinkingâ€”alive, but only just. And the war, it seemed, had already begun.

---

# Session 05: Fingers, Coins and Incriminations

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The Thistlewound massacre, cursed coins and a roadside ambush deepen the conspiracy around Anthea and Perfid.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 5 |
| **Recorded** | 02 October 2025 at 11:14 |
| **Arc** | Return from Thistlewound |

## Thrones, Fires and Fingers

Smoke still clung to their lungs and blood to their boots as the party stepped out of the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] ruin, hoping for daylight, perhaps clarity. Instead, they found **carnage**.

What had once been the **clearing of Thistlewound** - quiet, still, strangely untouched â€” was now a stage set for horror. Bodies lay in ruin, **twisted and torn**, blood slicking the grass, steel shattered, the stink of death rising thick and wet from the soil.

The party spread out, hearts racing, stomachs tight.

- **Ieuan**, ever perceptive, knelt by a corpse that didnâ€™t belongâ€”a **drow**, out of place, his tunic torn and stained in dark violet. No one knew who he was. But someone had wanted him dead.
- **Enrak** examined another, noting the **brutality of it all**â€”both **blades and claws** had done the work. This wasnâ€™t clean. It wasnâ€™t professional. It was **desperate**.
- **Ballar** found what remained of **Buggie**, the bugbear warlord whoâ€™d once ruled this sorry patch of forest. He tore through the corpse, searchingâ€”urgently, almost obsessivelyâ€”for the **strange silver coin** he'd handed over just hours earlier. He found **nothing**.

Unseen by the others, **Erelmor** slipped away. Perhaps out curiosity or his rogue instinct to act alone. He crept toward the edge of the massacre, eyes peeled, bow nocked. A branch snapped. He spun, arrow readyâ€”but held his fire just in time as **Terrick** emerged from the trees, eyes wide, a single **terrified goblin** trailing behind him like a lost child.

The party reconvened, pressing both survivors for answers, but got little more than fragments. The attackers â€” whoever they were â€” had come **looking for something**. A **note**, they said. Stolen by Buggieâ€™s gang.

That was enough for **Ballar**, who wasted no time declaring the goblin their **â€œguest.â€** His tone didnâ€™t leave room for refusal.

**Terrick**, understandably, protested the idea of returning to [[Somnolent]] without rest â€” but was overruled. There was **something hunting in these woods**, and the party didnâ€™t intend to sleep where it might find them. As **nâ€™Dong, Enrak, and Ieuan** decided to search Buggieâ€™s Den, **Ballar** made a quiet request of **Erelmor**: a trophy. Something to show **Alistair Prusset**. Something to prove Buggie was truly dead. A **finger**, perhaps.

Erelmor, ever the dramatist, had a grander visionâ€”**the head**. Unfortunately, enthusiasm far outpaced skill. He hacked away, **missing the neck entirely**, tearing through **muscle and bone**, coating himself in bugbear blood like a mad butcher. When it was finally too much even for him, he took the finger. Just the finger. A **sickening rip**, and it was done. But something else dropped from the grasp of the dead bugbear chief; an insignia ripped from one of the assassins â€” one Erelmor immediately recognised as belonging to his old allies **the rootbound**.

Meanwhile the rest of the party inspected the cave that had once echoed with goblin songs and bullywug croaks. There, **nâ€™Dong** uncovered a **hidden compartment** beneath the stone throne. Inside: **50 gold**, and more importantly, **the strange silver coin**. The one Buggie had demanded be returned to him.

Then, for reasons only the gods might understand, **nâ€™Dong, Enrak, and Ieuan** decided to **torch the camp**. Whether it was vengeance, cleansing, or pure spite was unclearâ€”but the smoke rose high into the night as they **stumbled coughing** from the cave, eyes stinging, lungs scorched.

They emerged to see Buggie's corpse defiled, a shocking scene of Erelmor's "handiwork". nâ€™Dong vomited on the grass.

## Blood for gold

With all â€œloose endsâ€ tied, corpses looted and burned, trophies in hand, and a shell-shocked goblin in tow, the group turned toward the **moonlit path back to Somnolent**. Still bloodied from the ruins, lungs raw from smoke, and nerves stretched thin, the party trudged deeper into the night. Their path home was far from peacefulâ€”and fate had no intention of granting them rest.

They found them by moonlight: a **ragtag band of outlaws**, deep in debate. Sat in a loose circle, they bickered over a **haul of gold**â€”not how to spend it, but whether to **keep it** at all. Voices cracked with unease. Fear and greed clouded their thoughts, hanging over their fire like smoke. It should have been a warning.

But **Ballar**, bold as brass and tired of walking, stepped into their midst and invoked the image of Talik the laughing coin as he sang: *â€œSpinner of fortunes.â€.* It didnâ€™t go down well. Though the bandits outnumbered them and the party was battered, bruised, and sleepless, **the Squishy Boys did not falter**. As blades were drawn and magic flared, battle erupted in the forest clearing.

As the clash began, their **goblin prisoner** seized his chance. With Terrick fumbling behind, the goblin darted into the treesâ€”gone like a whisper. **Ballar**, still dripping with the blood of the bandit leader he'd just crushed, rounded on Terrick with a **burning fury**, all but roaring his incompetence before launching into the woods in pursuit.

Meanwhile, **nâ€™Dongâ€™s magic missile** carved through the remaining bandits like divine judgment, ending the skirmish in a flash of arcane light. Back at the treeline, **Ieuan**, flanked by a sheepish Terrick and a furious Ballar, somehow managed to persuade the goblin to stay put, despite Ballar making no attempt to hide that the goblin would die in Somnolent.

But something **stranger** was happening behind the battleline. As **Erelmor** plucked a **scimitar** from the fallen bandit leader and immediately tossed it away with an audible sneer (â€œworthlessâ€), a **hooded woman** appeared from **thin air**â€”no footfall, no warning, only presence.

Without urgency or fear, she began to **collect the bodies** of the fallen with a unerring professionalism. She looked to Erelmor, calm and businesslike: â€œSixty gold, for the effort.â€

He snatched the gold like a dragon claiming tribute. â€œSee you again soon,â€ she said, with a knowing smirk. Then she was **gone**, vanishing into nothing as if the trees had swallowed her whole.

**nâ€™Dong and Enrak stood frozen**, mouths open, the hairs on their arms prickling. It was **not right**. Something **deeply wrong** had just happened. When the group reconvened, **Ieuan listened grimly** as nâ€™Dong and Enrak relayed what they'd seen. Whatever **magic** or **power** had just traded with Erelmorâ€”it wasnâ€™t natural. It wasnâ€™t safe.

They turned their attention to the **banditâ€™s gold**, hoping to salvage something usefulâ€”only to find it **tainted**, laced with **arcane markers**. Tracking magic. Cursed money. Erelmor, still holding his bounty, curled his fingers tighter. They warned him. Pleaded. He *accused nâ€™Dong* of hoarding goldâ€”**the irony lost entirely** on him.

Eventually peer pressure swayed the decision, Erelmor threw 50 gold into the bandits pile. **Ieuan**, ever patient, watched him like a schoolmaster catching a student cheat. He didnâ€™t need wildshape to see the greed. He simply folded his arms and waited.

Eventually, **begrudgingly**, **Erelmor gave up the last of the cursed coin**, flinging it away with the petulance of a child forced to return stolen sweets.

## Fighting against the current

With the cursed coins discarded and the woods behind them still humming with uneasy silence, the party resumed their journey back to Somnolent. The strange hooded womanâ€”who had so casually collected corpses and vanished into mistâ€”remained a dark question on everyoneâ€™s mind. There were no answers to be found in the trees, only more shadows. But the path ahead, unfortunately, held familiar dangers.

As they neared the river crossing once more, the haunting sound of rushing water stirred an involuntary shudder in Ieuan, his last encounter with the waterfall still fresh in memory. Ballar, less sentimental and more impatient, bristled at the delay and announced his intention to go firstâ€”commanding the goblin prisoner to follow once he was across. With the force and flair of a seasoned athlete, Ballar gripped the rope swing and sailed through the air, landing with practiced ease on the far bank. What he didnâ€™t expect was the welcoming committee.

From the underbrush emerged two robed figuresâ€”**priests of Elgira**. Ballar had landed in their trap, one cast *Zone of Truth*, the other *Hold Person*. Ballar resisted the first with sheer force of will, but the paralysis took hold. Locked in place, unable to speak or move, he listened as they coldly demanded to know the whereabouts of the **stolen letter from Prussetâ€™s study**. Ballar held his nerve, managing to obfuscate that the note was in his bag. The priests gave their judgment in the form of crueltyâ€”**kicking Ballar into the river** like a sack of meat. He crashed down the waterfall, armor and limbs colliding with rock and current, vanishing from view.

From the far bank, the party watched the scene unfold and erupted into action. Firebolts, acid and conjured daggers flew across the river in a hailstorm of retaliation. The battle quickly turned savage. **Radiant flames** licked through the trees as the priests retaliated, catching **Erelmor off guard** and dropping him to the ground, unconscious. In the chaos, the goblin prisonerâ€”finally sensing true freedomâ€”made its escape, darting into the undergrowth and out of their grasp for good.

Dragging himself from the river with water clinging to his skin and fury in his veins, **Ballar broke the paralysis through sheer rage**, staggered upright, and locked eyes with the cliff above. With a roar, he **charged up the 30-foot incline**, scaling it with maddened determination and unleashing his gem of brightness in a wave of light that lit the enemy up like a beacon. The rest of the gang followed his lead, their anger boiling over. The last of Elgiraâ€™s enforcers fell shortly afterâ€”**crushed beneath steel and spell**.

As the dust settled, **Ieuan rushed to Erelmorâ€™s side**, stabilizing him with practiced hands and a rare moment of tenderness. The party, battered and breathless, took a moment to breathe. Across the river, the rope swing still hung like a dare.

With Ballar alone on the far bank, Ieuan took it upon himself to rally the team, offering **Guidance and encouragement** in equal measure as each member made their crossing. The moment of calm was almost pleasantâ€”until **Terrick**, who had contributed little during the battle beyond missed shots and nervous mumbling, **somersaulted elegantly across the ravine** with a flourish. The entire party stared, somewhere between disbelief and disgust. It took all of Ieuanâ€™s inner strength, but he followed nextâ€”**conquering his fears** and landing safely at last.

Bruised, soaked, exhausted, and still haunted by too many unanswered questions, the group finally saw the flickering lanterns of **Somnolent** on the horizon. The town had not yet stirredâ€”still cradled in the final hours of night. Wordlessly, they agreed to rest. The sun would rise soon, and with it, **new threats, new truths, and perhaps darker tides still to come**.

# Session 06: Smoke, Song and Secrets

> [!abstract] Session summary
> Back in Somnolent, each adventurer follows a separate lead involving the Pike, Ardynia and Broken Fang contraband.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 6 |
| **Recorded** | 23 October 2025 at 20:15 |
| **Arc** | Somnolent investigations |

## 4AM â€“ The Walk of Shame

The Squishy Boys stumbled back into [[Somnolent]] under the cloak of predawn silence â€” bloodied, burned, and barely upright. Their return did not go unnoticed. Deputy Gayle Bramble was waiting. With arms crossed and eyes sharp, she dressed down Terrick for his impromptu adventure, making it clear that his wife, Rowena, had been worried sick. The ranger sheepishly scurried home to whatever frosty reception awaited him.

Gayle passed on one final message: Romayne Drayton expected a full report that evening.

The group scattered, agreeing on a 1PM rendezvous at the Drowsy Coin. Ballar suggested rest.

Ballar, of course, ignored his own advice.

## 4:30AM â€“ Ballar Gets a Step Ahead

Taking a moment to make sure he was perfumed to perfection, Ballar made his way to the [[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] Embassy. Inside the cold marble halls, he was met by Stacii, Ambassador Ouin Brond's ever-prickly assistant. After some theatrical insistence, she begrudgingly let him through.

Ouin was pensive, glass of wine in hand, uninterested at first â€” until Ballar began talking. He revealed everything: the Bromere coin, the [[Rootbound]], Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s assassination, and more. Ouin dismissed any direct Bromere involvement in the abduction attempt, but pointed Ballar toward a potential link â€” Ervain â€œThe Pike,â€ a former Bromere commander frequenting a dice game at the One Ball Inn, run by Alaaric Hammerfall.

Ouin encouraged caution, asking Ballar to keep his findings quiet until they could be exploited. She reminded him that secrets were a currency of his family and challenged him to keep his unpredictable companions in check. Ballar noddedâ€¦ somewhat evasively.

Before leaving, Ballar leveraged Ouin's goodwill to press for more on the Broken Fang, the mercenary group nâ€™Dong had mentioned. She was only too eager to point them out on the map, confirming they had disrupted Doro trade routes and promising to provide more intelligence.

Mission complete, Ballar headed to the gym to get a pump on, followed by a well-earned trance in the sauna.

## 8AM â€“ Stars, Smoke and Ashes

Elsewhere, Erelmor tranced in the quiet solitude of Dustmere Grounds, but found no peace. A song crept into his thoughts, unbidden and sinister â€” a melody from the Maw, tied to the darkest day of his life. His trance spiralled into a vivid memory: smoke, ash, his home in ruin.

The song was real.

He awoke to hear it drifting from a drunken trader. Without hesitation, Erelmor tackled the man into the bushes, blade to throat. Desperate, panicked, the man revealed he was a former House Daskel soldier. Heâ€™d heard the tune earlier that morningâ€”someone had sung it at a dice game in the One Ball Inn to taunt a man named Ervain.

Almost sated, Erelmor rifled through the manâ€™s belongings, claiming a handful of coins and a dram of whiskey, warning him never to sing of The Pike again.

Consumed by rage, Erelmor charged to the One Ball Inn. Finding it closed, he fought the urge to break in, instead wandering toward the arcade, simmering, waiting.

Meanwhile, Ieuan awoke in quiet contemplation, only to find Father Zantos waiting. The two spoke at length. Zantos praised Ieuanâ€™s choice to walk the Circle of Stars, linking it to Orun the Verdant Kin. Their conversation turned to the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynians]], with Zantos expressing worry at how recklessly the party had disturbed the ruins. He shared his upbringing in [[Clan Erzhin]], where ancient truths about the Ardynians were kept in the Annals of Stoneâ€”knowledge forgotten elsewhere in [[Rakara]].

Talk shifted to the Rootbound. Though both men were guarded, Ieuan learned that Zantos once called himself a member, before withdrawing when the group turned too rebellious, even murderous. Zantos was heartbroken to hear they might now be aligned with Elgiraâ€™s Church and [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]].

As they parted, Zantos offered a gift: a Star Seed, promising it would bloom once Ieuan fully embraced his druidic path.

## 10AM â€“ Making a Mark

Refreshed, dressed in finery and fully attuned to his Ring of Mind Shielding, Ballar tried to claim reimbursement from Stacii, but was met with the usual Doro bureaucracy. Undeterred, he marched to the Silent Court and was finally shown to Alaaricâ€™s office, where the treasurer unlocked a complicated safe and handed over the Anthea Bloodclot death report.

Not done yet, Ballar leaned into his next objectiveâ€”infiltrating the dice game. Playing the role of curious newcomer, he managed to catch Alaaricâ€™s interest. The price of admission: 250 gold, plus 25 gold per companion watching. Ballar, ever the opportunist, now had one foot firmly inside Somnolentâ€™s underbelly.

## 12PM â€“ Alcohol-Fueled Escapades

nâ€™Dong woke in the woods, sun dappling through the trees. After a failed attempt at river fishing, he heard a racket from upriver. A man floated by on a makeshift barrel-raft, clearly up to no good.

nâ€™Dong sprinted ahead, reaching the bridge just as the raft passed beneath. Without hesitation, he leaptâ€”crashing onto the raft with a thud that nearly capsized it. A quick Suggestion spell convinced the smuggler to dock the raft and turn over the goods, but as they reached shore, nâ€™Dong slipped, face-planting in the shallows.

The man tried to flee, only to be snagged by nâ€™Dongâ€™s clawed grip. During the interrogation, the man admitted he was just the delivery boy. Heâ€™d make the dropâ€¦ and someone else would take the loot. That was all nâ€™Dong needed.

Volunteering himself as cargo, nâ€™Dong climbed into one of the whiskey barrels. The journey was long, wet, and deeply uncomfortableâ€”but he stayed the course.

Meanwhile, Enrak woke in a Drowsy Coin filled with drunken song. At the centre was Ersil Loquella, bard and rascal, leading a rousing tavern anthem about Enrak the Brazen Flame. Delighted, Enrak cast Firebolt into the hearth â€” accidentally scorching the bricks â€” and bought another round.

Between drinks, Ersil pressed for stories. Enrak spoke warmly of his companions: Ballar the Bold, nâ€™Dong the Poisonous, Erelmor the Gold-Seeker, and Ieuan the Mystical. Though he rejected the notion of being the group's leader, he did share his ambition â€” to rise in [[Clan Khaali]], and uncover Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s killer.

Ersil played him a different tune: *The Ballad of Anthea Bloodclot* a mocking, bitter piece that unsettled Enrak. When questioned, Ersil admitted he and Anthea were childhood friends, though neither were born in Khaali. His nostalgia was laced with mystery.

Xene Redmayne soon arrived, ensuring their cups stayed full, and conversation gave way to laughter.

## 1PM â€“ Secrets Unfold

The barrel-raft bumped against the dock. Inside, nâ€™Dong was bruised, soaked, and furious. His thief had vanished the moment they landed.

Trapped, nâ€™Dong spent fifteen agonizing minutes trying to break free from within until the barrel finally tipped and he clambered out. Alone among the stolen goods, he cracked open one of the barrels and found a note: â€œHalf the load. Midnight. One Ball Innâ€ signed with a Broken Fang insignia.

A grin crept across nâ€™Dongâ€™s wet face.

He had his lead.

# Session 07: Coffee and Contraband

> [!abstract] Session summary
> Elmwood reveals his interest in Tace Nox, nâ€™Dong uncovers a smuggling network and a cursed mask forces personal reckonings.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 7 |
| **Recorded** | 11 November 2025 at 22:36 |
| **Arc** | Somnolent investigations |

## 9AM â€“ Patience Wears Thin (Erelmor)

With the dawn barely settled and the rest of [[Somnolent]] still nursing hangovers or hang-ups, Erelmor set off toward the arcades, singularly focused on acquiring something a little more practical than the battered leathers heâ€™d been wearing since Thistlewound. He found his mark in Zephir Tomes, a gnomish leatherworker whose shop had only just opened its shutters for the day.

What began as a civil conversationâ€”helm versus bycoket, flexibility versus coverage â€” quickly became an increasingly bizarre negotiation, one where Erelmor seemed less interested in the armour's craftsmanship and more in its delivery time. Eventually, with a huff and 45 gold lighter, Erelmor secured a custom set of studded leather armour, to be completed in two to three days.

Business concluded, most would have left. Not Erelmor.

He simply turned, found a bench across from the shop, and began to stare. Quietly. Unblinking. Waiting.

It didnâ€™t take long for Zephirâ€™s comfort to wear thin. The gnome came out himself to request â€” politely but firmly â€” that Erelmor go stare somewhere else. With his social grace laid bare like a gutted rabbit, the rogue finally rose and wandered off to find Ballar, sulking, perhaps, or simply confused that time didnâ€™t pass faster just because he wanted it to.

## 10AM â€“ Unveiling the gambit (Ieuan)

For the better part of a day and a half, Ieuan had been doing his best to casually ask every tavern owner, barkeep, and wayward soul whether Elmwood had mentioned him. When that yielded no results, he finally did what heâ€™d clearly wanted to do all along: he went to the Crooked Hat.

Navigating the arcane defenses of the eccentric wizardâ€™s crooked tower â€” first the Seeing Door, then the disorienting, logic-defying staircase â€” Ieuan arrived once more in the presence of Elmwood, the ever-frazzled, ever-focused scholar of the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] past.

Elmwood wasted no time. He launched into his concerns about the growing dangers around Ardynian ruins, the power they held, and how vital it was that the wrong people didnâ€™t get their hands on the scrolls he believed lay buried within. Ieuan listened, an amused smile tugging at the corners of his mouth, realising almost immediately that the ruin Elmwood spoke of, Tace Nox, was one he'd already explored.

And yet, he said nothing.

Elmwood continued. He confessed he had once been a lead researcher at the Pale Seminary in [[The Powers That Be#Minir|Minir]], but had fled, disillusioned by the increasing influence of radical factions like the Broken Fangâ€”people who sought power, not understanding. He had moved to Somnolent in secret, hoping to gain access to the ruins before his rivals could reach them. The notes he'd stolen, the misinformation he'd fed to his colleaguesâ€”it had all been a gambit, a desperate bid to protect something ancient.

What he needed now was a crewâ€”fast, competent, and discreet. He offered the job to Ieuan, unaware heâ€™d already completed part of the mission. Ieuan, to his credit, tried to keep the lie going. He chuckled about assembling a new team, downplayed the importance of his "back-up" crew, and offered vague reassurances. But the moment Elmwood mentioned the scrolls againâ€”their potency, their responsibilityâ€”Ieuanâ€™s mind flashed to Enrak juggling flaming cantrips while shouting about destiny. The druidâ€™s mask cracked. He flinched. And Elmwood saw through it.

Silence followed.

The old manâ€™s demeanour shiftedâ€”from kindly to cold. The pain of betrayal was evident. He demanded an explanation. He deserved one. Ieuan backpedalled. He admitted theyâ€™d explored Tace Nox, that he knew where the scroll was, and that he hadnâ€™t acted alone. This was now his primary party, he insisted. He would return with the scrollâ€”and with them.

Elmwood didnâ€™t reply immediately. He merely nodded, disappointed but resolved.

The stakes had changed.

## 11AM â€“ Brewed and branded (Ballar)

After a morning of quiet scheming, Ballar set out with a singular mission: *coffee.* His destination: Brewed Awakening, the trendiest caffeine cart in Somnolent, manned by the unflappable and perennially unimpressed Saffron.

An argument over cortados somehow turned into a philosophical debate on loyalty programs, which culminated in Ballar begrudgingly offering his actual flesh for a loyalty sigil. One â€œ[[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] Diplomat,â€ one â€œAshwake,â€ and two of Saffronâ€™s buns later, the tab was tallied â€” but just as Ballar reached the end of his patience, Erelmor appeared. Buzzing with new-coffee energy, the rogue ordered another drink, and Ieuan zoomed past requesting one to-go. Ballar picked up the tab for both, and earned a few more loyalty brands.

Caffeinated and clinging to sanity, the pair made their way to Balur Moonclaw, the tiefling blacksmith. Ballar, in a moment of self-indulgent bravado, snapped a chain in half with his bare handsâ€”one Balur claimed had previously restrained an owlbear. With raised eyebrows, the smith presented a thicker one. This one held, and Ballar happily purchased it along with a pair of manacles.

Their testosterone display turned theatrical as Balur mentioned the fighting pits again, challenging Ballar to a future duel. The two squared off like a low-budget promotional poster â€” bulging arms, scowls, mutual respect hanging in the air like sweat.

Still flush with coffee and bravado, Ballar dragged the giddy Erelmor (now vibrating from his first caffeine experience) to the Doro Embassy, where he promptly demanded funds from Stacii to infiltrate Alaaricâ€™s dice game.

Stacii, ever the administrative predator, slid over a contract dripping in fine print. â€œWe donâ€™t just hand out 250 gold,â€ she said sweetly. Ballar signed without reading, then whisked Erelmor off for a celebratory tour of the embassyâ€™s gym for some flexing and a sauna.

## 1PM â€“ The barrel and the bricks (nâ€™Dong)

Still soggy and shaken from his ill-advised whiskey barrel ride, nâ€™Dong emerged at the docks trying to piece together where exactly heâ€™d landed. The shack nearby looked decrepit at first glance, but his sharp eyes caught the glint of new hinges and recently turned soil. A recently disturbed barrel concealed a set of keys, which opened the shack to reveal something far more sinister.

Inside, he found crates of suppliesâ€¦ and clingfilm-wrapped bricks of something suspicious. Driven by curiosity (and a touch of madness), nâ€™Dong dipped a finger into one and rubbed it on his gums.

A mistake. A catastrophic, nerve-shredding, gut-twisting mistake.

The pain knocked him to the floor â€” convulsing, screaming, his brain reduced to a static of agony. Ten minutes passed before he staggered upright, pale and drenched in sweat.

Somewhere in the haze, he found a manifest of drop-offs, linking multiple locations:

- *One Ball Inn (Somnolent) - 4 x Barrel whiskey,*
- *The Scythian tree (Orvak) - 2 x Rations, 4 x weapons/armour*
- *Fighting pits (Voruun) - Agony, Powdered Power, Dragon's Dandruff*
- *Ashka's den (Khaali) - Unicorn wax, Ember Berries*

Before he could process it, he heard hoofbeats. Acting on pure instinct, nâ€™Dong submerged himself like a swamp creature, hiding beneath the dock.

A halfling merchant approached. Curious, unbothered, and only mildly concerned after having a rock hurled at his horse, he soon met nâ€™Dong â€” who revealed himself like a half-drowned monster from a folk tale. The halfling introduced himself as Theo Dodds, a travelling trader

Theo was heading to Somnolent, and after some hesitation (and grabbing a barrel of whiskey), nâ€™Dong decided to join him. Perhaps the best way forward was to follow the flow of illicit goodsâ€¦ back to where it all began.

## 2PM â€“ Secrets and schemes

By mid-afternoon, the Squishy Boys reconvened at the Drowsy Coin, one lizard short of a full party. Enrak was already deep in his cups, holding court like a one-man carnival while Ieuan paced behind him, eyes wide with worry, quietly praying he hadn't said too much â€” or worse, said something true.

Ballar, all poise and presence, scanned the tavern once and decided this was no place for secrets. Calling over the ever-jovial Callum Redmayne, he demanded privacy. Though Callum noted Enrak already had a room, Ballar waved him off with an upturned palm and ordered the finest room available, complete with drinks â€” vermouths and cortados, naturally. As they made their way upstairs, he left one final instruction: if a lizard man showed up, send him up immediately.

In their private chamber, the chaos of the tavern melted away â€” replaced by a tangled conversation of half-truths, tension, and too many threads to pull cleanly.

Ieuan, working subtly, managed to retrieve the Ardynian scroll from a very drunk Enrak, stashing it safely before the others noticed. He pressed the group to focus on the ruins, on Elmwood, on the mysteries buried beneath Vaelond that could change their world.

Ballar, however, had more tangible ambitions. Dice games and heists. He pitched a plan to raid Alaaric Hammerfallâ€™s safe while distracting him at the One Ball Inn gambling tables. But his motivations were layered â€” carefully concealed beneath bravado. He held his cards tight, manipulating the conversation while deflecting attention from his own inquiries.

The mention of the One Ball Inn stirred Erelmor, who revealed he was tracking a man, the Old Grey Pike, and suspected he might be tied to this shadowy gambling ring.

At last, nâ€™Dong appeared, drenched from his barrel-bound escapade but unbothered. He presented a list found at the drop siteâ€”various contraband and substances, most identifiable save one: Agony, which they realized nâ€™Dong himself had imbibed during his infiltration.

As plans and theories flew across the room, Callum Redmayne poked his head in with perfect timing â€” and a classic side quest. There was something strange in the basement, and heâ€™d appreciate the group looking into it.

Resigned to distraction, the group agreed â€” if only to clear their heads.

## 4pm â€” The Mask in the Dark

In the musty cellar of the Drowsy Coin, the partyâ€™s search began with a distinct lack of subtlety. Crates were kicked, dust was disturbed, and spirits were stirred.

Ballar, perhaps trying to move things along, landed a firm kick to a pile of cratesâ€”and his foot struck something unnatural. A mask, old and brittle, slid free from the wreckage.

And then the world shifted.

Each of them was suddenly, terrifyingly alone â€” but not in body. In memory.

- nâ€™Dong saw Prodosius, grinning with mirthless spite, boasting of slaughtering his parents and warning his death would soon follow.
- Ieuan watched a wood elf poisoner, whispering of betrayal and treachery, crowing, *â€œYou always knew it was me.â€*
- Enrak stood before Ashka, goddess of fire and chaos, who gazed into him and asked simply: *â€œWho are you?â€*
- Ballar was confronted by his mother, eyes burning with disdain as she spat, *â€œYou are no son of mine.â€*
- Erelmor saw his wife, dying and broken on the floor, her blood pooling around her as she looked up and begged: *â€œWhy didn't you save us?â€*

The illusions fractured. A wave pulsed through the basement, knocking the party back into themselves â€” only two figures remained: Ballarâ€™s mother, and Erelmorâ€™s wife, now apparitions of fury and grief.

The fight was brutal â€” not in blows traded, but in what it cost. The spectres tore into their minds and bodies. Ballar, whose strength had long been a pillar of pride, felt his muscles wither in real time, sapped by the very image of his motherâ€™s disappointment. And still, they fought â€” cutting down the ghosts with spells and steel, until at last, the echoes were silenced.

What remained was the mask â€” still and silent.

As the group caught their breath, nâ€™Dong approached the mask with rare solemnity. Without prompting, without thought, he recited a forgotten prayer â€” a supplication to Virei, the Shattered Mask, whose broken visage watches over secrets and spirits.

The curse lifted. The mask fell inert. Without ceremony, nâ€™Dong pocketed it.

No one questioned it.

The group returned upstairs, shaken. Ballar bolted to his room, hollowed out by the vision of his motherâ€™s scorn, strength draining from his frame. Erelmor was wild-eyed, haunted by the bloodied plea of the woman he could not forget. The others followed, each retreating to silence in their own way.

Whatever had happened in the basement wasnâ€™t just a haunting. It was a reckoning. And none of them had walked away unscathed.

# Session 08: The Weight of Secrets

> [!abstract] Session summary
> Romayne forces the truth into the open as the party pursues Ambassador Prusset through Somnolent.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 8 |
| **Recorded** | 01 December 2025 at 20:36 |
| **Arc** | The Prusset affair |

The air was heavy in the Drowsy Coin. The encounter in the cellar still clung to the party like soot. As the day wore on toward evening, each member tried in their own way to pretend things were normal. But normal had long since left [[Somnolent]].

Upstairs, Ballar was alone, still reeling. With slow, deliberate motions he slid into his armour, each buckle and strap a ritual, a shield against the voice of his mother still echoing in his skull. As he adjusted the last piece, something strange happened â€” the ring on his finger whispered to him.

It wasnâ€™t a voice in his head. It wasnâ€™t comfort. It was something darker. Ballar tried to speak back. No reply. He removed the ring. Waited. Put it back on.

Still silence.

His eyes narrowed. Was it cursed? Was it watching?

He said nothing to the others.

## The Summons

Downstairs, Callum Redmayne approached the rest of the group â€” Ieuan, nâ€™Dong, Erelmor, and Enrak (who, wisely or not, was left to his drink). A messenger had come from Romayne Drayton, requesting their presence immediately.

When they went to collect Ballar, they were met only with a sharp rebuke through the door. â€œIâ€™m not coming. Donâ€™t tell Romayne anything.â€

A decision was made: Enrak would stay at the Drowsy Coin, where he could do the least damage. The rest would go, minus Ballar, with Erelmor deciding to wait outside Draytonâ€™s office, wary of saying something unwise.

Inside Draytonâ€™s study, the tone was different than before. The usual calm of Somnolentâ€™s headman had turned quietly sharp, like a blade beneath silk. He motioned for Ieuan and nâ€™Dong to sit.

They chose their words carefully â€” but not carefully enough. They claimed ignorance. The goblin attack, they said, was random, opportunistic. Nothing more. Romayne didnâ€™t buy it for a second.

He reminded them of the note, the one the party had found on the goblin commander that read: No kill, just scare. Get Pruzet. Crow want alive. Buggie.

Their bluff shattered. Calm but firm, Romayne Drayton demanded the truth. All of it. No more lies, no more delays.

nâ€™Dong, with an uncharacteristic show of openness, handed over the silver coin. Ieuan followed, revealing what they had found: proof that Alistair Prusset was involved in the murder of Anthea Bloodclot. Drayton turned the coin over in his hands. His expression darkened.

â€œThisâ€¦ is a Bromere favour coin,â€ he said quietly. â€œIf this is what it looks likeâ€¦ it implicates [[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] in the attempted abduction of a [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] ambassador.â€ The room went still. Romayne demanded the proof of Perfid and Prusset's involvement in Anthea Bloodclot's murder.

With Romayneâ€™s words still echoing in their heads, Ieuan and nâ€™Dong left Draytonâ€™s office in a hurry â€” tails tucked, truth reluctantly spilled. Outside, Erelmor fell into step without question, sensing the urgency but not yet knowing the reason. There was no time for explanation. They needed one thing: Ballar.

Back at the Drowsy Coin, Ballar had emerged from his self-imposed exile. Recovered, at least in posture, he greeted Ieuan with surprising levity. They spoke like old friends discussing a pleasant evening ahead, not like conspirators standing on a political powder keg. Plans for the night took shape quickly: Ballar would attend the dice game at the One Ball Inn, while Erelmor and Enrak â€” the proverbial loose cannons â€” would attempt to infiltrate Alaaricâ€™s office and crack the mysterious safe.

With the air seemingly clear, Ieuan pivoted casually, asking for the documents Ballar had. The paladin handed them over with minimal fuss, trusting that someone else might better make sense of them. With the proof Romayne needed now in hand, Ieuan and nâ€™Dong slipped out once more. Neither of them noticed Erelmor, quietly slipping into the shadows behind them.

As they reached the street, Erelmor watched in surprise as the two split ways â€” Ieuan heading back toward Draytonâ€™s office, and nâ€™Dong turning toward the One Ball Inn. Suspicion flared. He tailed nâ€™Dong, unwilling to risk the dragonborn stumbling into The Old Grey Pike unprepared.

The One Ball Inn was a different beast in daylight. Less den of intrigue, more forgotten corner of Somnolentâ€™s underbelly. Inside, the bar was nearly empty. Only the town drunk, Billy Hill, and the innâ€™s landlord, Antoinne Cassan, kept the place breathing. nâ€™Dong, casual and composed, ordered a drink and pressed Antoinne for information about the dice game. But Antoinne, slick as oil and twice as hard to catch, offered little but cold stone and empty words.

As nâ€™Dong turned to leave, Billy Hill wobbled in his seat and beckoned. Slurring through his beard, he offered information for beer. nâ€™Dong obliged whilst, outside, Erelmor crouched beneath the window and listened intently. Billy, now thoroughly rehydrated with ale, managed to string together a few slurred truths:

The dice game runs two to three times a week. The game of choice is Pig â€” simple, risky, and perfect for gambling favours. Once, heâ€™d caught Antoinne doing something shady. He couldnâ€™t quite recall what, only that it was the kind of thing a man doesnâ€™t talk about unless he wants to end up in the river. It wasnâ€™t much â€” but it was enough. nâ€™Dong left without fanfare. Erelmor melted into the street behind him, his thoughts now knotted with more than just The Pike.

## Ballads, balconies and bad decisions

Back at the Drowsy Coin, tensions flared not from threats or omens â€” but from song. Ballar Novis sat at a table, drink untouched, his jaw tight and his knuckles white. Across the room, Ersil Loquella, the blonde bard with a silver tongue and dangerous charm, once again raised his voice to the delight of the patrons. This time, however, he named names. Ballarâ€™s name.

The paladinâ€™s glare could have split stone. As soon as the performance ended, Ballar approached Ersil with all the warmth of a drawn blade, inviting him to his room for a private drink. Ever the entertainer, Ersil accepted with a gracious bow and a suspiciously curious smile.

The moment the door closed, the act dropped. Ballar lunged, seizing the bard by the throat and dangling him over the second-storey balcony, eyes wild with fury. â€œHow do you know my name?â€ he growled.

Ersil, remarkably composed despite the precarious situation, gave a simple answer: â€œEnrak told me.â€

That didnâ€™t help. Rage boiling over, Ballar threatened to drop him, demanding he never sing about him again. But Ersil only smiled faintly and misty stepped from Ballarâ€™s grip â€” reappearing inside the room, untouched. The tension snapped. Ballar swung, landing a solid hit across Ersilâ€™s face.

A mistake. With a wry grin, the bard whispered an incantation and cast Tashaâ€™s Hideous Laughter. In moments, the iron-clad paladin was on the floor, shaking, giggling, convulsing, a wretched chorus of laughter exploding from his lungs against his will. Ersil left him there, humming to himself. Downstairs, Ballar could hear it start again: â€œEnrak! Enrak! The brazen flame!â€

Ballar didnâ€™t wait for the encore. Once the spell wore off, he stormed out, red-faced and seething, heading straight for the Doro consulate with the fury of a man thoroughly humiliated by rhyme.

Meanwhile, blissfully unaware of the lyrical assassination and subsequent balcony assault, Enrak was enjoying his growing celebrity, soaking in the praise like a dry sponge. His moment of peace was broken, however, by the arrival of a tired and frantic woman. She introduced herself as Rowena â€” wife to Terrick, the ranger who had accompanied the party to Thistlewound.

â€œHe left four hours ago to meet you,â€ she said, barely holding herself together. â€œHe said he was meeting you before going to see Romayne. I havenâ€™t seen him since.â€ Enrak blinked.

â€œTerrick?â€ he asked, confused. â€œNever heard of him.â€

Rowena stared. She explained againâ€”patiently at first, then with rising frustration. Enrak remained utterly nonplussed, offering the most unhelpful reassurance imaginable. â€œIâ€™m sure itâ€™s fine. Donâ€™t worry.â€

Rowena slapped him. Hard. Then she left, furious and heartbroken. Enrak stood there a moment longer, processing nothing, then returned to his drink. Probably still humming his own theme song.

## The Ambassadorâ€™s Escape

While chaos played out everywhere else â€” bards thrown from balconies, wives begging for lost husbands, and plying drunks with booze for info â€” Ieuan stayed the course.

With the Perfidian documents in hand, he made his way alone to Romayne Drayton, the only one still treating the threat of civil war with the gravity it deserved. Behind closed doors, Ieuan laid bare the truth: proof of Perfidâ€™s involvement in Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s assassination. Romayneâ€™s expression barely shifted, but the lines of tension deepened on his face.

â€œPerfid is pulling Prusset from Somnolent. They claim itâ€™s due to a lack of protection. But now? We know itâ€™s a coverâ€”theyâ€™re hiding their involvement.â€ He leaned forward, tone sharpening.

â€œStop him from leaving. Do not kill him. But if he escapes â€” you and your party will be held responsible for obstructing justice.â€ The weight of that sentence settled hard on Ieuanâ€™s shoulders.

Ieuan wasted no time. He rushed back to the Drowsy Coin, gathered everyone he could find â€” Enrak, nâ€™Dong, Erelmor â€” and made for the Doro Consulate to retrieve Ballar. They stopped once. For cortado-related bickering at Brewed Awakening. It was brief. Barely 10 minutes. It'd be fine.

Inside the consulate, they found Ballar â€” fresh from a sauna, glowing with calm that vanished the moment the group started loudly declaring their intent to arrest the Perfid ambassador. Stacii, Ballarâ€™s eternally unimpressed colleague, raised a brow. Ballar nearly screamed. Slamming on his armor with record speed, he marched them out before they could say anything worse in front of more witnesses.

No sooner had they hit the street than they spotted Alistair Prusset, slipping through the townâ€™s outer edges. The chase began immediately, both sides caught off guard. Prusset, desperate, turned and cast Slow, catching half the group. But Ieuan, unfazed, wild shaped into an elk, letting Ballar leap onto his back before charging after the ambassador like a battering ram.

Just as they closed the distance, Prusset blinkedâ€”shifting into the Ethereal Plane, disappearing from view. But the party had a wild card: Erelmor, and his Hagâ€™s Eye amulet. He locked on to the flickering spirit of Prusset beyond the veil and shouted his location.

The pursuit twisted through back alleys, shifting planes, and cramped houses. Prusset popped in and out of existence, his Spirit Guardian tearing into anyone who got too close â€” dropping nâ€™Dong and battering Ballar into bloodied fury.

Ballar, through grit and desperation, revived nâ€™Dong and lunged to grab Prusset one last time. And then, from behind, a crack of force. Ieuan, in a panic, cast Thunderwave, ostensibly to help.

The result? Ballar was blasted backward, smashed into a wooden fence, the wind knocked clean from his lungs. His grip on Prusset gone. The ambassador slipped free once more.

Whether out of fear, instinct, or error the â€” blow might have cost them everything.

And as Prusset vanished into the Ethereal again, flickering like a dying flame, the party stood scattered, injured, and breathless â€” the stakes now higher than ever, and the consequences closing in fast.

# Session 09: The Capture of Prusset

> [!abstract] Session summary
> After a desperate running battle, the party captures Prusset but fractures over who should receive him.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 9 |
| **Recorded** | 23 December 2025 at 22:18 |
| **Arc** | The Prusset affair |

The hunt for Ambassador Alistair Prusset resumed in a blur of sweat, arcane flashes, and increasingly unhinged improvisation. The party â€” bruised, burned, and borderline deliriousâ€”picked up their pursuit with desperate energy, knowing every second counted.

A trap was set: ball bearings rolled across the alley, grease spells slicked the flagstones, and even a shattered vat of vegetable stock was spread to turn the terrain into a soupy mess. It was crude, it was chaotic, and it was almost beautiful.

But Prusset blinked through it, vanishing from their perfect trap with maddening grace. Again and again, he appeared where he shouldnâ€™t be, darting between houses, alleyways, and outcroppings like smoke through fingers. The Defracturers grew more desperate, hounding him at every turn, bounding across the rooftops as they tried to keep sight of their quarry.

Amid the chase, Ballar felt a foreign presence try to touch his mind â€” a whisper of telepathic contact â€” but he rejected it instantly, using his ring of mind shielding as he refused to allow anything to break his focus. Then, the tide turned.

Just as Prussetâ€™s arcane reserve ran dry, his reinforcements arrived â€” two robed priests of Elgira, casting radiant fire and divine protections. The battle became a blur: Erelmor, Ieuan, nâ€™Dong, and Enrak all fell at different points, strewn across [[Somnolent]]â€™s quiet backstreets like broken chess pieces.

But slowly, the party rallied. They concentrated fire, burned spells, swung wildly. Ballar, bloodied and raging, knocked Prusset unconscious â€” only for the priests to bring him back again and again.

At one absurd point in the melee, as fire and death rained around them, nâ€™Dong paused mid-combat to offer to repair Ballarâ€™s manacles. â€œNow is not the time,â€ someone probably screamed.

Eventually, the priests fell, and Prusset, gasping, broken and bloodied, was finally subdued and shackled â€” hogtied like a prize boar.

Victory brought no peace. Ballar, always thinking five moves ahead (and ten degrees to the left), suggested they take Prusset to the [[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] embassy first, arguing it was only right that Doro, not Somnolent, handle the matter.

But the rest of the party â€” led by Ieuanâ€™s sense of duty and nâ€™Dongâ€™s keen paranoia â€” refused. Their loyalty was with Romayne Drayton and Somnolentâ€™s fragile peace. The split was final. Furious and humiliated, Ballar stormed away from the group, his face a silent scream.

Back at the embassy, Stacii was waitingâ€”livid. She revealed the truth: the voice in Ballarâ€™s mind had been Ambassador Ouin, urgently telling him to stay with the party and deliver Prusset to Drayton.

â€œYou should not have left them,â€ she snapped. â€œDidn't you get Ouin's message?!â€ Ballar didnâ€™t respond. He just turned and walked away.

Back with the rest of the group, a new plan formed. Ieuan would locate Romayne, while the others would drag the unconscious ambassador to The Shackles, the makeshift Somnolent jail. Ieuan, tired and hopeful, made his way toward Draytonâ€™s estateâ€”only to run straight into Ballar, seething and silent. Before words could be properly exchanged, Ballar did what he had clearly been building toward for hours: He punched Ieuan in the face.

The paladin marched off toward the river, trying to find a piece of peace in a world that no longer made sense.

Meanwhile, Erelmor, Enrak, and nâ€™Dong arrived at The Shackles. Deputy Gayle Bramble, still yawning through a dawn shift, was not prepared to see three bloodied adventurers and a gagged, chained ambassador. Her jaw dropped. Her eyes widened. But to her credit, she quickly stepped into duty.

She unlocked a cell, allowed them to store the prisoner inside, and immediately sent a runner to fetch Drayton. The Defracturers, now scattered and wounded, had done it: Prusset was in chains.

But victory had come at a steep costâ€”fractured friendships, bruised egos, and the looming threat of greater powers moving in the shadows. Somnolent was no longer a sleepy town.

And the Defracturers? They were no longer just adventurers. They were now players in a dangerous game.

# Session 10: Saints, Spies and Snake Eyes

> [!abstract] Session summary
> Prusset names the Church behind Antheaâ€™s murder while the party infiltrates the One Ball Inn gambling circle.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 10 |
| **Recorded** | 04 January 2026 at 22:05 |
| **Arc** | Saints and spies |

With the ambassador in chains and the town still spinning from the chaos of the chase, Ieuan wasted no time. He found Romayne Drayton at the town hall and relayed the news: Prusset had been apprehended. The village headman didnâ€™t hesitate. He swept scrolls from drawers into his bag and marched toward The Shackles with Ieuan at his side.

At the cells, Romayne verified everything with the stunned Deputy Bramble, then unleashed the magic of a Zone of Truth. While only Ieuan and Prusset fell under its sway, it was more than enough. The interrogation began, and with each question, the noose tightened.

Prusset, defeated and cornered, spilled everything:

- The [[Perfidian Faith|Church of Elgira]], acting through its High Illuminant, had ordered Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s assassination.
- The Illuminant believed Anthea was actually Saint Marra, one of Elgiraâ€™s last living followers.
- This belief came not from faith, but from divine communionâ€”the High Illuminant claimed to speak directly to Elgira.
- Mara (Anthea) was suspected of plotting to restore the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] Empire.
- The Church feared her influence, and wanted her silenced.
- Worst of all, the [[Rootbound]] were used to enact the murderâ€¦ though Prusset couldnâ€™t name the specific agent.

The room chilled. None of them had expected to unearth a holy war.

In an unexpected moment of compassion, Ieuan asked about Terrick. Prusset confessed: the ranger had been abducted and taken to the [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] embassy. Romayne immediately dispatched Gayle Bramble to investigate, fury laced through his usually measured expression.

When asked why he had begged for his life so pathetically, Prusset was blunt â€” if Elgiraâ€™s church was willing to kill Anthea in [[Rakara]], what would they do to him after this betrayal? He didnâ€™t fear the adventurers. He feared his own masters.

Romayne, ever the tactician, left him to rot in the cell, and returned to his office to ponder the growing threat of Perfid.

## Riverside Regrets & Rude Messages

Meanwhile, Ballar stood alone by the river, trying to find some shred of calm. He prayedâ€”not to any one god, but to all those he thought might be listening. His meditation was shattered by a message spell from Ouin, to which he responded with deadpan sarcasm:

â€œBallar isnâ€™t here right now. Please leave a message and I will respond as soon as I can.â€

Having vented, he returned to his room at the Drowsy Coin, where he finally spoke at length with the soul in his Ring of Mind Shieldingâ€”a conversation more civil than one might expect from the usually guarded paladin.

Not long after, the rest of the Defracturers arrived. Ieuan led with peace, admitting that he had given everything to Drayton, and had made no copies. Ballarâ€™s fury flared but, perhaps softened by solitude and exhaustion, he chose understanding over conflictâ€”for now.

Their thoughts turned to Ersil the Bard, whose songs had been painting targets on their backs for days. Ballar and Ieuan railed against the exposure, but Enrak, utterly baffled by their paranoia, declared it a boon. And slowly, the others came around. With so many enemies gathering, perhaps a little fame was a shield of its own.

## The Dice Game and the Pike

With the weight of politics briefly lifted, the group turned to their next objective: the gambling den at the One Ball Inn. Erelmor had a vendetta. The Old Grey Pike, he believed, was tied to the destruction of his home and the death of his wife.

Ballar needed information. He suspected the Pike once carried the House Bromere coin that funded Buggieâ€™s mercenaries to kidnap Prusset. nâ€™Dong wanted proof. He believed the Pike and the game were linked to Prodosius and the Broken Fang.

An uneasy pact was struck. Ballar promised Erelmor that, once the Pike had given up what he knew, the rogue could kill him. Satisfiedâ€”for nowâ€”Erelmor agreed to hold his blade.

## Infiltration and Investigation

At the One Ball Inn, the group split:

- Erelmor and nâ€™Dong circled around the back, scoping out the Broken Fang drop site.
- Ballar, Ieuan and Enrak entered the gambling room after a tense greeting from Antoinne Cassan.

Inside, the players were laid out:

- Billy Hill, already drunk.
- Summer Quaresh, the diplomatic liaison for [[Clan Khaali]], joined by Eden Carrion (Voruun) and Jericho Beech (Temir).
- Three rough-looking strangers, one flanked by obvious muscle.

Ballar identified The Old Grey Pike: a hulking half-orc whose scowl could crack stone. He tried to leverage their shared [[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]] heritage, but the Pike spat his contempt, especially toward House Nirrell.

Meanwhile, outside, Erelmor went invisible and nâ€™Dong hid as they watched Antoinne Cassan conduct a clandestine deal in the cellar. Two men reported a bungled drop â€” something about a dragonborn smashing whiskey barrels (nâ€™Dongâ€™s doing, of course). Cassan was livid, lambasting the Broken Fangâ€™s reputation. The dealers slunk off to search the brewery, with Erelmor and nâ€™Dong tailing them into the woods.

## Roll the Bones

Back inside, the game began. Six rounds of Pig. Stakes were high. 300 gold a round. Ballar had to win to avoid crippling debt to Doro.

The results:

- The Pike wins round one.
- The Pike again. Silent. Unshakable.
- Billy Hill, somehow.
- Summer Quaresh, flashing that smug Khaali grin.
- Summer again, tightening her grip on the pot.

Ballar sat on the edge of ruin. Only a final round win could save him.

The dice rattled.

And somehowâ€¦ he won. A narrow, brutal victory.

But just as the sweat dried and the coins were clutched, the Pike smiled, his teeth like broken knives. He pushed his winnings forward and asked, low and cold:

â€œDouble or nothing?â€

# Session 11: Ashes, Brands and Windfalls

> [!abstract] Session summary
> A Broken Fang lead, a failed safe robbery and Romayneâ€™s formal patronage transform the party into agents of Somnolent.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 11 |
| **Recorded** | 11 February 2026 at 21:34 |
| **Arc** | Agents of Somnolent |

## Shadows in the Woods

While fortunes were rising inside the One Ball Inn, something far uglier was unfolding in the dark beyond its walls. Determined not to let another Broken Fang lead slip through his claws, nâ€™Dong pursued the two agents who had been sent to investigate the sabotaged whiskey drop. Erelmor followed close behind, quieter than usual, but just as intent. For once, they showed restraint. They shadowed the pair through the woods, waiting for a name, a location, a careless mention of a superior.

They got no name. What they did get was flame.

From their hidden vantage, they watched as the agents found nâ€™Dongâ€™s crude woodland shelter â€” and without hesitation, set it ablaze. The fire spread quickly. The makeshift home collapsed inward in a shower of sparks and smoke. The restraint ended there.

The two Defracturers burst from the shadows like hunting beasts. Steel and spellwork ended the fight swiftly â€” a short, brutal skirmish beneath crackling firelight. But killing them wasnâ€™t enough. They needed information.

The leader of the pair, a man known as **Sunshine**, broke first. Between blows and blood, he offered a scrap of value: The Broken Fangâ€™s operations in [[Rakara]] were overseen by **Rhul Slowdrift**.

It was the last word he would ever speak. Sunshineâ€™s throat was cut. His companion drowned in a barrel of stolen whiskey â€” forgotten, a grim ending. Then, in a final act of caution â€” or maybe cruelty â€” the bodies were burned. An â€œaccident.â€ Just two careless smugglers and a poorly tended fire. By the time the flames died down, nâ€™Dong had his name, but no home.

## Double or Nothing

Inside the warmth and lamplight of the One Ball Inn, the tension was no less sharp. When The Old Grey Pike leaned forward and growled â€œDouble or nothing?â€ Ballar didnâ€™t hesitate, accepting the challenge with gusto.

What Ballar had envisioned as a private duel of nerve quickly spiralled when Billy Hill and Summer Quaresh inserted themselves into the wager. Pride and greed outweighed etiquette as the stakes swelled. Before the dice rolled, Alaaric Hammerfall quietly pulled Ieuan and Enrak aside. His message was calm, but pointed: â€œYour lizard friend is making dangerous enemies. Antoinne isnâ€™t forgiving. Keep an eye on him.â€

The game began, a sudden-death format with high tension and high stakes. The Pike played cold and clinical â€” but fate turned on him first. He was eliminated early, but not quietly. Chairs scraped. Voices rose. He made sure the entire room heard him threaten Ballar â€” promising to see him at the dueling pits, his flare lingering long after he exited.

Ballar immediately attempted to negotiate â€” offering to split the pot three ways so he could leave and tail the Pike, but Billy and Summer refused, continuing the game. Their greed would be their downfall as the final roll settled, leaving Ballar victorious and 1,200 gold richer.

1,200 gold.

## The Silent Court

While fortunes shifted and tempers flared at the One Ball Inn, nâ€™Dong and Erelmor pressed forward with the second half of the eveningâ€™s plan: infiltrating the Silent Court and uncovering whatever secrets lay within Alaaric Hammerfallâ€™s safe.

The building loomed over them in the dim light, austere and unwelcoming even at rest. After circling the perimeter, they discovered what appeared to be a discreetly concealed entrance â€” not something meant for public use, but clearly known to someone with reason to avoid the front door. With quiet determination, they pried it open. Unfortunately, in a lapse of judgment that would define the rest of their evening, they left the hidden door propped open with a crowbar rather than securing their exit properly.

Inside, confidence began to erode quickly. The Silent Court was larger and more labyrinthine than anticipated, and neither of them actually knew where Alaaricâ€™s safe was located. What began as cautious exploration soon devolved into aimless searching, opening office doors without method, scanning rooms with rising impatience, and trying to appear purposeful while having no real plan. The longer they remained inside, the more fragile their composure became.

That fragility shattered when they heard the metallic scrape behind them. The crowbar fell. The hidden door slammed shut.

The sound carried through the corridor like a verdict.

Panic surged through them both, but nâ€™Dong acted immediately. Without hesitation, he shifted his form, adopting the appearance of Alaaric Hammerfall himself. The transformation was convincing â€” not merely facial, but posture, expression, even the subtle irritation of a bureaucrat inconvenienced by lesser minds. Erelmor, recognizing the risk, vanished into shadow and let the disguise carry them forward.

When guards approached to investigate the disturbance, nâ€™Dong held his nerve. With curt authority and the air of a man annoyed at being questioned, he redirected suspicion and dismissed them. The deception held. The guards withdrew. It was an audacious recovery â€” and it bought them just enough time to press on.

Eventually, after more tense wandering, they located the safe. It was not a simple lockbox hidden behind a painting. It was a deliberate construct of layered defenses: heavy mechanical locks reinforced with runes etched into the metal casing, abjuration magic humming beneath the surface, and something older and less easily categorized woven into its framework. Even without fully understanding it, they could feel the complexity radiating from it.

They studied it carefully at first. They traced the runes, examined the locks, whispered theories about how to bypass the enchantments. The conclusion was clear long before either of them voiced it â€” they were not equipped for this. And yet, neither of them was willing to walk away empty-handed.

When they attempted to force the mechanism, the safe responded immediately. There was no dramatic explosion, no thunderous alarm. Instead, a focused surge of psychic energy erupted outward in a silent, devastating pulse. The sensation was intimate and invasive â€” not merely pain, but violation. Both nâ€™Dong and Erelmor felt something burn into their flesh, a searing heat that carved sigils into their arms as if pressed there by invisible branding irons.

The safe did not open. It marked them. The weight of their mistake settled in quickly. This was not a trap meant to kill. It was meant to identify.

Realizing how exposed they now were, the pair abandoned any further attempt and fled. nâ€™Dong dropped the illusion of Alaaric, reverting to his true form, but immediately concealed himself behind the visage of Vireiâ€™s Shattered Mask, understanding that his distinctive appearance would be remembered.

## Marks, Mandates & War

By the time the full party regrouped at the Drowsy Coin, the night had grown heavy with consequence. Callum Redmayne met them at the door, unusually serious, and informed them that Romayne Drayton required their presence at Town Hall at 9:00am sharp. There was no room for negotiation in his tone. Whatever was coming next would not wait for hangovers.

Once seated and secured, the recounting began.

Ballar, Ieuan, and Enrak listened in stunned silence as nâ€™Dong and Erelmor revealed the chaos of their evening â€” the Broken Fang agents, the name *Rhul Slowdrift*, the burning of bodies to disguise murder, the infiltration of the Silent Court, and worst of all, the magical brands seared into their arms when they tampered with Alaaricâ€™s safe. The room grew quieter with each revelation.

They examined the marks carefully. nâ€™Dong sketched the runes as best he could remember them, crude lines drawn with shaking hands. They compared theories, searched for meaning, tried to determine whether the brand was tracking magic, a curse, or something worse.

They made no progress.

It was Ballar, of all people, who broke the spiral of speculation. â€œLetâ€™s go now. Confess everything to Drayton.â€ It was not a comfortable suggestion â€” but it was the only clear one.

And so, long after decent folk had gone to bed, the Defracturers marched to Town Hall and demanded that Romayne be woken immediately. His attendant resisted at first, but the collective urgency of the group wore him down.

Romayne emerged groggy and visibly strained, already carrying the weight of the coming day. He listened with thin patience as they explained their break-in and the branding.

Through gritted teeth, he informed them that whatever they had done tonight paled in comparison to what awaited [[Somnolent]] at sunrise.

He assured them they were in no immediate danger from the marks on Alaaricâ€™s safe â€” and that if the safe had truly wanted them dead, it would have done so. He implored them to rest and return at the appointed hour. All would be explained in the morning.

Reluctantly, they obeyed.

## Counsel at Dawn

After finally taking a long-overdue rest, Ballar rose early and went to see Ouin Brond. She was not amused.

Her usual sharp sarcasm had softened into something more measured. When Ballar attempted to recount his successes, she brushed past the bravado and spoke plainly instead. Glory, she explained, is often mistaken for purpose. Nations and families demand loyalty, but they rarely offer clarity in return. If he wanted to be more than a weapon in someone elseâ€™s war, he would need to choose his own path.

And when Ballar scoffed at her wisdom, she calmly demonstrated her foresight by having Brewed Awakening deliver a perfectly made cortado â€” exactly as he liked it. It was both humbling and irritating.

## The Announcement

The Defracturers arrived at Town Hall slightly late, to find it already crowded. Inside stood: Alaaric Hammerfall, Deputy Gayle Bramble, Ouin Brond and Petra Murwood, Ambassador of [[The Powers That Be#Minir|Minir]].

Romayne looked older than he had the night before. With a heavy breath, he delivered his decision. [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]]â€™s diplomatic immunity was officially revoked in Somnolent. The justification was clear: their involvement in the murder of Anthea Bloodclot and the abduction of Terrick Lowgins.

The room shifted. This was no longer intrigue. This was escalation. Romayne made it plain: Perfid would respond. And while his limited forces would focus on holding Prusset, he would need the Defracturers to do something far more dangerous. They were to storm the Perfid Embassy and rescue Terrick.

Petra Murwood excused herself almost immediately, offering polite wishes of success while preserving Minirâ€™s plausible deniability. Ouin, however, remained.

She recognized the opportunity for [[The Powers That Be#Doro|Doro]], but also the peril for the Defracturers. She challenged Romayne directly: if he intended to send them into open conflict, he needed to formally back them. It was a situation Romayne was well prepared for as Alaaric produced pre-drafted contracts.

Romayne offered them patronage under Somnolent, granting them protection, resources, and diplomatic immunity for actions taken in the townâ€™s defense. Ballar, however, would have to relinquish his formal status as Doroâ€™s ambassador.

The incentives grew more personal as the discussion continued:

- Enrak was promised the group could use the Perfid Embassy as a base if they succeeded.
- Erelmor and nâ€™Dong were quietly granted pardons for crimes committed in the last twenty-four hours â€” a concession delivered with just enough mischief to acknowledge what had happened.

The weight of the decision settled over them. This was no longer freelance adventuring. One by one, they signed as The Defracturers entered into formal patronage under Somnolent.

And with that, the next morningâ€™s objective became clear: Storm the Perfid Embassy. Rescue Terrick. Prepare for the consequences.

---
