# Sessions 12â€“14: Fire, Faith and the Corona of the Ashen Choir

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The Defracturers assault the Perfid embassy, rescue Terrick and pursue the Corona as Prusset is murdered in custody.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 12â€“14 |
| **Recorded** | 26 March 2026 at 19:55 |
| **Arc** | The Perfid embassy |

## Preparation and Poor Decisions

The Defracturers knew what was coming. War, in all but name.

They spent what little time they had preparing â€” though, as ever, preparation took many forms. Ballar and Erelmor took to the shops, only to find the armour they sought unfinished. Ballar compensated in the only way he knew how: buying an alarming number of healing potions and commissioning a new sign for his bedroom door, priorities firmly intact.

Meanwhile, Ieuan took a more measured approach. Through Noctua, he mapped the [[The Powers That Be#Perfid|Perfid]] Embassy, identifying entrances, weak points, and patterns. It was careful, thoughtful workâ€”the kind that might have changed everything.

It didnâ€™t.

Before departing, Ballar emptied his Bag of Rust Tricks, releasing a petrified goat, a boar, and an owl into their ranks. It was equal parts strategy and spectacle.

Then, with what confidence they could muster, they chose their entry point: The chapel door.

It was, unfortunately, the worst possible choice.

## The Embassy Breach

The battering ram struck once and the embassy answered.

A runic blast detonated outward, throwing the party back in a thunderous shockwave that echoed through the halls. Any hope of stealth died instantly. What followed was chaos.

Waves of Perfidian enforcers poured in â€” skinheaded zealots and hardened thugs, all rallying around a fanatic of Elgira who radiated fervor and fear in equal measure. The battle was immediate and brutal, with bodies dropping quickly on both sides.

The fanatic proved the greatest threat, twisting the battlefield with fear and divine fury, pushing the Defracturers toward retreat. Then Enrak intervened.

A precise volley of Magic Missiles shattered the fanaticâ€™s concentration, breaking the spell that had begun to unravel the party. The momentum shifted instantly. The Defracturers surged forward, cutting down the remaining enemies and reclaiming control of the room.

For a moment, it seemed they had regained the upper hand. Then everything fell apart.

## Fragmentation

In the aftermath of victory, the party did what they did best: They scattered.

Ballar, driven by instinct â€” or perhaps something stranger â€” charged down the main hallway, focused less on the mission and more on claiming territory. His owl followed. His boarâ€¦ did not, becoming lodged firmly in the doorway.

Ieuan, meanwhile, transformed into a rat and began a solo reconnaissance mission upstairs, quietly inspecting bedrooms with no immediate goal beyond curiosity. This left the rest of the party divided just as danger returned.

Ballar had unknowingly walked into a kill zone: two zealots and six thugs surged toward him. Alone, he took a defensive stance at the doorway, holding them at bay with sheer resilience. Blow after blow glanced off him. It was a remarkable stand. It did not last.

When the others finally forced their way into the hall â€” thanks to the boar quite literally smashing through the wall â€” they attempted to assist. Erelmor cast Faerie Fire. It caught Ballar.

Stripped of his defensive advantage, Ballar was overwhelmed in seconds. The tide turned violently. Only Ieuanâ€™s return from his upstairs excursion prevented a complete collapse, allowing the group to rally and finish the remaining enemies. The zealots, however, fledâ€”retreating deeper into the embassy.

## The Descent

The chase led downward. Erelmor vaulted the staircase in pursuit, while Ballar followedâ€”only to be sent crashing down by a hidden rune, the embassy itself fighting back at every turn. Below, in the dungeon, the truth of Perfidâ€™s operation was laid bare.

A torturer, hulking and methodical, stood over a barely conscious Terrick, preparing to finish what had been started. Erelmor hesitated but Ieuan did not. He struck first, disrupting the execution and immediately healing Terrick, pulling him back from the brink. The group descended upon the torturer, overwhelming him with coordinated force.

Among the remains, Ieuan claimed a Periapt of Wound Closure, a small but potent reward.

## Truths and Tunnels

Terrick, battered but alive, shared what he knew. The fanatic and zealots had come through a hidden passage deeper within the dungeon, forcing others out before preparing their own escape. He spoke of interrogation, of refusing to betray the party, and of a strange phrase the fanatic had sought: â€œTheâ€¦ Coca cola of a shaky wire?â€

The party stared. Then Din â€” speaking through Ballarâ€™s ring â€” laughed. â€œCorona of the Ashen Choir.â€

The real prize. The real objective.

## The Church of Elgira

The pursuit led through the passage and out into an unexpected location: The [[Perfidian Faith|Church of Elgira]], standing quietly on the edge of [[Somnolent]].

Inside, the final confrontation awaited. The fanatic, now crowned in glowing radiance, stood at the altar alongside the remaining zealots. A portal churned at their feet.

The Defracturers charged.

The fanatic responded instantlyâ€”Thunder Step erupted through the room, scattering the party violently. Enrak attempted to answer with overwhelming firepower, unleashing Aganazzarâ€™s Scorcherâ€”only for it to be counterspelled effortlessly.

The power disparity was unmistakable. Before the party could recover, the fanatic and one zealot escaped through the portal, leaving the last behind to ensure their retreat.

They killed him quickly. It changed nothing.

## Ash and Aftermath

With the portal gone and the church searched, the Defracturers stepped outside and found Elmwood Bluevine, Job Ashmere, and a contingent of guards waiting.

The truth came together quickly. The failed embassy assault had triggered a wider response. While the party had pursued the zealots, others had moved to secure Prusset. Elmwoodâ€™s reaction was immediate â€” and alarming. He opened a Dimension Door, dragging Job with him as he shouted: â€œBack to the Shackles â€” now!â€

When the Defracturers arrived, the smell hit first. Burnt flesh. Burnt hair.

Inside the cell, Alistair Prusset was dead â€” burned to a charred husk within his cage, with Summer Quaresh and Eden Carrion gone. Enrak protested, against all evidence in front of him, insisting Summer would never do this.

## One Manâ€™s Victory

Not all of the Defracturers shared in the horror. Ballar, seeing opportunity in chaos, had doubled back to the embassy. While the others chased zealots and uncovered conspiracies, he claimed his prize: The master bedroom.

## What Comes Next

By the time Romayne Drayton arrived, the damage was done. The ambassador was dead.

The zealots had escaped. The truth was unraveling faster than anyone could contain it.

Romayne thanked the party, though the weight behind his words was heavier than gratitude. He instructed them to regroup at the embassy in the morning.

The Defracturers agreed and, in true fashion, began planning something utterly absurd in the face of mounting crisis: A grand canapÃ© luncheon.

# Session 15: Stewards, Saddles and Shambling Roots

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The new bastion gains staff and horses before the road to Rana Viam begins with a deadly shambling mound.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 15 |
| **Recorded** | Friday, 27 March 2026 at 18:34 |
| **Arc** | The road to Rana Viam |

With embassies burned, diplomats dead, and [[Somnolent]] now far too small for the scale of the troubles gathering around it, the Defracturers turned, at least briefly, to something resembling administration.

It was time to hire staff.

## A Household in Need of Order

The interviews for a new head steward were, in their own way, as dangerous as any dungeon.

First came Mariasz Salhum, a former chief steward from a Temiri trading vessel. She carried herself with the calm authority of someone who had long ago learned how to keep difficult people fed, organized, and alive. Strong-willed, sensible, and plainly competent, she immediately stood apart from the field.

Then came Gorbels Valebrew, a so-called coffee mage whose references were dubious, his manner erratic, and whose deep personal vendetta against Brewed Awakening suggested a man with as many grievances as recipes.

Igon Duft followed, a gaunt and deeply unsettling figure with a glass eye and the air of a man who could absolutely solve problems, though perhaps not in ways anyone would later be comfortable learning about.

Then there was Ghorgadush, a towering half-orc swaddled in a patchwork vest made from bits of assorted wildlife, speaking in the third person and presenting the group with freshly baked cookies as though this were the most natural thing in the world.

Finally came Derrick Lowgins, Terrickâ€™s brother, whose interview somehow revealed less about his own capabilities than it did about the deeply worrying shape of Terrickâ€™s private life. His hints at abuse and misery left an unpleasant stillness in the room.

In the end, the choice was no choice at all. Mariasz Salhum was appointed Head Steward, the only candidate who seemed remotely suited to the role. The others, fortunately or unfortunately, were still welcomed into lesser positions.

With the household somewhat settled, the Defracturers gathered and did something even rarer than hiring responsibly:

They agreed to put aside their personal vendettas.

At least for now.

## The Luncheon of Great Importance

The group knew they needed to project competence. They were no longer merely adventurers blundering from crisis to crisis. They were patrons, agents, and increasingly visible players in the politics of Somnolent. The next gathering had to go well.

Plans were made. Appearances mattered. Gorbels and Igon were to remain hidden away in the kitchen, out of sight and out of mind. That, naturally, did not happen. The new household steward either ignored or overruled that instruction, and the result was a spread of distinctly underwhelming pastries that did not quite match the grandeur the occasion demanded.

Still, the guests came.

Romayne Drayton, Alaaric Hammerfall, and Elmwood Bluevine all attended. Alaaric, in particular, arrived with the energy of a man on the brink of divine revelation, setting up what could only be described as a Pepe Silvia board of threads, clues, and frantic connective logic. Together, the group sifted through everything they had uncovered and finally began to shape the larger picture.

Several conclusions emerged.

Eden Carrion was almost certainly the loyal Crow they had been circling around for so long.

The Old Grey Pike, while clearly a dangerous figure with a bloodstained history, did not appear to have been directly involved in the immediate events. The House Bromere favour coin used to pay Buggieâ€™s gang had once belonged to him, yes â€” but he had lost it to Eden in the gambling den.

The possibility that Anthea Bloodclot had in truth been Saint Marra no longer seemed absurd.

Most importantly, the group recognized that too many factions were converging on the same objective. Antheaâ€™s circle, the Broken Fang, and others still unseen were all interested in the [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] ruins. If the Defracturers wanted answers, they would need to pursue that trail above all others.

The relics tied to the Sundering â€” names like the Staff of Broken Time and the Bands of Distortion â€” remained unaccounted for, and dangerously coveted.

The Broken Fang would need further watching, with Alaaric and Job agreeing to keep a close eye on them.

Then Elmwood took up the scroll the party had recovered from Tace Nox and offered the next step: another Ardynian site, Rana Viam. He could not say precisely what waited there, but he knew enough to warn them that it would not be a quick excursion. Whatever lay within, they should prepare for a long stay.

He also kindly offered to cure Ballarâ€™s petrified goat, only for Ballar to discover, to his embarrassment, that he could not actually produce it from the Bag of Rust Tricks at all.

## Provisions and Horses

Romayne, for his part, proved generous. He supplied the group with adventuring packs, ample rations, and pushed along Erelmorâ€™s studded leather order so it would be ready in time. As one final gesture, he sent them to Marda to secure horses for the road ahead.

Marda loved her animals with the intensity of a devout priestess, and she made it immediately clear that she would not tolerate fools riding them. This seemed aimed quite specifically at Ballar, who became instantly obsessed with Kingcup, the most vain and unmanageable beast in the stable.

Marda had recommendations, and to their credit, most of the group listened.

Enrak was paired with Bracken, a slow but incredibly steady mount, the kind that would remain upright even if its rider did not.

Ieuan took Cobb, another dependable horse whose calm and sturdiness made it a natural extension of his own temperament.

nâ€™Dong was given Nail, a former courier horse: quick, enduring, and somewhat shy when battle loomed.

Erelmor received Vesper, an elegant dark horse suited equally to grace and stealth.

And Ballar, ignoring all good sense, chose Kingcup â€” beautiful, dramatic, difficult, and clearly bred for parade grounds rather than warfare.

It was, in other words, a perfect match.

## The Road to Rana Viam

With provisions packed and horses at least partially under control, the Defracturers finally set out for Rana Viam.

For the first few hours, the road was mercifully uneventful. Then they saw it: drag marks leading from the road into the brush. Something had been taken, or had taken something else.

Naturally, Erelmor went first to investigate.

Naturally, it went badly.

The moment he approached, a trap snapped shut. Vines lashed around him, dragging him violently into the bushes and knocking him unconscious almost at once.

Ballar reacted first, hacking at the grasping growths and trying to cut Erelmor loose before whatever had laid the trap could strike again.

Then it emerged.

A Shambling Mound, massive and sodden, rose from the greenery like the road itself had decided to kill them.

The group attacked hard and fast, first scrambling to get their horses to safety, then turning all their attention to the creature. Their confidence faded quickly as blow after blow seemed to do almost nothing. The beast absorbed punishment with horrifying ease, and for a few moments it seemed as though they might be carved apart beside the roadside.

But numbers, persistence, and sheer bloody-mindedness began to tell. Slowly, painfully, the Defracturers brought the thing down.

When at last it collapsed into a heap of rotting vegetation and swamp-flesh, the party stood catching their breath, shaken but victorious.

Then nâ€™Dong, seeing not horror but opportunity, crouched by the corpse and harvested ten pieces of shambling mound jerky.

Because of course he did

# Session 16: Cackles, Corpses and Catastrophe

> [!abstract] Session summary
> Bandits, Eula, stirges and Cackle Fever turn the journey towards Rana Viam into a disastrous retreat.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 16 |
| **Recorded** | 08 April 2026 at 17:03 |
| **Arc** | The road to Rana Viam |

Before the road called them onward once again, the Defracturers took a moment to bring some order to the growing machinery of their bastion. Their instructions were clear enough, if not exactly ambitious across the board. The workshops were tasked with preparing uniforms, the libraries were directed to begin research into the loyal crows, and the sanctuary was, for now, left entirely idle. With those orders settled, the group turned their focus back to the road and to the looming mystery of Rana Viam.

The journey resumed under a lingering shadow. Their recent encounter with the shambling mound had shaken them more than most were willing to admit, and perhaps because of that, they chose to press on without taking even a short rest. If the road ahead promised more danger, then speed seemed preferable to lingering weakness. That decision was tested before long.

As they approached a farm along the roadside, the party was ambushed by a group of bandits. The attack came quickly, but the Defracturers were in little mood for being delayed by common cutthroats. Though caught by surprise, they responded with ruthless efficiency and made short work of their attackers.

As ever, Erelmor was the first to the captainâ€™s pockets. He found 50 gold pieces, but immediately noticed something deeply wrong about them. They bore the same reddish hue as the cursed coins they had encountered before â€” the same sort that heralded the appearance of the strange hooded woman. The group tossed the gold to the ground and began debating what to do. That was invitation enough.

The hooded woman appeared as suddenly and unnervingly as before, stepping into the scene with the calm confidence of someone arriving precisely where she expected to be. This time, she introduced herself as Eula. Without hesitation, she doubled the value of the coins in exchange for the bodies of the fallen bandits, treating the arrangement as though it were the most natural transaction in the world. This did not sit well with everyone.

Ballar, offended both as a paladin and as a man with some still-functioning sense of the sanctity of the dead, challenged the arrangement. He bristled at the idea of corpses being reduced to mere currency, especially when no proper rites or dignity would follow. The party pressed Eula for answers, but she was careful and maddeningly vague. All she would admit was that she was taking the bodies back to her base, and that she would be willing to pay well if they continued to provide her with fresh work.

Ballar, refusing to accept this quietly, attempted to ride off with one of the corpses in open defiance of her. Eula responded instantly, Misty Stepping across the space between them, plucking the body straight from his horse, and vanishing through a portal before any of them could properly react.

In the end, Erelmor came to the conclusion that money was money, and pocketed the reward without further moral struggle.

The party then made their way to the nearby farm, where they were greeted by the farmer Cobb â€” a fact which amused Ieuan, given his horse shared the name. Cobb was more than pleased to hear that the local bandits had been dealt with and offered the group food, cider, and room in the stables for the night. True to form, Ballar pushed that hospitality beyond its intended limits by simply taking Cobbâ€™s bed for himself.

Not to be outdone in poor judgment, Erelmor attempted to â€œrepayâ€ Cobbâ€™s kindness by slipping him one of the cursed coins. Fortunately, Ieuan caught the exchange and quietly replaced it with two pieces of legitimate gold before any lasting harm could be done.

The following morning, as the party departed, Ballar and Erelmor took some time on the road to discuss the cursed coins in greater detail. They debated what exactly the magic was doing, whether it marked ownership, summoned Eula, tracked the dead, or operated by some more complex rule none of them yet understood.

Their theorizing was interrupted by another grim curiosity. Just off the road lay two dead bodies beside a small burrow. Naturally, this was more than enough to invite immediate investigation.

Enrak leaned forward and peered into the hole. That proved to be a mistake.

Two swarms of stirges burst out and descended upon the party with savage speed, latching onto Enrak and Ballar and driving their proboscises into flesh with alarming efficiency. Blood flowed quickly, and for a moment the whole encounter threatened to become far more dangerous than such miserable vermin had any right to be.

The party responded swiftly. Erelmor struck from range with unnerving precision, while nâ€™Dong, in one of the more baffling tactical choices of the journey so far, cast Vicious Mockery at the swarms while bending down to theatrically suck at the corpses in what appeared to be an attempt to unsettle the stirges through imitation. Whatever the logic, the creatures were eventually destroyed.

With the immediate danger passed, the group examined the bodies. They looked freshly dead, and both wore a horrible rigor-mortis grin stretched across their faces. A second inspection of the burrow turned up a tightly sealed vial and a scroll of Lesser Restoration. The party then pushed the corpses down into the hole, and Erelmor, never one to miss a chance at experimentation, tucked a cursed coin into one dead manâ€™s pocket to see if it would summon Eula. It did not.

They carried on, but less than an hour later something far stranger took hold. Without warning, Ballar, Ieuan, Erelmor, and nâ€™Dong all began to giggle, snicker, and then laugh uncontrollably, each unable to understand what exactly was so funny. Only Enrak was spared.

A quick and alarmingly competent medicine check from nâ€™Dong revealed the truth: the afflicted had contracted Cackle Fever, more commonly called the Shrieks. The disease caused fits of incapacitating laughter under stress and, if left unchecked, could become deadly.

Ballar acted at once, using his paladinâ€™s cleansing powers to purge the disease from himself. The effort left him drained, however, and unable to offer the same cure to anyone else for the remainder of the day. With no better option, the group agreed to continue traveling while keeping distance between the infected and the healthy, hoping to find shelter before the disease or the road killed them. The road had one more cruelty in store before granting them that mercy.

With Ballar and Enrak riding slightly ahead, Ballar spotted danger on both sides of the trail: a cluster of giant centipedes to one side, and a giant scorpion lurking to the other. Recognizing the fragile state of his companions, he made a snap decision. Rather than alert the others and risk provoking one of their laughing fits, he simply continued riding forward as though nothing were wrong, hoping they might all slip through unnoticed. The scorpion had other ideas.

It scuttled out into the path, blocking their way and throwing the group immediately into combat. As battle began, Ieuan and Erelmor were both seized by the Shrieks, wracked with helpless laughter in their saddles and unable to act. At that moment, nâ€™Dong made a choice that would define the encounter. Judging the numbers to be the greater threat, he unleashed his breath weapon across two groups of centipedes.

In an instant, the problem multiplied. What might have been a manageable confrontation with one major predator became a nightmare of nine enemies, all active and enraged.

Enrak paid the price first, swarmed by centipedes and dragged down into unconsciousness while nâ€™Dong himself barely remained upright. The scorpion hammered at Ballar, who looked around and quickly recognized the full scope of the disaster: two allies laughing helplessly, one unconscious, one on the brink, and the enemy pressing in from all sides. His conclusion was simple and brutally practical. Get out.

Ballar healed Enrak just enough to bring him back, then turned Kingcup and fled the field at speed. As he escaped, Ieuan and Erelmor managed to fight through their laughter and regain control. It was not enough to salvage the battle. Enrak, only just restored, was immediately overwhelmed again and dropped. nâ€™Dong and Ieuan, panicked and recognizing collapse when they saw it, also fled.

The scorpion then turned on Erelmor, slamming its claws into him and bringing him down as well. Fortunately, Vesper had more sense than most of her riderâ€™s companions and bolted from the melee, carrying him to safety.

That left only Enrak, unconscious in the center of the chaos, with Bracken standing firm beneath him. Against all reason, he stirred back to consciousness one final time and managed a desperate escape.

The party galloped away for what felt like hours, not daring to slow until the sounds of pursuit had fully faded. By the time they finally stopped, the whole group had been reduced to a miserable, rattled, and thoroughly humbled state. They agreed that after such a hellish day of travel, rest was no longer optional.

Ieuan was tasked with finding a suitable place to make camp, and to his credit he found one: secluded, defensible, and nearly perfect for the long night ahead. After everything the road had thrown at them, it was the first decision all day that had gone entirely right.

# Session 17: Shrieks, Widows and Time Juice

> [!abstract] Session summary
> After an owlbear attack and an ogre rescue, the party reaches Rana Viam and hears Saint Vaelenâ€™s warning.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 17 |
| **Recorded** | 22 April 2026 at 19:23 |
| **Arc** | Rana Viam |

After the misery of the previous dayâ€™s travel, the Defracturers finally settled into the secluded camp Ieuan had found for them. The place was defensible, sheltered, and about as close to peace as the road seemed willing to grant them. The group spent some time tending to their horses, all of whom had endured the horrors of the last day with varying degrees of grace. While every mount responded well enough to food, water, and rest, it was impossible not to notice that the two sturdy beasts, Bracken and Cobb, seemed the least bothered by anything, as though hardship simply rolled off them.

As the camp settled into its rotation of watches and rest, Ballar was eager to trance first. Unfortunately, he did not find silence there. The soul within his Ring of Mind Shielding, the one calling itself Din, interrupted him again, pressing for answers. It wanted to know where they were, where they were going, and why.

Ballar, unsurprisingly, had questions of his own. Who was Din? What did he want? And, perhaps most importantly, why should Ballar care in the first place? Din answered more plainly than before. He wanted a body. He believed Ballar might be the sort of man capable of finding one for him, and in return he would help guide the group through the ruins ahead, at least where he could. Ballar did not seem particularly impressed by this bargain, and the two eventually reached an uneasy pause. They would shelve the conversation until the promised help was actually needed.

Later, while Erelmor entered his own trance, things went much worse. His rest became, as it so often did, a descent into nightmare. But with Cackle Fever still clinging to him, the nightmare did not remain private. Instead, he burst into wild, unhinged laughter for half a minute, the sound carrying through the trees and shredding the calm of the camp.

Even the best resting place could not protect them from that.

It was not long before the laughter drew the attention of two hulking owlbears. Worse still, as the creatures closed in, Ieuan and nâ€™Dong also succumbed to the shrieks, leaving half the party helpless with laughter just as the camp came under attack. Ballar heard the chaos and, in a moment of grim tactical assessment, apparently decided that Enrak could probably handle one owlbear alone. This proved optimistic. Within moments, the beast had buried its claws into the fire mageâ€™s very soft and vulnerable flesh.

Then Ballar did something unexpected. Drawing on strange and specific knowledge from his youth, apparently his family had kept owlbears in the Novis family zoo, he somehow managed to intimidate one of the creatures so completely that it fled. It was a ridiculous and yet extremely effective display.

By then, Ieuan and nâ€™Dong had shaken off their hysterics and rushed to help Enrak. But the true hero of the moment was Bracken, Enrakâ€™s broad, sturdy, deeply unglamorous horse. Seeing his rider in danger, the great beast took the owlbear head-on, buying the group the time they needed to kill it and, more importantly, to keep Enrak alive.

The rest of the long rest passed in an uneasy half-silence. No one truly relaxed after that. Still, when morning came, Ieuan and nâ€™Dong found themselves free of the shrieks, their bodies having finally shaken off the disease. Ballar, recognizing how much of a liability Erelmor remained under its effects, used Lay on Hands to cure him, though the effort left him with even fewer healing reserves for the road ahead.

The party resumed their journey. They had not gone far before they came upon another grim roadside scene: a broken cart, the corpse of an elf, the corpse of a horse that appeared to have been bitten, and a clear trail of blood leading onward. Ballar took point and quickly spotted the source of the dangerâ€”a pair of ogres lurking near a house ahead. With uncharacteristic good sense, he immediately sent his own horse running from the danger.

The party advanced, hearing screams from inside the house just as a third ogre emerged from the shadows. Battle began in earnest. The group fought well at first, and nâ€™Dong in particular acted decisively, using his psychic shove to place himself between one of the ogres and the woman trapped inside the house.

But then one of the creatures landed a strike on Ballar so brutal it nearly took his head from his body. The mood changed instantly.

Resolve gave way to fear and caution. nâ€™Dong disengaged and hid, while the rest of the Defracturers abandoned any thought of a clean engagement and instead began taking potshots from range. The strategy worked well enough to bring down two of the ogres, but the last was no fool. It crashed through the house and turned its attention on the woman inside.

Ballar threw himself back into the fray, casting Compelled Duel to drag the ogreâ€™s attention onto himself, while nâ€™Dong pulled the woman through a window to safety. This was heroic. Unfortunately, nâ€™Dong then undercut the moment entirely by casually explaining to the deeply injured and traumatized woman that her husband had been â€œcompletely destroyed,â€ apparently without realizing the sacrifice he had made to keep the woman alive.

Thankfully, Ieuan had better instincts. He quickly healed the woman, then rejoined the fight and struck the ogre viciously, leaving it tottering on the brink of death. Ballar stepped in and delivered the final blow. The rescued woman introduced herself as Mireya. She had been traveling to Hushvale with her husband, planning to establish a gambling hall there, but his sudden and thoroughly unpleasant death had understandably cast that dream into doubt.

At this point, an old friend appeared. Eula, absolutely delighted to add some Ogres to her cache of bodies, provided them with 75 coins for the three. The group questioned her as intensely as they felt they could, gaining an understanding that the coins let Eula know when a murder was committed nearby, and with that vanished.

The Defracturers began to discuss what they should do with the coins, whether to take them, spend them, keep them at the bastion, or to leave them in Rana Viam. When they mentioned Rana Viam, Mireya visibly paled. She had heard enough rumours about the ruinsâ€”how people could become trapped within for impossible lengths of timeâ€”to immediately ask the most practical question anyone had raised all day:

â€œWho is going to look after the horses?â€ It was an excellent point.

The party agreed to let Mireya travel with them and tend the horses while they explored the ruins. Ballar was convinced, if not delighted, by her competence almost immediately, especially when she managed to get Kingcup eating from her palm with insulting ease. Bracken took on the additional burden of hauling the cart and the husbandâ€™s body, while Mireya rode Cobb alongside Ieuan. Just outside Rana Viam, the party paused long enough to give the dead man a proper burial. Only then did they finally approach the ruin itself.

At the entrance, the group encountered a riddle and, whether through intelligence or sheer luck, solved it quickly enough that even they seemed unsure which of the two deserved the credit. Inside, they took stock of the initial layout. A collection of bodies to the right immediately suggested danger, and the party sensibly chose to avoid that problem for the time being. Erelmor, still not fully trusting the space, went one step further and turned invisible.

They checked the central corridor first, where they found a basin containing a familiar green residue. Ieuan immediately recognized it as the same â€œTime Juiceâ€ they had encountered in Tace Nox. Ballar tried, and failed, to solve the adjacent door with brute force, so they moved on.

The left corridor led them to what looked like a bedroom, one that Erelmor suspected had once belonged to a chwinga. The room was cluttered with arcane paraphernalia: alchemical devices, notes, books, and one very notable flimsy wall.

Ballar, being Ballar, chose not to inspect it carefully. Instead, he hurled himself at it with all the subtlety of a siege engine, smashing through in a single leap like some unholy combination of the Kool-Aid Man and a charging war elephant. Beyond, the group discovered a more deliberate workspace, containing a desk with a single stone upon it and a chest nearby.

Ieuan examined the stone and, through instinct and a growing familiarity with [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] devices, deciphered the runes enough to activate it. What followed was a recorded message from Saint Vaelen, and it dumped a terrifying amount of information into the room. The message confirmed that the Saints of Elgira were not merely myth, but real figuresâ€”and deeply divided ones at that. Vaelen feared for his life, caught between Marra, whose hunger for war threatened to consume everything, Desmon, whose obsession with power seemed equally dangerous, and Erith, whose detachment was perhaps worst of all.

While the others absorbed that revelation, nâ€™Dong turned his attention to the alchemical workstation and, with a practiced hand no doubt informed by years of poisoncraft, managed to extract two more vials of Time Juice from the equipment in addition to the beaker they had already found.

Only then did the group return to the bodies they had avoided earlier. nâ€™Dong examined them and determined they had likely died one to two weeks ago. More importantly, they bore the unmistakable sigil of the Broken Fang.

At that point, still invisible, Erelmor knocked on a nearby door and heard the unmistakable sound of a troll on the other side. The party wasted no time. They fled back to the centre corridor, where nâ€™Dong poured some Time Juice into the basin. Arcane green light shimmered through the adjoining door in a web-like pattern, and the passage slowly opened.

Beyond lay one of the strangest sights the Defracturers had yet seen. The room ahead was filled with motionâ€”mephits, snakes, cockatrices, stirges, imps, all zig-zagging chaotically across the chamber in every direction.

The whole thing looked like a living puzzle. Or, as Erelmor put it with perfect clarity: â€œLooks like Frogger.â€

---

# Session 18: Frogger, Flames and the Golemâ€™s Toll

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The party crosses Rana Viamâ€™s living time puzzle and is annihilated by the golem guarding the relic.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 18 |
| **Recorded** | 03 May 2026 at 21:35 |
| **Arc** | Rana Viam |

As the Defracturers stared into the strange chamber ahead, the chaos before them suddenly stopped. The clouds of creatures that had been darting in every direction â€” mephits, flying snakes, cockatrices, stirges and worse â€” were all dragged into perfect stillness, frozen in place as though time itself had been paused. It was as if the room were holding its breath, waiting to see which of them would move first.

Naturally, Ballar was the one to break the silence. He strode straight into the room, and the moment he did, the magic snapped back into motion. A flying snake slammed bodily into him with a sickening thud, and in an instant Ballar found himself back at the beginning of the chamber, as though the entire attempt had been rewound.

That was enough to make it clear what they were dealing with, though not enough to make them approach it sensibly. nâ€™Dong, true to form, became almost completely paralyzed by indecision, choosing to stand and observe the moving creatures in the hope that some hidden pattern would reveal itself. Enrak, at the opposite extreme, hurled himself into the challenge with reckless confidence and reached the middle of the room before nâ€™Dong had managed to take even a single meaningful step.

Meanwhile, Erelmor and Ieuan attempted something like cooperation, moving together for a time and trying to solve the flow of creatures as a pair. That effort ended abruptly when a stirge caught Erelmor off guard and sent him back to the beginning.

At the center of the room stood a plinth, and when Enrak reached it, he immediately did what Enrak does best: he pressed the button without much thought for consequences. A distinct sound rang out through the chamber, and for reasons he could not fully explain, Enrak suddenly felt safe.

That was enough for the others. Ballar, nâ€™Dong, and Ieuan made their way to the middle in relatively short order, though Erelmor remained stranded at the entrance. As nâ€™Dong continued to linger and study the patterns, the others launched themselves toward the final stretch.

Once again, Enrak sprinted ahead â€” but this time, just a little too carelessly. He stumbled into the path of a cockatrice and took a hit that seemed to drain the strength straight out of his body, the petrification creeping over him almost instantly. Ballar, thrown off by Enrakâ€™s collision, was struck by a cockatrice as well, though he managed to resist the same fate.

The room spat them both back to the center. Enrak, however, returned as a stone statue.

For once, Ballar was the only one who seemed to care immediately. While Din whispered unhelpfully in his ear that perhaps this was a good time to take a long rest, Ballar instead threw himself into the absurd logistics of rescuing a petrified companion. He tried to work out whether Enrak could be dragged with block and tackle, wondered if he could somehow fit him into the bag of holding, and finally attempted to simply lift the stone man by brute force. Impressively, he managed at least to tip Enrak onto his side.

Erelmor arrived at the center just in time to witness this display of commitment, offered precisely no assistance, and moved on. Spotting the second plinth at the far end of the room, he briefly attempted to solve the problem from range by throwing a dagger at it, but his aim was well off, coming far closer to hitting one of the roaming creatures than the mechanism he intended.

Realizing no one else was going to solve Enrakâ€™s predicament, Ballar caught up with the others and informed Ieuan. The druid then tried something entirely bizarre: he cast Moonbeam directly onto Enrakâ€™s petrified body. Against all logic, and after dealing the helpless fire mage a respectable amount of damage, the spell actually reversed the petrification. Disaster narrowly avoided.

At last, the group stumbled their way through the final segment of the chamber and collapsed onto the five pressure plates at the back. As they did, the rear wall slid open, revealing a narrow corridor and another sealed chamber beyond. Ignoring Dinâ€™s increasingly sensible suggestion that they rest while they still could, the Defracturers pressed on.

Inside the next chamber stood a massive stone golem, dormant but imposing, wearing a pair of ornate bracers that immediately drew the groupâ€™s attention. Nearby was a small dais holding a scroll within a bowl, accompanied by a cryptic warning etched into its stand.

> ***What strength you clutch, will quickly be lost.***
> ***What power you yield, will increase your odds.***
> ***Feed me all you would spend in war,***
> ***And you'll be granted one chance more.***
> ***Empty hands may yet prevail -***
> ***But those who offer nothing, will fail.***

The group, naturally, focused all their energy on the golem, trying to make sense how it related to the warning in the least efficient ways possible. They handed it weapons. They placed things in its hands. Ballar, for reasons perhaps known only to him, tried putting a javelin into its mouth. When that achieved nothing, nâ€™Dong decided to try an emerald instead. The golem remained inert.

Eventually, having exhausted these baffling possibilities, they returned to the dais. Ieuan took the scroll from the bowl. That was the trigger. The golem stirred to life as the bracers began to flicker with ancient power. A surge of stasis magic washed over the room, seizing at the party and demanding a toll. Each of them felt some invisible pressure urging them to surrender their strength.

Enrak, Ieuan, and nâ€™Dong yielded willingly, gasping as magic and power drained from them. Ballar resisted at first, but eventually relented and let some of his strength flow away. Only Erelmor refused entirely, hoarding his power without hesitation.

When the effect ended, four green flames burned above the dais. Then the golem roared to life.

Ballar charged first, stepping into melee against the construct without hesitation. Erelmor attempted a different tactic, trying to reason with it through Dissonant Whispers, but the construct had no interest in negotiation. nâ€™Dong, in what may have been the best decision of the encounter, mocked the golem by calling it a â€œshit transformer,â€ and somehow the insult landed with surprising force.

While Ballar and the others occupied the construct, Enrak and Ieuan turned their attention to the strange green flames, trying to work out what they represented. Their investigations were cut short when the golem brought one of its enormous fists down on Ballar with horrifying force, dropping him before the group could properly process what they had just seen.

Suddenly the encounter stopped being amusing.

Erelmor rushed in and tried to strip the bracers from the golem, managing to undo one before being hurled away. Ieuan revived Ballar. Enrak unleashed the power of his Circlet of Blasting. Erelmor hurled psychic blades in frustration, only to realize they were all but useless against the construct. Desperate, he sprinted to the dais and tried to surrender his power belatedly â€” but the chamber remained silent. That opportunity had passed.

Even with the group now fully engaged, they were hopelessly outmatched. The golem moved with methodical brutality, felling them one by one until the chamber fell quiet.

And then, somehow, they awoke. The Defracturers found themselves outside the golemâ€™s chamber, bloodied, battered, but alive. Whatever magic governed the ruin had spared them a true death â€” or perhaps merely reset the challenge once again.

When Erelmor cautiously peeked back into the room, he saw one crucial difference. There were now only three green flames above the dais.

That was enough to make the lesson clear. Shaken and finally willing to listen, the group at last took Dinâ€™s advice and settled in for a long rest, knowing they would need every scrap of strength if they hoped to survive their next attempt.

# Session 19: Bands, Broken Stone and the Glass Fang

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The golem falls, the relic is secured and Broken Fang scouts confront the party as they leave Rana Viam.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 19 |
| **Recorded** | 23 June 2026 at 13:23 |
| **Arc** | Rana Viam |

Battered and bruised from their previous defeat, the Defracturers finally took the long rest they had so thoroughly earned. For once, the night passed with only minimal disturbance from Erelmor, whose nightmares remained troubling by ordinary standards, but were positively tranquil by his own. It was as close to peace as the ruins of Rana Viam seemed willing to offer.

During the rest, Ieuan devoted time to the Star Seed gifted to him by Father Zantos. Under his careful study, a single leaf unfurled, and within it he recognized the constellation of his Starry Form. It was a quiet but significant momentâ€”one of growth and affirmation amidst the oppressive stone and strange time magic of the ruin. Elsewhere, the rest of the party focused on a more immediate concern: how, exactly, they were going to kill the golem that had crushed them so thoroughly the last time.

By the end of their rest, the group felt renewed. More than renewed, in factâ€”there was a fresh sharpness to them, a greater confidence and power humming beneath the surface. They prepared themselves thoroughly. Mage Armour was cast, Aid was bestowed, a steed was summoned, and the group moved back toward the golem chamber with far more caution and coordination than before.

When they entered, they found the golem once again inactive, looming in silence as though it had never annihilated them in the first place. For a moment, the chamber held still. Then Ieuan, accepting that there would be no progress without risk, stepped forward and investigated the dais, reactivating the construct. What followed was, by Defracturer standards, a masterpiece of planning.

Ieuan and Ballar took immediate control of the battlefield, repeatedly using Command to halt the golemâ€™s movements and rob it of momentum. While the construct stood helpless under their magic, the rest of the group unloaded everything they had. Fire, force, steel, and spellwork hammered into the stone giant. Erelmor quickly discovered that poison was useless against a construct of solid stone, but by then the party had plenty of other ways to hurt it.

nâ€™Dong further locked the golem down with a well-placed Web, making the great brute even easier to punish. For a time, it was almost surgical. The golem was stalled, restrained and systematically dismantled as the party poured their strongest magic into it. Eventually, though, as their most powerful spells began to run dry, the construct broke through their control and fought back.

The chamber filled with the sound of grinding stone and thunderous impacts as the golem began swinging its colossal fists, slowing the party and forcing them onto the defensive. But by then, the Defracturers could sense it: this was not the rise of a second defeat, but the death rattle of a great enemy. They pressed the advantage. At last, the golem collapsed, crashing to its knees before exploding into a heap of broken rubble. Victory was theirs.

The group rushed to the remains and quickly recovered the Bands of Distortion, the ornate relics the construct had been wearing. Ieuan, once again proving invaluable in matters arcane, investigated the wreckage and determined that the bracers were linked to the golem through a heart stone. If they took that stone with them as well, the bracers would remain bound to the party rather than vanishing back into the ruin the moment they tried to leave.

That led, inevitably, to a discussion about whether any of them should attempt to wear or use the bracers immediately. Thankfully, good sense prevailed. When Erelmor found a fitted holding case for the relic among the remains, the group chose caution over greed and stored the bracers away safely. They also gathered whatever other riches they could, including a sealed [[The Sundering of Ardynia|Ardynian]] scroll, before turning their attention to the next question.

Should they press their luck and go back through the ruin to loot everything they had missed, or take their victory and leave? The debate split the group neatly in two. Some saw the chance for more treasure. Others felt they had already pushed fate far enough. In the end, compromise won out: they would take a short rest, then leave Rana Viam and make for Hushvale, where they could recover properly and decide their next move from safety. The world, however, was not done with them yet.

As they left the ruin, they found a stranger waiting for them on the stairs outside. He ordered them all to stay exactly where they were. That, unsurprisingly, was not well received. As two other scouts made a break for it deep into the woods, nâ€™Dongâ€”already on edge and immediately recognizing the Broken Fang sigil on the scoutâ€™s lapelâ€”wasted no time at all. He surged forward, grappled the man and hurled him back down into the entrance of Rana Viam before leaping after him and demanding answers.

The scout, terrified and completely outmatched, blurted out the most important name he could have chosen: [[Ulsar Quinn]]. The name hit nâ€™Dong like a physical blow. Ulsar had once commanded the unit nâ€™Dong had served in before his disastrous expedition into the Ardynian ruinsâ€”before his transformation, before his escape from the Broken Fang. This was not just another scout. This was a thread tying directly back into the darkest parts of nâ€™Dongâ€™s past.

As the rest of the group caught up and took in the scene, the scout confirmed something even worse: their horses had been taken. Ulsar had them. That was enough to turn the entire moment from tense encounter into looming crisis. Ballar began to speak, no doubt ready to pry more useful information from the man. But before he could get far, Erelmor drew back his dagger, clearly ready to end the conversationâ€”and the scoutâ€”then and there.

And there the session hung: with the Bands finally won, the ruin behind them, the Broken Fang suddenly in front of them, and the next move poised on the edge of a knife.

# Session 20: Plague, Pursuit and Plan E

> [!abstract] Session summary
> The Defracturers pursue two fleeing Broken Fang scouts, bargain with a sentient fungus named Plague and secure rough maps of Ulsar Quinnâ€™s campâ€”at the cost of one prisoner and several increasingly questionable decisions.

| | |
|---|---|
| **Session** | 20 |
| **Arc** | Broken Fang Pursuit |

The moment hung for only a heartbeat.

Erelmorâ€™s arm snapped forward, dagger flashing as the party looked on with a mixture of hope and dread. For one brief instant, they all seemed to pray for the same thingâ€”that the rogueâ€™s strike might go wide, that they might still salvage a live prisoner, that reason might somehow prevail.

It did not.

The blade struck cleanly, slitting the scoutâ€™s throat with a wet, ugly finality. The body sagged in nâ€™Dongâ€™s grip, and whatever advantage interrogation might have brought died with it. The rest of the party swallowed their frustration as best they could and turned immediately to the more pressing problem: two more scouts had fled, and if they reached the Broken Fang camp with warning, the horsesâ€”and perhaps any advantage the Defracturers still heldâ€”would be lost.

The response was immediate and chaotic in that distinct Defracturer fashion. nâ€™Dong took to the air for reconnaissance, while Ieuan shifted into elk form and carried Erelmor westward through the forest. Ballar, meanwhile, summoned a new steed: a hideous necrotic doe, an ooze of venomous tendrils forced into equine shape, which he and Enrak rode into the trees like some nightmare procession.

Ieuan picked up the fleeing scoutsâ€™ trail almost immediately, but it soon split into two routes. With no time to waste, the party divided. Erelmor established a psychic link between Enrak and nâ€™Dong, keeping the separated groups in contact while nâ€™Dong tried to maintain an aerial position somewhere between them.

Ballar and Enrak, taking the north-eastern route, were the first to catch their man. They drove the terrified scout toward a riverbank and boxed him in. He babbled in panic, pleading with them not to make too much noise, not to call out, not to draw attention.

Then the forest itself answered the warning.

From somewhere to the north came a sweet, lilting shanty, almost impossibly charming, the kind of song that seemed to drift directly into the mind rather than the ear. For a moment, everything threatened to go sidewaysâ€”but Ballar, Enrak and even the scout all managed to resist the lure. The prisoner, now even more frantic, stammered out the explanation: a bog siren, something that haunted the swamps and had lured many of the Broken Fang to their deaths.

Ballar had heard enough. The man had no horse, no useful information worth the time it took to wring it from him and no dignity left to preserve. So Ballar simply knocked him unconscious and stuffed him into the bag of holding, ignoring the muffled panic and increasingly reasonable complaints emanating from within. With Enrak in tow, he followed the creek, determined to rejoin the others.

That was when the forest began to rustle.

To the west, Ieuan and Erelmor had problems of their own. nâ€™Dongâ€™s aerial scouting, while earnest, was of debatable usefulnessâ€”his reports of â€œcowsâ€ and then â€œnaked cowsâ€ provided little clarity. Still, the pair pressed on until they came to a choice between scaling a cliff face or traversing a patch of suspicious-looking fungus. True to form, Ieuan chose the fungus without hesitation.

Having identified it as poisonous, he made an even stranger choice and cast Speak with Plants.

The fungus responded.

It called itself [[Plague]].

The entity was simple-minded but disturbingly earnest, utterly consumed by its singular purpose: to spread. It explained that it had been left there by a â€œbad mad manâ€ of the forest, one later killed by the â€œforest guardians.â€ It knew little beyond that, except its own hunger to proliferate.

In one of the more alarming negotiations the party had yet engaged in, Ieuan struck a bargain. If Plague allowed them to pass without harm, he would take five cuttings and spread them elsewhere. As he carefully clipped samples from the fungus, concentrating with all the delicacy of a druid handling some eldritch horticultural contract, he felt Erelmor tug at his cloak.

Something was wrong.

Through the telepathic link came panicked cries from the othersâ€”webs and spiders.

Ballar and Enrak had barely entered the creek before two giant spiders descended upon them. Ballarâ€™s grotesque summoned doe was quickly torn apart, while Enrak was wrapped in fresh webbing almost at once. From inside the bag of holding, the prisonerâ€”now identified as Sallaâ€”screamed the incredibly fair question:

> â€œWhy did you run into the spiders?!â€

Ballar, however, was in no mood for criticism from luggage. He threw himself into full heroics, helping Enrak and charging the spiders head-on. The pair fought with savage urgency, throwing magic and steel into the fray, and Ballar even released a small rat from his bag of tricks to aid the battle. The ratâ€™s contribution was, charitably, limited. Still, the spiders fell, and the pair pushed on through the dense forest toward the others.

Back on the western route, Ieuan and Erelmor emerged past the poisonous growth only to find the ground split again, their path blocked by a pool of tar. Opinions on how best to proceed varied wildly. There was talk of misty stepping. There was, from Ballar through the psychic link, the deeply unhelpful but very sincere suggestion to â€œlick it.â€

Before anyone could commit to that spectacularly poor idea, nâ€™Dong spotted the second scout from the air. He acted instantly, hurling a bolt of fire down at the fleeing man and calling for aid by announcing, with admirable uselessness, that the target was near â€œthe naked cows.â€

Salla, from his imprisonment, insisted they would never catch the man. This second scout, he explained, was named Malt, and Malt was â€œthe fastest man alive.â€

That turned out to be a bold but inaccurate claim.

With nâ€™Dong raining fire from above and Ieuan and Erelmor racing through the trees to cut off his escape, Malt was swiftly cornered and singed into surrender. The full party regrouped at last, now with two prisoners instead of one corpse and one lost lead.

Faced with the need for useful intelligence, the Defracturers offered the scouts a grim little contest: one of them might live, depending on who gave the most useful information. Their immediate demand was simple enoughâ€”a map of the Broken Fang camp, especially the location of the stolen horses.

Even in manacles, both scouts did their best. The resulting sketches were crude, sparse and lacking in the detail the party would have preferred, but they were close enough to one another to be believable. The Defracturers, though unimpressed, decided the pair would liveâ€”for now. There was always the possibility they might still prove useful later.

The party pressed on through the forest in search of somewhere safe to rest and think.

The forest, however, had not finished with them.

With nâ€™Dong taking point, the group blundered straight into a nest of Egg Hunter Hatchlings, and the creatures swarmed them almost instantly. nâ€™Dong was the first to go down, quickly followed by Salla and Malt, both of whom proved less resilient when caught in the middle of a monster ambush. Ballar managed to revive nâ€™Dong, while Ieuan cast Spare the Dying on Malt. But with the hatchlings crashing over the party in waves, there was no chance to save Salla.

The fight that followed was ugly, frantic and exhausting. By the time the last of the creatures were dead, the Defracturers were panting, bloodied and thoroughly sick of the forest.

Then Ieuan made things stranger still.

Calmly, almost proudly, he took one of the Plague cuttings and planted it directly into Sallaâ€™s corpse.

The group stared at him in open confusion.

That, somehow, was the point at which the conversation finally turned to planning. How exactly were they going to infiltrate the Broken Fang camp and recover their horses? Ieuan, still buoyed by whatever deeply questionable confidence had led him to bargain with sentient fungus, launched into an elaborate proposal centred around Plague.

The reception was immediate and firm.

It was, he was told, an â€œacceptable Plan E.â€

Thus chastened, and increasingly aware that the others did not share his enthusiasm for fungal warfare, Ieuan looked down at his feet while the rest of the Defracturers debated what their actual next move should be.

And there, deep in hostile woods, burdened by a surviving prisoner, cuttings of a spreading blight and two hand-drawn maps of the Broken Fang camp, the session came to rest.

---

**Chronicle:** [[content/05 Campaign Chronicle/index]]

**Navigation:** [[Session 19 - Bands, Broken Stone and the Glass Fang|Previous session]] Â· â€”

# Horses

The party's five mounts, as recorded in the campaign tracker and Session 15. The rider assignments below are the confirmed repository canon.

## Bracken

![[Bracken.png|260]]

**Recorded rider:** [[03 People/Player Characters/Enrak Kiran|Enrak Kiran]]

**D&D Beyond sheet:** [Bracken](https://www.dndbeyond.com/monsters/6298820-bracken)

Slow but exceptionally steady. Bracken stood against an owlbear to protect Enrak.

## Cobb

![[Cobb.png|260]]

**Recorded rider:** [[03 People/Player Characters/Ieuan|Ieuan]]

**D&D Beyond sheet:** [Cobb](https://www.dndbeyond.com/monsters/6298828-cobb)

A dependable, calm and sturdy mount. The name later caused some amusement when the party met a farmer also called Cobb.

## Vesper

![[Vesper.png|260]]

**Recorded rider:** [[03 People/Player Characters/Erelmor|Erelmor]]

**D&D Beyond sheet:** [Vesper](https://www.dndbeyond.com/monsters/6298790-vesper)

An elegant dark horse suited to stealth and grace. Vesper fled the scorpion battle carrying an unconscious Erelmor to safety.

## Nail

![[Nail.png|260]]

**Recorded rider:** [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong]]

**D&D Beyond sheet:** [Nail](https://www.dndbeyond.com/monsters/6298832-nail)

A former courier horse: quick, enduring and somewhat shy when battle approaches.

## Kingcup

![[Kingcup.png|260]]

**Recorded rider:** [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar Novis]]

**D&D Beyond sheet:** [Kingcup](https://www.dndbeyond.com/monsters/6298836-kingcup)

Beautiful, vain, difficult and more suited to a parade ground than a battlefield; an immediate match for Ballar.

## Current situation

At the end of [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 20 - Plague, Pursuit and Plan E|Session 20]]:

- The horses remained at a Broken Fang camp associated with [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]].
- Captured scouts Salla and Malt produced separate maps that broadly agreed on the camp's layout and the horses' approximate position.
- The party had not yet entered the camp or recovered any of the mounts.
- There is no confirmed indication that any horse has been injured.

# Open Threads

> [!info] Review basis
> This tracker has been reconciled against the chronicle through Session 20.

**New** threads emerged recently, **active** threads still drive the campaign, **reframed** threads remain relevant but no longer in their original form, and **stale** threads have seen no meaningful progress for many sessions.

## Immediate after Session 20

| Status | Thread | Current position |
|---|---|---|
| **Urgent** | Recover the party's horses and confront or evade [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] | Salla and Malt drew broadly consistent camp maps, including the approximate horse location. The party has not agreed on an infiltration plan. |
| **Urgent** | Decide what to do with Malt | Malt survived the Egg Hunter attack after Ieuan stabilised him and remains the party's prisoner. |
| **New / dangerous** | Resolve Ieuan's bargain with [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Plague|Plague]] | One of five cuttings was planted in Salla's corpse. Ieuan retains four and promised to spread them. |
| **Urgent** | Protect the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]] | The relic and its heart stone have been removed from Rana Viam and are likely targets for the Broken Fang and other relic hunters. |
| **Active** | Reach Hushvale | The party intended to travel there after leaving Rana Viam, but the pursuit and camp problem have interrupted that plan. |

## Active investigations

| Thread | Focus | Current position |
|---|---|---|
| Find **Prodosius** | [[03 People/Player Characters/n'Dong|n'Dong]] | Ulsar Quinn and the named high-ranking member [[03 People/Mentioned Figures/Rhul Slowdrift|Rhul Slowdrift]] may provide links towards him. Rhul's exact role is unknown. |
| Investigate [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]] activity | Whole party | The faction runs a smuggling network, searches Ardynian sites and has seized the party's horses. |
| Discover who murdered Erelmor's family | [[03 People/Player Characters/Erelmor|Erelmor]] | [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ervain Megbanas|Ervain Megbanas]] remains connected to the history of the Maw, but no longer appears to be part of the immediate Anthea and Bromere-coin conspiracy. |
| Understand [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eula|Eula]] and the cursed coins | Whole party | The coins alert Eula to killings nearby. Why she collects bodies, who she serves and what the corpses become remain unknown. |
| Trace the remaining Ardynian relics | Whole party | The party has the Bands of Distortion. The Staff of Broken Time, the Coronas of the Ashen Choir and other Sundering tools remain in play. |
| Understand Din and find him a body | [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar]] | The soul in Ballar's ring has offered guidance in return for a body. His identity and full agenda remain unresolved. |
| Determine whether Anthea was Saint Marra | Whole party | The Perfidian Church believed or claimed that she was. Vaelen's message confirms ancient conflict but does not substantiate Anthea's identity. |
| Locate Eden Carrion and Summer Quaresh | Whole party | Both disappeared after Alistair Prusset was killed in custody. Eden is identified as the Loyal Crow who hired Buggie. |
| Identify the exact Rootbound cell and killer | Whole party / Ieuan | Perfid hired a Rootbound cell, but the individual who administered Bloodspore remains unknown. |
| Recover the escaped Corona | Whole party | Perfidian agents escaped Somnolent through a portal with a Corona of the Ashen Choir. |
| Understand the sealed Ardynian scrolls | Whole party / Elmwood | Scrolls from Tace Nox and Rana Viam may reveal more about the Sundering and its relics. |

| Original thread | Status | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Identify and question or kill the Old Grey Pike | **Reframed** | The party met Ervain and established that Eden won his House Bromere coin at dice. The immediate conspiracy thread is closed, but Erelmor's family connection remains active. |
| Identify the Loyal Crow | **Identity closed; pursuit active** | Eden Carrion is identified, but her whereabouts and current plans remain unresolved. |
| Investigate other Ardynian ruins | **Ongoing relic arc** | Tace Nox and Rana Viam have been explored. The broader search for Sundering tools is now the meaningful version of this goal. |
| Find who ordered Anthea's murder | **Substantially closed** | The Church ordered it, Prusset arranged it and a Rootbound cell carried it out. The precise killer and the truth of the Marra claim remain open. |

# Plague

> [!summary] At a glance
> A sentient colony of poisonous fungus whose only clearly expressed desire is to spread.

## Known details

- Plague was discovered in the forest near [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]] while [[03 People/Player Characters/Ieuan|Ieuan]] and [[03 People/Player Characters/Erelmor|Erelmor]] pursued a fleeing Broken Fang scout.
- Ieuan communicated with it using **Speak with Plants**.
- It identified itself simply as **Plague**.
- It appeared simple-minded but capable of bargaining and understanding promises.
- Its overriding purpose is to spread.
- Plague claimed it had been left in the forest by a â€œbad mad manâ€.
- According to Plague, that figure was later killed by â€œforest guardiansâ€.
- It agreed to let Ieuan and Erelmor cross its poisonous growth unharmed in exchange for Ieuan taking and spreading five cuttings.
- Ieuan planted one cutting in Salla's corpse following the Egg Hunter attack.
- Four cuttings remained in Ieuan's possession at the end of the session.
- Ieuan later proposed using Plague as part of the assault on the Broken Fang camp. The idea was designated **Plan E**.

## Unanswered questions

- Who was the â€œbad mad manâ€ who created or cultivated Plague?
- What were the â€œforest guardiansâ€ that killed him?
- What will happen to Salla's infected corpse?
- What consequences might follow if Ieuan spreads the remaining cuttings?
- Is Plague connected to the other unnatural creatures found in the forest?

## Campaign appearances

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 20 - Plague, Pursuit and Plan E|Session 20]]

# Anthea Bloodclotâ€™s Note on Tace Nox

![[Anthea Bloodclot's Note on Tace Nox.png|500]]

## Transcription

> To whatever poor fool reads this â€” allow me to save you some teeth-grinding.
>
> The lights must form an arrow. Thatâ€™s the key. Point it at the altar, or youâ€™ll be swarmed. Youâ€™ve got six moves. No more. Six.
>
> Why an arrow? Because of course the Ardynians â€” curse be their brittle, egotistical bones â€” could never pass up a chance to overcomplicate a situation. Gods forbid they just wrote: â€œPush here to not die.â€
>
> I swear, every vault, every lock, every tomb of theirs is a shrine to smug superiority. Ageless? Yes. But if you outlive death and still need to solve three riddles and cast a starlight incantation to open your bloody wine cabinet, you havenâ€™t conquered time â€” youâ€™ve wasted it.
>
> Iâ€™ve already lost three crows to the damned thing, only Eden and I remain. If I ever find a chronarch that survived, if just one managed to evade Elgiraâ€™s clutches, I will put a knife through their eye and scatter their ashes across Rakara.
>
> A.B.

The source image also contains a flower-like mark and several columns of symbols. These have been preserved in the image but not interpreted.

## Significance

- Gives the solution to the six-move light puzzle in Tace Nox.
- Indicates that Anthea and Eden were the last two surviving Crows at that time.
- Shows Antheaâ€™s hostility towards surviving Chronarchs and suggests Elgira acted against them.

# Broken Fang Camp Sketches

> [!summary] At a glance
> Two crude maps drawn by the captured Broken Fang scouts Salla and Malt, showing the camp commanded by [[03 People/Notable Figures/Ulsar Quinn|Ulsar Quinn]] and the approximate location of the Defracturers' stolen horses.

## Circumstances

During the pursuit outside [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]], the Defracturers captured two fleeing Broken Fang scouts: **Salla** and **Malt**.

The party demanded that each scout draw a map of the nearby camp, placing particular emphasis on the location where the stolen horses were being held. The request was presented as a contest in which useful information might determine which prisoner survived.

Both scouts produced a sketch.

## Salla's sketch

<!-- Add the corresponding image to _Assets and embed it here.
Suggested filename: Broken Fang Camp Sketch - Salla.png
Suggested embed: ![[Broken Fang Camp Sketch - Salla.png|600]]
-->

Salla's map was crude and sparse. She died later in the session during an attack by Egg Hunter Hatchlings, preventing the party from questioning her further.

## Malt's sketch

<!-- Add the corresponding image to _Assets and embed it here.
Suggested filename: Broken Fang Camp Sketch - Malt.png
Suggested embed: ![[Broken Fang Camp Sketch - Malt.png|600]]
-->

Malt's map was similarly rough. Malt survived the hatchling attack after Ieuan stabilised him and remained in the Defracturers' custody at the end of Session 20.

## Comparison and reliability

The two sketches were similar enough that the Defracturers considered their broad layout believable.

Information that can be treated with the greatest confidence:

- a Broken Fang camp exists nearby;
- it is associated with Ulsar Quinn;
- the Defracturers' horses are being held there;
- both prisoners described broadly the same arrangement.

Information that remains unreliable or incomplete:

- precise scale and distance;
- compass orientation;
- the number and positions of guards;
- patrol routes;
- fortifications, traps and hidden approaches;
- whether the camp layout changed after the scouts departed.

The sketches should be treated as reconnaissance aids rather than accurate surveyed maps.

## Current significance

The maps are the party's principal source of information for planning an infiltration or assault to recover:

- Kingcup;
- Bracken;
- Nail;
- Vesper;
- Cobb.

It is not yet known whether the camp has been warned about the missing scouts or the party's possession of the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Bands of Distortion|Bands of Distortion]].

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Which route offers the safest approach?
> - How many Broken Fang members occupy the camp?
> - Where is Ulsar Quinn within the camp?
> - Are the horses guarded, restrained or prepared for movement?
> - Has the camp learned that Salla and Malt were captured?
> - Did either scout deliberately omit important defences?

# Broken Fang Contraband Manifest

> [!summary] At a glance
> A manifest recovered by n'Dong from a concealed Broken Fang drop site, identifying a supply network connecting locations across Rakara.

## Recovery

n'Dong discovered the manifest inside a shack used to store smuggled supplies near Somnolent's docks.

The site contained crates, whiskey and wrapped bricks of an unidentified substance. After sampling one of the bricks, n'Dong suffered severe pain, convulsions and temporary incapacity. The party later identified the substance on the manifest as **Agony**.

## Reconstructed transcription

The following entries are preserved in the Session 7 record:

| Destination | Recorded delivery |
|---|---|
| **One Ball Inn, Somnolent** | 4 Ã— barrels of whiskey |
| **The Scythian Tree, Orvak** | 2 Ã— rations; 4 Ã— weapons or armour |
| **Fighting Pits, Voruun** | Agony; Powdered Power; Dragon's Dandruff |
| **Ashka's Den, Khaali** | Unicorn Wax; Ember Berries |

## Significance

The manifest shows that the [[04 Organisations/Broken Fang|Broken Fang]] operated a distribution network extending across several Rakaran territories.

It also established that:

- the [[03 People/Notable Figures/Antoinne Cassan|One Ball Inn]] was part of the supply route;
- weapons and armour were being moved towards Orvak;
- unusual substances were being delivered to the Voruun fighting pits;
- a separate Khaali contact or destination received rare materials;
- the Somnolent shipment was only one part of a wider operation.

The purpose and legality of each listed substance have not all been confirmed.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Who prepared the manifest?
> - Who collected each delivery?
> - What are Powdered Power, Dragon's Dandruff, Unicorn Wax and Ember Berries?
> - Why were weapons and armour being sent to the Scythian Tree?
> - Is Ashka a person, organisation or place?
> - Does the same distribution network remain active?

## Campaign reference

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 07 - Coffee and Contraband|Session 7]]

| Document | Author | Key relevance |

| [[Report on the Death of Anthea Bloodclot]] | [[03 People/Notable Figures/Alaaric Hammerfall|Alaaric Hammerfall]] | Records Bloodspore poisoning, lost evidence and the earliest formal murder inquiry. |
| [[Loyal Crow's Orders to Buggie]] | An unidentified Crow, later believed to be [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eden Carrion|Eden Carrion]] | Orders Prusset's abduction during Emberwake. |
| [[Stolen Letter from Alistair Prusset's Study]] | Arch-Illuminant Serathiel | Authorises action against Anthea and directs Prusset to the Rootbound. |
| [[Anthea Bloodclot's Note on Tace Nox]] | [[03 People/Notable Deceased/Anthea Bloodclot|Anthea Bloodclot]] | Solves the light puzzle and describes the loss of three Crows. |

The three recovered letter images are preserved alongside transcriptions.

The Anthea death report is retained as its original DOCX and as a readable Markdown record.

# Report on the Death of Anthea Bloodclot

| | |
|---|---|
| **Classification** | Murder |
| **Status at filing** | Unsolved |
| **Death discovered** | 21st Greengold, 5137 |
| **Report entered** | 29th Greengold, 5137 |
| **Keeper of record** | [[Alaaric Hammerfall]] |

[Open the original report](../../_Assets/Documents/Report%20on%20the%20Death%20of%20Anthea%20Bloodclot.docx)

## Record

On the morning of the [[Emberwake Festival]], Anthea Bloodclot was discovered dead inside her caravan, stationed approximately two miles east of [[Somnolent]] along the southern road. She had been scheduled to address the gathered people later that day. Her attendance had been promoted most strongly by [[Alistair Prusset]], envoy of Perfid, and [[Eden Carrion]], liaison of Clan Voruun.

The body was discovered by **Sable Greentree**, a local merchant employed by the town to deliver festival supplies.

## Known recent contacts

- **[[Callum Redmayne]]** saw Anthea on the evening before her death with Eden Carrion and **[[Summer Quaresh]]**, sharing drinks.
- **[[Elmwood Bluevine]]** reported that Anthea had visited him one week earlier seeking information about a venture into Thistlewound.
- **[[Ersil Loquella]]** spoke with her two days before her death while gathering material for a ballad.

## Cause of death

Initial examination suggested natural causes. **[[Father Zantos]]**, Elder Druid of Somnolent, identified signs consistent with poisoning by **Bloodspore**, a rare and potent herb cultivated exclusively in Clan Orvak's lands.

The poison was probably administered in the wine found inside the caravan, which remained half-consumed.

## Loss of the crime scene

No comprehensive inquiry was conducted by the town guard. During the Emberwake festivities, the caravan and all evidence within it were removed by unknown parties. This prevented preservation of the scene and any later complete examination.

## Suspected interests

The report records several possible motives without assigning guilt:

- The **Rootbound** possessed access to Bloodspore and had openly opposed Anthea, but may have lacked the wealth and logistics to act alone.
- **Somnolent** resisted incorporation into Anthea's project, and [[Romayne Drayton]] had publicly opposed attempts to force the town into the fold.
- **Doro** feared the trade and military consequences of a unified Rakara.
- **Perfid** had condemned Anthea's interest in Ardynian history as heretical, despite Prusset publicly championing her Emberwake appearance.
- **Minir** was rumoured to have supported some of Anthea's early activity before becoming quieter as her power grew.

## Later significance

The report established Bloodspore poisoning and the removal of the caravan as central facts in the party's investigation. Later evidence connected Perfid, Alistair Prusset and a Rootbound cell to the murder; those conclusions were not available when this document was entered into Somnolent's records.

## Related

- [[Rootbound]]
- [[Church of Elgira]]
- [[Session 06 - Smoke, Song and Secrets]]
- [[Session 10 - Saints, Spies and Snake Eyes]]

# Stolen Letter from Alistair Prussetâ€™s Study

![[Letter from Arch-Illuminant Serathiel.png|500]]

## Transcription

> Ambassador Prussett,
>
> The High Council has reviewed your concerns regarding the Bloodclot woman. Her defiance is not merely political â€” it is theological trespass. Her elevation threatens the balance we have laboured to maintain.
>
> We concur that decisive action is now justified. You are advised to seek the rootbound. They operate beneath the canopy, but their reach extends far beyond it. They understand silence, and how to gift it.
>
> As ever, discretion is paramount.
>
> Arch-Illuminant Serathiel.

## Significance

The letter directly authorises decisive action against Anthea Bloodclot and instructs Prusset to contact the Rootbound.

> [!info] Church offices
> **Arch-Illuminant Serathiel** and **High Illuminant Derathel** are different people in different offices. Derathel is the head of the Church; Serathiel is the senior official who signed this instruction.
