# FILE: C:\Users\ellis\Documents\vaelond-wiki\content\06 Library\Letters and Documents\Vaelen's sending stone.md
---

My end is near. I am certain, even if I cannot yet name the hand that will finally reach me. If I am fortunate, this record will outlive me. If I am not, then perhaps it will merely serve as proof that at least one of us understood the charge laid upon us.

We were not meant to rule. That is the truth the others have forgotten. We were charged to guide the world, to prepare it, to leave behind something strong enough to resist should the ritual ever fail and Ardynia rise again. We were to cultivate endurance, not dominion. Readiness, not empire. We were never meant to lead the world by the throat.

Of them all, Erith has held closest to that truth. He built what he wished to build, gave Minir its structure, and then stepped away. Some would call that selfishness. Perhaps it is. He has spent his long years pursuing pleasure and his own amusements while the rest of us tangled ourselves in duty and fear. Yet there is honesty in him. He understood that this world was meant to stand on its own feet, not remain beneath ours. I do not admire his indifference, but I understand it better than the ambitions of the others.

Marra understands none of this. She believes our charge unfinished. Worse, she believes herself chosen to complete it. She wants the relics not to preserve them, but to wield them â€” to finish what Elgria began and destroy the threat of Ardynia utterly, whatever the cost. She speaks of necessity, as zealots always do when they wish to make horror sound disciplined. To her, my refusal to join her is proof that I stand against her. She cannot imagine restraint except as betrayal. I believe she comes for me, and I believe she will call it righteousness when she does.

Desmon is no better, only more vain. He has grown tired of immortality in mortal shape, tired of being served by the fettered masses of Perfid. The long years have not humbled him; they have only made him greedy for a grander form of survival. He looks upon Elgriaâ€™s ascension and sees not accident, not tragedy, but possibility. He wants to rise as she did â€” no, more than she did, because he would choose it. He has begun to think like a man who sees divinity as an attainment. I do not trust what he would become if given the means.

Perhaps Erith is the wiser of us. Willingly giving up power was the only honest act any of us committed after Elgria ascended. Yet even now I cannot follow him fully. He can watch the pieces fall. I still feel compelled to place my hand between the board and those who would overturn it. For that, I think I shall die.

I confess I do not meet that prospect of death with grace. I fear it. I fear ending. So I have made preparations. If my enemies take my body, they shall not so easily claim all of me. I have preserved what I can preserve. My soul, at least, will not be surrendered willingly to oblivion. If this is cowardice, then it is cowardice I embrace gladly.

â€” Vaelen

The **Bands of Distortion** are an Ardynian relic recovered from the stone golem guarding Rana Viam.

The bands were linked to the golem through a **heart stone**. Ieuan determined that removing the stone alongside the relic would prevent the bands from returning to the ruin in the manner of the [[Staff of Broken Time]]. The party stored the bands in a fitted holding case and did not immediately attempt to wear them.

Their precise powers remain unknown.

# Eula's Red Coins

> [!summary] At a glance
> Reddish gold coins marked by tracking magic and used by [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eula|Eula]] when purchasing corpses.

## Appearance

The coins resemble ordinary gold at a glance but possess a distinctive reddish hue. Magical examination has revealed arcane markers associated with tracking.

The party first encountered the marked currency after a hooded woman paid Erelmor for the bodies of slain bandits. The same woman later identified herself as Eula.

## Known behaviour

The Defracturers have established or observed that:

- Eula pays marked coin in exchange for fresh corpses.
- The coins can alert Eula when a killing occurs nearby.
- Eula may appear rapidly through teleportation magic to collect bodies and conduct a transaction.
- Placing a coin on a body that was already dead did not immediately summon her.
- The coins remain usable as currency, but carrying or spending them may spread Eula's influence or allow her to track events connected to them.

The exact trigger, range and limitations of the magic remain unknown.

## Campaign record

### First encounter

After a roadside battle, an unidentified hooded woman appeared and offered sixty gold for the defeated bandits. The party discovered arcane tracking marks on the payment and discarded the coins.

### Eula identifies herself

The party later found fifty reddish coins on a bandit captain. Eula appeared and doubled their value in exchange for the bodies of the dead bandits.

Erelmor attempted to give one of the coins to a farmer, but Ieuan replaced it with ordinary gold before the exchange was completed.

Erelmor also placed one of the coins on an already dead body as an experiment. Eula did not immediately appear.

### The ogres

After the Defracturers killed three ogres, Eula arrived and paid seventy-five marked coins for the bodies. Questioning her led the party to understand that the coins notify her when killings occur nearby.

## Current status

The party retained at least some of Eula's payment. The exact number carried and their current storage have not been recorded.

> [!warning] Caution
> The coins should not be treated as ordinary treasure. Their tracking effect may expose the holder, nearby deaths or the party's movements to Eula.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - What precisely activates a coin?
> - How far away can Eula detect a killing?
> - Does spending a coin transfer the mark to a new holder?
> - Can the magic be removed?
> - Why does Eula collect bodies?
> - Who or what receives the corpses after she takes them?

## Campaign references

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 05 - Fingers, Coins and Incriminations|Session 5]]
- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 16 - Cackles, Corpses and Catastrophe|Session 16]]
- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 17 - Shrieks, Widows and Time Juice|Session 17]]

## Related

- [[03 People/Notable Figures/Eula|Eula]]
- [[06 Library/Campaign Reference/Open Threads|Open Threads]]

# Ring of Mind Shielding

> [!summary] At a glance
> A ring recovered from [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]], currently worn by [[03 People/Player Characters/Ballar Novis|Ballar Novis]] and inhabited by a soul calling itself [[03 People/Notable Figures/Din|Din]].

## Discovery

Ballar found the ring inside one of the stone ottomans in Tace Nox. He equipped it without initially telling the rest of the party and had fully attuned to it by the time the group returned to Somnolent.

The ring was one of the objects that successfully left Tace Nox. This distinguishes it from the [[06 Library/Relics and Artefacts/Staff of Broken Time|Staff of Broken Time]] and the emeralds taken from the ruin's guardian, which vanished or disintegrated when the party crossed the site's threshold.

## The soul within

A consciousness within the ring later began speaking privately to Ballar. It calls itself **Din**.

Din has:

- questioned Ballar about the party's location, destination and purpose;
- asked Ballar to help find it a body;
- offered to guide the group through Ardynian ruins where it is able to do so.

Ballar has not accepted Din's proposed arrangement, although their conversation remains open.

## Known properties

The chronicle establishes that:

- the ring contains or preserves a communicating soul;
- Din can speak directly to Ballar while he wears the ring;
- the ring remained in Ballar's possession after leaving Tace Nox.

The full magical properties of the ring have not been systematically recorded in the campaign chronicle.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - Who was Din before entering the ring?
> - How and when was Din's soul bound to it?
> - What kind of body does Din want?
> - What guidance can Din provide within Ardynian ruins?
> - Can Din communicate with anyone other than the ring's wearer?
> - What would happen if the ring changed hands?

# Star Seed

> [!summary] At a glance
> A mysterious seed gifted to [[03 People/Player Characters/Ieuan|Ieuan]] by [[03 People/Notable Figures/Father Zantos|Father Zantos]], said to bloom once Ieuan fully embraces his druidic path.

## Origin

Father Zantos gave the Star Seed to Ieuan during their conversation in Somnolent.

Their discussion touched on:

- Ieuan's choice to follow the Circle of Stars;
- the connection between that path and [[01 Welcome to Vaelond/Religion/Orun the Verdant Kin|Orun the Verdant Kin]];
- ancient Ardynian truths preserved in Clan Erzhin's Annals of Stone;
- Zantos's past connection to the [[04 Organisations/Rootbound|Rootbound]] and his disillusionment with the direction they had taken.

As they parted, Zantos offered the Star Seed and promised that it would bloom once Ieuan fully embraced his path.

## Known properties

The Star Seed has not yet demonstrated any clear magical effect in the campaign chronicle.

What is known:

- it was deliberately given to Ieuan rather than the wider party;
- Zantos linked it to Ieuan's druidic development;
- its blooming appears conditional rather than immediate;
- its exact nature, origin and purpose are unknown.

## Possible significance

The Star Seed may be a personal druidic focus, a symbol of Zantos's trust or an object connected to Orun and the Circle of Stars.

Until it blooms or reacts to a specific event, its role should remain uncertain.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - What must Ieuan do to "fully embrace" his druidic path?
> - What will the Star Seed become when it blooms?
> - Did Zantos create, inherit or recover it?
> - Is the seed tied to Orun, Clan Erzhin, the Circle of Stars or something older?
> - Can anyone other than Ieuan cause it to bloom?

## Campaign reference

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 06 - Smoke, Song and Secrets|Session 6]]

# Time Juice

> [!summary] At a glance
> The Defracturers' informal name for a green Ardynian substance associated with temporal machinery in Tace Nox and Rana Viam.

## Appearance and discovery

The substance appears as a green fluid or residue within Ardynian ruins.

When the party entered [[02 Places/Rakara/Rana Viam|Rana Viam]], Ieuan recognised residue in a central basin as the same material previously encountered at [[02 Places/Rakara/Tace Nox|Tace Nox]].

The name **Time Juice** is a party nickname. Its original Ardynian name and intended classification are unknown.

## Rana Viam samples

Within Rana Viam, the party found:

- a beaker containing the substance;
- alchemical equipment from which n'Dong extracted two additional vials;
- a central basin designed to receive the fluid.

n'Dong poured some Time Juice into the basin. Green light spread across the adjoining door in a web-like pattern and opened the passage into the crossing chamber.

## Known function

The only directly demonstrated function is that Time Juice can activate at least some Ardynian mechanisms designed to accept it.

Its wider connection to time magic is strongly suggested by:

- its presence in Chronarch ruins;
- its familiar green appearance;
- the temporal mechanisms and relics found at those sites.

The substance has not been deliberately consumed or applied to a living creature in the recorded sessions.

## Current status

The party recovered multiple samples but used some to open the Rana Viam passage. The quantity remaining has not been recorded.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - What is Time Juice made from?
> - Was it a fuel, reagent, coolant or stabilising substance?
> - Does every Chronarch site use the same material?
> - Can it affect creatures, objects or the passage of time directly?
> - Is it safe to transport?
> - How much remains in the party's possession?

## Campaign references

- [[05 Campaign Chronicle/Session 17 - Shrieks, Widows and Time Juice|Session 17]]

# Virei's Shattered Mask

> [!summary] At a glance
> An old brittle mask found beneath the Drowsy Coin, which forced the Defracturers into private visions before n'Dong invoked Virei, the Shattered Mask, and lifted its curse.

## Discovery

The mask was found in the basement of the [[02 Places/Rakara/Somnolent/Drowsy Coin|Drowsy Coin]] after Callum Redmayne asked the Defracturers to investigate something strange below the tavern.

Ballar kicked through a pile of crates and struck the mask, causing it to slide free from the wreckage.

## The visions

When the mask was disturbed, the Defracturers were pulled into isolating visions or memories.

Known visions included:

- n'Dong seeing [[03 People/Notable Figures/Prodosius|Prodosius]] boast of slaughtering his parents and warning that n'Dong would soon die;
- Ieuan seeing a wood elf poisoner whispering of betrayal;
- Enrak standing before Ashka, goddess of fire and chaos;
- Ballar being rejected by the image of his mother;
- Erelmor seeing his dying wife begging him to explain why he had not saved her.

When the visions fractured, apparitions of Ballar's mother and Erelmor's wife remained and attacked the party.

## The prayer to Virei

After the apparitions were defeated, n'Dong approached the mask and recited a forgotten prayer to **Virei, the Shattered Mask**, whose broken visage watches over secrets and spirits.

The curse lifted. The mask fell inert, and n'Dong took it.

## Known properties

The mask has demonstrated the ability to:

- trigger intensely personal visions or memories;
- manifest hostile spiritual apparitions;
- draw upon guilt, grief, fear or hidden trauma;
- respond to a prayer associated with Virei.

After n'Dong's prayer, the mask appeared inert. It has not yet shown any further powers in the chronicle.

## Current status

n'Dong currently carries the mask. Whether it is harmless, sleeping, purified or waiting for another trigger remains unknown.

> [!warning] Caution
> The mask should not be assumed safe merely because the immediate curse was lifted. Its connection to secrets, spirits and personal trauma remains unexplained.

> [!question] Unresolved
> - What is Virei?
> - Why did n'Dong know the prayer?
> - Was the mask a relic, holy object, cursed object or prison?
> - Why was it hidden beneath the Drowsy Coin?
> - Can the mask still reveal secrets or summon spirits?
> - Did the visions show literal truth, manipulated memory or symbolic fear?
